# Decisions

## DECIDED

- Master is the current Work proxy.
- Temporal snapshots are mandatory.
- Evaluation is out-of-time.
- Historical baselines come before ML.
- Time Machine uses historical out-of-time predictions.
- master_id is a unique key in masters_df (verified, 0 duplicates across 2,589,349 rows).
- status is dropped from releases_df (100% null, not present as an XML attribute in this dump, verified against raw XML).
- year == 0 in masters_df is treated as missing and converted to NaN, not as a real date (6.7% of rows). The decision was logged back when it was made but the notebook cell that applies it to masters_df was never written (the equivalent fix for standalone_candidates was written and ran fine), so 100,877 zero-year master rows leaked into works_df uncleaned, inflating reissue-candidate year gaps into the hundreds or thousands of years. Added the missing masters_df["year"] fix in the notebook and re-ran sections 7 to 9 on 06-10-2026.
- work_id = master_id when master_id != 0, otherwise a synthetic id is required (41.6% of releases_df have master_id == 0).
- Masters with artist_names in {"Various", "Unknown Artist", "No Artist"} are excluded from the Works dataset (174,000 masters, 6.7%, verified via value_counts).
- Masters tagged Non-Music are excluded from the Works dataset (38,685 masters, not music even when released by a real artist).
- Masters tagged Children's are excluded from the Works dataset (22,390 masters, not relevant to musical style prediction).
- master_id 376240 ("Dummy1") is a placeholder/test record (empty artist_ids, no genres, no styles, year 0) and is dropped.
- Masters marked data_quality == "Entirely Incorrect" are dropped (139 masters, flagged unreliable by Discogs' own community).
- Masters marked data_quality == "Needs Vote" are kept (2,155 masters, unconfirmed but not known to be wrong).
- Masters flagged is_compilation or is_unofficial (via a representative release's format_descriptions) are excluded from the Works dataset (165,172 compilations, 32,158 unofficial, 8,421 overlap).
- release_year > 2026 (snapshot date) is treated as missing inside _add_release_year itself, not just in standalone_candidates. Gap found: of 38,670 master years recovered by recover_missing_master_years, 0 were > 2026, no rows were actually affected, but the exclusion only lived in the standalone_candidates cell, so a future-dated release could in principle have been picked up as a recovered master year. Fixed in src/works.py so every caller shares the rule. Re-ran notebook 01 end to end on 08-10-2026, every checkpoint parquet is byte-identical (md5) to the pre-fix run, confirming no shift; notebook 02 was not re-run since nothing changed upstream of it.
- Standalone releases (master_id == 0) belonging to artists with 3+ standalone releases become synthetic Works (recovers 388,610 artists otherwise lost entirely under Masters-only).
- The same exclusion rules applied to masters_df (non-artist placeholders, out-of-scope genres, Entirely Incorrect data_quality, compilation, unofficial) are applied to these standalone releases: 899,514 + 685,589 + 826 + 401,541 + 182,761 excluded (with overlaps), 4,218,546 remain.
- year == 0 and year > 2026 in standalone_candidates are treated as missing, same rule as masters_df (115 zero-year rows, 1 future-dated release).
- works_df combines masters_df (2,015,417 rows, work_source="master") and standalone_candidates (4,218,546 rows, work_source="synthetic") into one unified Works table, 6,233,963 rows total, work_id verified unique. Credited artist rule (src/works.py build_works_table, notebooks/01_cleaning_eda.ipynb cell 113): each Work takes the first credited artist (artist_ids[0], artist_names[0]) as its artist_id and artist, because the Works table holds one artist per row; later credited artists are not kept. The share of Works with more than one credited artist is not measured in the repo.
- Exact duplicates in works_df (same artist_id, normalized title, year) are removed, keeping one per group: master preferred over synthetic, then smallest work_id. 119,492 rows removed (107,537 groups), works_df now 6,114,471 rows.
- Re-ran the above after the year==0 fix: exact-duplicate removal now resolves to a slightly different split (counts shift because some former year==0/year==NaN mismatches no longer collide or separate the same way), works_df before reissue handling stands at 6,114,471 rows, unchanged in final size.
- Re-ran the above again after adding the Master missing-year recovery step (recover_missing_master_years in src/works.py, called right after works_df is first built, before any dedup or title filter runs, see the recovery entry below): exact-duplicate removal now resolves to 119,043 rows removed (107,208 groups, was 119,492/107,537), works_df before reissue handling now 6,114,920 rows (was 6,114,471), for the same reason as the year==0 re-run above, a Master's year changing from missing to a real recovered value changes which rows collide on the dedup_key. Every downstream filter count through the rest of the pipeline shifts by a similarly small amount for the same reason; the new final checkpoint figure is logged separately below.
- Works tagged reissue/repress/remaster (via format_descriptions) that share artist_id + normalized title with an earlier-year work by the same artist are removed, keeping the earliest work in each group (the earliest row can never match its own condition, so it survives automatically, no separate keep-rule needed). Verified sane after the year==0 fix: year gap median 12, max 89 (Robert Johnson, 1930s recordings reissued in 2026); only 20/4758 candidates (0.42%) have a generic title ("Untitled", "Live", "Demo"), accepted as noise. 4,758 rows removed, works_df now 6,109,713 rows.
- Live recordings are excluded from the Works dataset entirely (same reasoning as compilations/unofficial: a performance of existing material, not a new stylistic work). No reliable Discogs tag exists for this (format_descriptions and style both checked, neither carries a "Live" value), so detection is title-only (regex \blive\b, case-insensitive). Checked the word following "live" in matching titles: dominated by "at"/"in"/"from"/end-of-title/parenthetical patterns consistent with real live albums, accepted residual noise in both directions (idiom false positives like "Live To Tell", untitled-as-live false negatives). 92,531 rows removed, works_df now 6,017,182 rows.
- "Greatest Hits" works that slipped through the earlier is_compilation exclusion (Discogs did not tag them Compilation in format_descriptions) are removed by title match ("greatest hits", case-insensitive). Small population (1,622 rows, 0.027%), same reasoning as the Live exclusion: a compilation of previously released material, not new creative work. works_df now 6,015,560 rows.
- Other clear compilation-type titles that also slipped through is_compilation ("best of", "anthology", "box set", "discography", "b-sides"/"bsides", "rarities") are removed the same way. Sample of 20 confirmed overwhelmingly real compilations, one accepted edge case ("Best Of Me", could be an original song title, same order of noise as Live/reissue). 6,622 rows removed, works_df now 6,008,938 rows.
- "Remixes"-titled works (regex remixes|remix album) are removed: a package of mostly third-party remixes of an already-existing track, credited back to the original artist, same reasoning as reissue (repackaging, not a new creative step). Sample of 20 was uniformly clean, no ambiguous originals. 24,917 rows removed, works_df now 5,984,021 rows.
- "Tribute"-titled works (regex \btribute\b) are removed: overwhelmingly "Tribute To [other artist]", covering/performing someone else's material as homage, not the credited artist's own new work. A handful of standalone matches ("Tribute", "My Tribute") don't name a target and could be original compositions, accepted as the same order of noise as other patterns. 5,394 rows removed, works_df now 5,978,627 rows.
- "acoustic" alone (3,824 matches) was too ambiguous to exclude (counterexample: "Acoustic Soul" by Fink is a genuine original studio album, not a redo), moved to CUT. Narrowed to the parenthetical "(Acoustic)" suffix pattern instead (1,060 matches): sample of 20 confirmed uniformly "Song Name (Acoustic)", a redo of an existing song, same reasoning as Live. 1,060 rows removed, works_df now 5,977,567 rows.
- "(Instrumental)" parenthetical suffix (757 matches, went straight to the narrow pattern given the acoustic precedent, broad "instrumental" never tested): a vocal-less version of an existing song. Sample of 20 confirmed the pattern. 757 rows removed, works_df now 5,976,810 rows.
- "unplugged" (983 matches) are removed: overwhelmingly MTV-Unplugged-style live acoustic performances of already-existing songs, same reasoning as Live. Sample of 20 was uniformly clean. 983 rows removed, works_df now 5,975,827 rows.
- "radio edit"/"radio version"/"bbc session" (1,735 matches combined) are removed: a shortened derivative edit of an existing track, or a BBC-style radio session re-performing existing material, same reasoning as reissue/Unplugged. "session" alone was checked and discarded (too generic, mostly original recording-session albums like "Tutorial Sessions"). 1,735 rows removed, works_df now 5,974,092 rows.
- Placeholder-artist leak fixed: the original Various/Unknown Artist/No Artist exclusion was case-sensitive exact match, so "various"/"VARIOUS" and similar case variants slipped through (1,422 rows, found while reviewing Greatest Hits titles). Removed by a case-insensitive pattern covering Various/Various Artists/Unknown/Unknown Artist/No Artist/N A/VA. works_df now 5,972,670 rows.
- Fixed the root cause of the placeholder-artist leak at the source (06-10-2026): the masters_df and standalone_candidates exclusion cells now compare artist_names_str.str.lower() against a lowercased NON_ARTIST_PLACEHOLDERS set, instead of an exact case-sensitive match. Re-ran the full notebook from that point on; downstream counts shifted slightly (see updated numbers above). A final placeholder-leak QA check at the end of works_df cleaning still finds 1,422 remaining, other variants (bare "Unknown", "N/A", "VA") the original exclusion set didn't cover, removed there, 0 remaining after.
- Similarity-only fuzzy-dedup (rapidfuzz token_sort_ratio, bands 85-90/90-95/95-99), with no guard against numbered-series titles, was rejected as a filter on top of the existing exact-match title_normalized dedup: checked at full scale across all 699,079 multi-work artists / 5,774,455 works in works_df (window-10 alphabetical blocking per artist, no cap or skip), numbered/episodic series entries (radio show episodes, Vol./Partie/roman-numeral sequences) dominate every band as false positives, with a handful of plausible true duplicates (typos, accent variants) scattered across bands and no clean score separation from the noise. Superseded by the guarded version below, which fixes this false-positive pattern and is the one actually applied to works_df.
- Fuzzy-dedup implemented in src/works.py (remove_fuzzy_duplicate_titles): within each artist_id, using window-10 alphabetical blocking, pairs scoring >=90 on rapidfuzz token_sort_ratio are collapsed unless a number/roman-numeral guard applies. The guard strips embedded digit runs from every token (so "mix4" vs "mix7", "S01E03" vs "S01E05" are also caught, not just space-separated numbers like "Vol. 2" vs "Vol. 3") and skips the pair if titles are only different by that numeric content. Threshold chosen because a stricter 95+ cutoff would have missed known true duplicates in Bing Crosby's catalog (scores 89.5 and 91.4). Tie-break on a collapsed pair keeps the earlier year (missing year treated as latest), ties broken by smaller work_id. Verified at full scale against works_checkpoint.parquet (6,114,471 rows): removed 21,846 rows (0.36%), works_df now 6,092,625 rows. That check ran the filter in isolation against the stale pre-title-filters checkpoint (6,114,471 rows), not at its actual position at the end of the integrated pipeline. Re-ran the full notebook top to bottom from a fresh kernel on 07-10-2026 (see the checkpoint-overwrite fix below), confirming the filter's real position after all title filters (pre-fuzzy-dedup works_df at 5,972,670 rows, matching the figure logged above): removed 20,854 rows, works_df now 5,951,816 rows. This is the correct, current figure; the 21,846/6,092,625 numbers above are superseded. Two accepted known limitations: (1) "Vol."/"Volume" abbreviation mismatches aren't caught by the guard, so a small number of genuine volume-series pairs may still collide (full-dataset band sampling showed minimal residual noise after the guard, so this looks rare); (2) shared-template titles with a non-numeric varying word (e.g. a medley "X / Y" title where Y differs, such as "White Christmas / The First Nowell" vs "...The First Snowfall") can produce false positives, spot-checked at 1 of 11 in the Bing Crosby test. Applied at the end of the cleaning pipeline in notebooks/01_cleaning_eda.ipynb, right before the final checkpoint save.
- Fixed a checkpoint-overwrite bug in notebooks/01_cleaning_eda.ipynb (07-10-2026): three different pipeline stages (initial build_works_table output, post-exact-dedup, and the true final post-fuzzy-dedup state) all saved to the same works_checkpoint.parquet filename. A kernel restart followed by a partial re-run (stopping right after the exact-dedup save) silently regressed the on-disk file to that mid-pipeline state with no indication it was stale, which is what produced the incorrect 21,846/6,092,625 fuzzy-dedup figures above. Fixed by giving the two intermediate stages distinct filenames (works_checkpoint_raw.parquet, works_checkpoint_dedup.parquet), leaving works_checkpoint.parquet as the name only the final cell writes. Re-ran the full notebook top to bottom from a fresh kernel to regenerate the correct final checkpoint (5,951,816 rows, 9 columns, format_descriptions confirmed joined: 5,951,726 of 5,951,816 rows non-null).
- Extended the checkpoint-naming fix above (07-10-2026): the post-fuzzy-dedup state itself turned out to be a third stage sharing the same works_checkpoint.parquet name once two more filters (catalog-number dedup, mass-upload exclusion) needed to run after it, which would have reintroduced the exact overwrite risk just fixed. Renamed that stage's output to works_checkpoint_fuzzy_dedup.parquet, and moved its column-drop list to keep title_normalized and is_reissue_tagged alive (both still needed by the catalog-number filter below), dropping only the genuinely dead has_earlier_same_title/is_reissue_candidate/is_live_tagged there instead. works_checkpoint.parquet is now written by exactly one cell, at the true end of the pipeline, after both new filters. Rule going forward: any cleaning step added later must be inserted before that final cell, never after it.
- Catalog-number exact-match dedup implemented in src/works.py (remove_catalog_number_duplicates): targets the 55,642 same-artist_id + same-title_normalized, multi-year groups (122,569 rows) that carry no reissue/repress/remaster tag at all, invisible to the existing reissue filter. Checked at full scale (not sample) against works_checkpoint_fuzzy_dedup.parquet: 951 of those groups (1,918 rows) share an exact (label name, catalog number) pair between two or more entries, where the shared catalog number is real, not a blank/placeholder value ("none", "", "n/a" are never accepted as evidence; EMPTY_CATALOG_NUMBERS in src/works.py). A further 2,943 groups share only a blank/placeholder catno and are deliberately excluded, since two unrelated self-releases can easily both be uncatalogued. A 30-row sample of the 951 real-match groups was uniformly clean (same recording re-released under a different year/format, just never tagged by Discogs). Collapsed with the same tie-break as the fuzzy-dedup filter: earliest year survives (missing year sorts last), ties broken by smaller work_id; matches spanning 3+ entries in one group are resolved as connected components, not just pairwise, so a transitive chain collapses to one survivor. Verified at full scale against works_checkpoint_fuzzy_dedup.parquet (5,951,816 rows): removed 967 rows (951 groups, 1,918 rows involved minus 951 survivors), works_df now 5,950,849 rows. Residual limitations accepted, not resolved: sequential/incremented catalog numbers for a genuine new pressing, a label/country change on true reissue, and self-released artists with no catalog number system at all can all still slip through uncaught.
- Mass-upload artist exclusion implemented in src/works.py (remove_mass_upload_artists), resolving the PENDING question about "Nosferatu 1922" (artist_id 6391827): rule is general, not hardcoded to that artist_id, checked across every artist_id in works_df (not just the top few hundred by volume). Flags artist_ids with 50+ works (floor tied to the ~99th percentile of works-per-artist, ~51, from the univariate EDA in notebooks/02_eda_visualization.ipynb) where synthetic_share >= 0.90 AND roman_numeral_title_share >= 0.50 AND busiest_2yr_window_share >= 0.80, all three at once. Without the 50-work floor, the same three-signal rule matches 165 artists / 2,301 rows, but 164 of those have only 2-18 works, too few for the synthetic-share and 2-year-window signals to mean anything (both are almost automatically satisfied by a tiny, recently-entered catalog); the floor was added specifically to exclude that noise. With the floor, exactly one artist_id matches across the full dataset: 6391827 ("Nosferatu 1922", 1,854 works, 98.8% synthetic, 86.5% bare-Roman-numeral titles, 93.3% of its dated works landing in one 2-year window), confirming the no-floor matches above were noise rather than a second real case. Verified at full scale against the post-catalog-dedup works_df (5,950,849 rows): removed 1,854 rows (all of artist_id 6391827's works), works_df now 5,948,995 rows. True end-of-pipeline checkpoint (after dropping the now-dead title_normalized/is_reissue_tagged columns): 5,948,995 rows, 9 columns.
- notebooks/02_eda_visualization.ipynb was found executing against a stale works_checkpoint.parquet (5,972,670 rows, the pre-fuzzy-dedup/pre-catalog-dedup/pre-mass-upload-exclusion state), discovered because its Nosferatu-1922 outlier-inspection cell crashed when re-run against the current checkpoint (that artist is already excluded, see above). Re-ran the full notebook against the current final checkpoint (5,948,995 rows) on 07-10-2026: confirms the mass-upload exclusion worked as intended, no secondary outlier remains at the top of works-per-artist (current max is Bing Crosby, 1,486 works; sample of 15 titles confirmed real song/single releases spanning the 1940s-1980s, not an upload pattern). All downstream EDA numbers in that notebook (missing-year shares, works-per-artist stats, artist-volume concentration, works-per-year peak, genre counts) were refreshed to match the current checkpoint; see the updated PENDING entries below for the figures that changed.

- Master missing-year recovery implemented (recover_missing_master_years in src/works.py, 08-10-2026): a read-only check first found that of the 98,728 master-side works missing a year in the then-final works_checkpoint.parquet (5,948,995 rows), 37,473 (37.9%) had at least one Release with a parseable, non-zero released year, overwhelmingly with 2-5 candidate Releases (32,344), not a handful of oddly-linked masters (29 at most in the sampled set). This is real data already present in releases.parquet, not an inference or a fabrication, so the recovery is applied unconditionally: for each Master still missing a year, the earliest valid year among its own Releases (same _add_release_year parsing already used elsewhere) is taken as the recovered year. Inserted right after works_df is first built, before any dedup or title filter, since the exact/fuzzy/catalog-number dedup survivor tie-break all use year. Re-ran the full pipeline top to bottom from a fresh kernel (08-10-2026): at the point of recovery, 101,543 master works are missing a year, 38,670 (38.1%) are recovered. The final works_checkpoint.parquet now has 5,949,217 rows (was 5,948,995, superseded), with 61,388 of 1,951,057 master-side works still missing a year (3.15%, was 5.1%) and overall missing-year share down to 8.58% (510,364 of 5,949,217 rows, was 9.19%).
- Genre disparity in the recovery above is non-uniform and NOT resolved by it: a read-only per-genre breakdown found recovery rate correlates strongly negatively with each genre's original missing-year rate (Spearman r=-0.809, p=0.0026, n=11 genres), meaning genres that already had the easiest time (Rock 53.8% recovery, Electronic 47.9%, Blues 50.1%) recover at roughly 50%, while the hardest-hit genres (Latin 32.8%, Folk/World/&Country 30.2%, Reggae 38.9%) recover at the lowest rates. Every genre's absolute missing-year count only improves (Folk/World/&Country gets the single largest absolute recovery, 10,631 rows), but the relative disparity between genres does not close, if anything it widens slightly (missing-year spread went from ~17x to ~18x between the worst genres and Electronic, see the DECIDED entry below). Accepted as an unresolved limitation of this recovery, not presented as having fixed the genre equity gap.

- Missing-year artist dropout: do not drop artists/works with missing year. Rationale (08-10-2026): dropping the 173,938 artists (16.8% of 1,034,756) who have missing year would remove 1,995,677 works (33.6% of the 5,949,217 total), with a material genre/style skew in the dropped population (affected artists over-indexed in high-missing-rate genres: Latin 25.7%→36.6%, Folk/World/&Country 21.5%→37.2%, Reggae 23.0%→31.1%, Punk 2.6%→18.8%, Folk style 11.7%→29.5%; under-indexed in low-missing-rate genres: Electronic 1.5%→11.3%, Tech House 0.4%→4.8%; range of differences +5% to +17.8pp). Not a neutral trim of noise, but a selective removal of entire categories. Approach: keep missing-year works; add an explicit year_missing boolean feature; use a model family that splits on missingness natively (LightGBM/HistGradientBoostingClassifier), not sklearn's default RandomForest. Supporting numbers: 173,938/1,034,756 artists affected; of those, 39,706 fully missing (all works undated), 134,232 partially missing (mixed); 510,364 of their 1,995,677 works missing year (25.6%); genre/style distribution skew documented in the pasted analysis from notebooks/02_eda_visualization.ipynb re-run 08-10-2026 (before finalizing train/test split, revisit this decision if model performance on missing-year works drifts significantly at modeling stage).
- WHEN target is measured at year level, snapshots stay one per dated work (10-10-2026, src/snapshots.py): next_1y/next_2y use the gap to the artist's next year with activity after the snapshot's own year (always >= 1), not the next individual Work. Reason: 63% of dates are year-only, and with the next-Work definition 48.2% of snapshots (56.2% with 3+ prior works) had the next Work in the same year (gap 0), forcing next_1y/next_2y positive (76.2%/85.9% base rates). Collapsing to one snapshot per artist-year was tried first and replaced to keep the per-work unit. Re-validated on the full checkpoint (5,438,765 snapshots, 510,357 undated and 95 no-artist works dropped): next_1y 51.8%, next_2y 64.1% (62.4%/74.0% with 3+ prior works), 66.1% of next active years fall exactly 1 year later, and an independent recompute of both targets matches exactly. Added works_this_year (whole-year count, so it includes later same-year works), work_source/genres/year_missing pass-through, a deterministic within-year order (see the so-far entry), and 2025 as the last complete year for censoring (the 01-09-2026 dump makes 2026 partial). Known effect: next_1y rises with works_this_year (29.3% for 1 work, 86.3% for 5+), so productivity is a strong WHEN signal.
- new_style comparison fixed in src/snapshots.py (10-10-2026): the next Work is now compared against every Work up to and including the snapshot's Work, matching src/eda.py and the docstring. The earlier code left the snapshot's own Work out of the seen set, so a style repeated from the immediately preceding Work was wrongly flagged new (37.3% overall and 21.5% with 3+ prior works, now 26.7% and 18.8%, against 23.5%/18.1% in the EDA base rate). Superseded as the modeling target by the artist-year target (13-10-2026, see the entry at the end of DECIDED); the Work-level column stays as a diagnostic.
- Work-type feature added at snapshot level (src/snapshots.py, classify_work_type): album-like = Album/LP/EP/Mini-Album, single-like = Single/Maxi-Single, other = everything else. Over all 5,949,217 Works: 43.5% album, 21.3% single, 35.2% other. "Other" is mostly untagged physical or digital formats (MP3 24%, 7" 23%, 45 RPM 22%, 12" 16% of the other bucket) plus 185,784 Works with no format description at all, so single-like is a lower bound. Snapshot features: is_album_like, is_single_like, albums_so_far_this_year, singles_so_far_this_year (model-safe) and the whole-year albums_this_year, singles_this_year (descriptive only, see the next entry).
- Within-year productivity features split into model-safe and descriptive-only (10-10-2026, src/snapshots.py): works_so_far_this_year (works up to and including this one in the artist/year/work_id sort order), albums_so_far_this_year and singles_so_far_this_year are the modeling-facing features. works_this_year, albums_this_year and singles_this_year count the whole year, so they include same-year works that come after the snapshot's work and leak future information within the year; they stay in the output for EDA only and are marked in the docstring as not safe as model inputs. Re-check on the full checkpoint (5,438,765 snapshots), next_1y by works count: 1 work 38.7% (so far) vs 29.3% (whole year), 2 works 61.2% vs 52.3%, 3-4 74.3% vs 67.0%, 5-10 87.2% vs 83.6%, 11+ 92.4% vs 92.2%; Spearman with next_1y 0.351 (so far) vs 0.445 (whole year). On first-of-year rows only (all have works_so_far = 1, so the feature carries no information there), the whole-year count alone still separates next_1y from 29.3% to 92.0%, which is the leaked part: about a fifth of the correlation (0.445 to 0.351) disappears, and the rest of the signal (38.7% to 92.4% across the buckets) is genuine within-year productivity known at the snapshot. Caveat: the within-year order is not real chronology (the data only knows the year), so the so-far features are a proxy, and the strictly leak-free cutoff is the year. The first version ordered same-year works by work_id, which put every master before every synthetic work (synthetic ids carry the 10_000_000_000 offset); replaced the same day by a seeded pseudo-random order (hash of work_id plus WITHIN_YEAR_ORDER_SEED = 1, reproducible and independent of input row order). Re-check: Spearman of works_so_far_this_year with next_1y unchanged at 0.351 and the bucket rates unchanged to 3 decimals (38.7%, 61.2%, 74.3%, 87.2%, 92.4%), so the remaining signal is genuine productivity, not a source artifact; in the 358,052 artist-years that mix masters and synthetic works, the first slot is now synthetic 52.5% of the time (54.3% of those works are synthetic), was 0% by construction. The same order drives history_count, years_since_last and the next-work comparison in new_style.
- Backtesting year distribution inspected (10-10-2026, src/backtesting.py, notebooks/03_when_backtesting_design.ipynb): 5,438,765 snapshots, peak 191,013 in 2020. Low-volume year = fewer than 590 snapshots (10th percentile of yearly counts over complete years, 2026 excluded so the partial year does not pull the cut down). It flags 14 years, all 1892 to 1907, about 0.05% of snapshots, far from any plausible holdout or fold boundary. 2026 (54,958 snapshots, 1.01%) is flagged separately as a partial year (dump dated 01-09-2026), not through the low-volume criterion.
- Final holdout = snapshots with year 2018 to 2023 (10-10-2026, src/backtesting.py split_holdout): 1,020,151 snapshots, 18.8% of all (18.95% of the 5,383,807 that exclude 2026), landing within about 1 point of the 20% target; development = year <= 2017 (4,111,163 snapshots, 75.6%), the only pool for walk-forward folds and tuning. The holdout stays untouched until the final model evaluation. Chosen over 2019 to 2025 (20.5%) and 2020 to 2025 (17.4%) because those would put the lag-affected years in the metric that decides the project.
- 2024 to 2025 are excluded from training, tuning and the evaluation metric (bucket excluded_recent, 252,493 snapshots, 4.6%): the decline from 191,013 snapshots in 2020 to 134,893 (2024) and 117,600 (2025) is very likely Discogs submission lag (recent years still being backfilled by users), not a real drop in activity. It must not be read as a signal about recent years or as a reason for the model to weight them differently. 2026 (bucket demo, 54,958 snapshots) is excluded for a different reason (partial year). Both buckets stay in the dataset for demo and visualization use only, each flagged separately.
- Feature point-in-time audit of every src/snapshots.py output column (10-10-2026, src/backtesting.py point_in_time_check; notebooks/03_when_backtesting_design.ipynb section 2b). Method: code read of build_snapshots plus a truncation test (30,000 random artists, all dated Works after the cutoff removed, snapshots rebuilt, every column compared on snapshots with year <= cutoff): 0 rows differ for every feature and identifier column at cutoffs 1990, 2000 and 2010; only next_1y (571/1,071/1,896 rows), next_2y (913/1,700/2,805) and new_style (1,632/2,774/4,161) differ, as designed. Blind spots of the test: it cuts at year level (cannot see within-year leaks) and undated Works have no year to cut on (their column is identical by construction). Column by column. SAFE (uses only Works up to and including the snapshot's own year): works_so_far_this_year, albums_so_far_this_year, singles_so_far_this_year (cumcount/cumsum inside artist-year in the seeded sort order), is_album_like, is_single_like (this Work's own format_descriptions), history_count (cumcount per artist, dated Works strictly before this one; undated Works are split off before the cumcount, verified 13-10-2026 on a random sample of 3,000 artists with both dated and undated Works, mean 3.25 undated each, 33,064 snapshots: building with and without the undated Works gives identical history_count, years_since_last, years_active_so_far and historical_rate on every row, max history_count equals dated Works minus 1 for every artist, and the rebuilt history_count matches the saved snapshots.parquet on 100% of rows; so the undated blind spot above does not affect these four features), years_since_last (year minus the artist's previous Work year), years_active_so_far (year minus the artist's first dated Work year), historical_rate (history_count / years_active_so_far), genres (this Work's own tags). SAFE BUT WITH A CAVEAT: year and cutoff_year (the same value; no leak, but a calendar-time feature extrapolates badly out-of-time and would absorb the 2020-2025 submission-lag decline, so do not use as a feature). KEYS, NOT FEATURES: artist_id (float64, used only as groupby key in snapshots.py and as the cluster key of the bootstrap in baselines.py; no model consumes it as input today, the baselines read only history_count and historical_rate), work_id (join key and sort tiebreak; synthetic ids carry a 10_000_000_000 offset and master ids follow Discogs entry order, so as a feature it would leak work_source and submission time). TARGETS (look forward by design, never inputs): next_1y, next_2y, new_style. NOT SAFE: works_this_year, albums_this_year, singles_this_year (whole-year counts include same-year Works after this one in the sort order; the year-level truncation test cannot show this, the leak is within the year). NOT CONFIRMED, treat as unsafe until resolved: artist_undated_work_count and year_missing (artist-level counts over the artist's whole catalog of undated Works, same value on every row of the artist; an undated Work has no year so its position relative to the snapshot is unknowable, and a constant per-artist value also lets a model recognise the artist across train and test), work_source (a Work is synthetic only if the artist has 3 or more standalone releases anywhere in the dump, a rule that uses releases after the snapshot, so synthetic vs master encodes a future-looking cohort selection). Upstream limit: row presence also depends on whole-catalog cleaning rules in src/works.py (fuzzy and catalog-number dedup, mass-upload artist exclusion, missing-year recovery), not verifiable from snapshots.py alone. Consequence: the artist overlap between train and test inside one fold is not leakage for the SAFE features, but this entry must be revisited if artist_id or any non-time-respecting artist-level aggregate is later introduced as a raw model feature, and the NOT SAFE and NOT CONFIRMED columns stay out of the model inputs until resolved.
- Cleaning-pipeline cross-time audit (10-10-2026, notebooks/03_when_backtesting_design.ipynb section 2c; src/backtesting.py presence_truncation_check, mass_upload_truncation_check, master_year_provenance): no change to src/works.py, no rebuild. Distinction: src/works.py builds the canonical catalog (which Works are real and which are duplicates), and that step legitimately uses the artist's full history, as Discogs itself keeps re-resolving duplicates with complete information and as the model will be deployed against the fully curated data; restricting it to past-only data would make the catalog less accurate. The point-in-time requirement applies only to the feature and label computation in src/snapshots.py (what the model would know at prediction time), verified column by column in the feature audit entry. Different category from the gap entry below: 'catalog built with full history' is not 'model trained on leaked future labels'. Measured with truncation tests (remove an artist's Works dated after 1990, 2000 or 2010, rerun the rule, compare survival of dated Works with year <= cutoff), kept as a record and read as expected behavior of a full-history step, not leakage: remove_fuzzy_duplicate_titles 19/17/21 dated Works change survival (0.008%/0.005%/0.005% of 241,944/318,708/429,603; the earliest year always wins a pair and no attributes are merged, the change comes from the 10-title alphabetical window); remove_catalog_number_duplicates 0 of 492,803/631,386/792,875 dated Works change over the whole in-scope population (40,297 artists); remove_mass_upload_artists flags exactly one artist (6391827, 1,854 Works, all 2018 to 2024, none in development years), a whole-catalog decision by design; recover_missing_master_years is the adopted recovery (earliest year among the master's own Releases) and the three abandoned imputations left no trace (of 1,951,057 master Works, 1,852,315 keep the masters.parquet year, 37,354 were filled by recovery, 0 differ from a year Discogs holds, 61,388 stay NaN; caveat: a master whose only dated Release is a later reissue gets that later year). Undated Works also change survival (fuzzy 170/89/26 of 75,717, catalog-number 12/8/6 of 113,390) because they lose to any dated twin; expected, they are not snapshot rows. Read from code only: exact-duplicate step (same-year key), reissue filter (needs an earlier same-title Work), per-row exclusions.
- Label gap at the split boundaries (10-10-2026, src/backtesting.py GAP_YEARS, holdout_gap, make_folds): next_1y and next_2y look 1 to 2 years past the snapshot's own year, so a train row just before a test or holdout period has a label that already encodes activity inside the period meant to be sealed off. Rule (same idea as purged cross-validation for time series, the longer 2-year horizon covers both targets): exclude from training every row with year >= test_start - 2. Holdout boundary: development rows with year >= 2016 (2018 - 2) are not used to train the model evaluated on the holdout, 325,133 rows (7.9% of the 4,111,163 development rows), leaving final_train at 3,786,030 rows (1892 to 2015); they stay in development for the walk-forward test tranches. Walk-forward: per fold, train rows with year >= test_start - 2 are removed. This is a separate issue from the full-history catalog above: it is the real label leak.
- Walk-forward folds (10-10-2026, src/backtesting.py make_folds): 5 expanding-window folds over development, each test tranche 5 calendar years (1993-1997, 1998-2002, 2003-2007, 2008-2012, 2013-2017, 329,253 to 779,670 rows, not equalized); no tranche touches a low-volume year; train = every year before the tranche minus the 2-year gap (gap removes 101,120/143,236/164,334/205,259/278,347 rows; final train 1,401,791/1,688,928/2,061,043/2,496,904/3,053,146); censoring removes 0 test rows because every development target is resolved (0 NaN next_1y/next_2y for year <= 2017). The per-fold artist-overlap exclusion was tried and DROPPED: it removed 46.4% to 56.8% of each tranche's rows and cut the median history_count of the test rows to 0/0/1/1/1, biasing the tests toward debut artists, and it is not needed because features are point-in-time safe and train labels are gapped, so an artist with rows on both sides of a fold is not leakage. Without it the median history_count of the test rows is 2/3/3/3/4 and 33.8%/37.6%/41.2%/38.0%/41.5% of test rows belong to artists that also have train rows. Fold 5 test rows from 2016 and 2017 have next_1y/next_2y labels that read 2017 to 2019 activity; this is not a leak, a label needing real later activity is how the label is defined for any row, and the holdout's integrity only requires that holdout rows (2018 to 2023 snapshots) are never used in training or walk-forward evaluation, which holds. Tranches stay 1993 to 2017. Tranche length (5 years) is a provisional choice.
- Per-fold evaluation (10-10-2026, src/backtesting.py evaluate_folds; notebooks/03_when_backtesting_design.ipynb section 4): evaluate_folds(folds, target, fit_fn, score_fn) fits on each fold's gapped train rows only and returns one row per fold, never averaged: PR-AUC with 95% artist-clustered bootstrap CI (1,000 draws, KILL_GATE_CONFIDENCE and N_BOOTSTRAP from src/baselines.py), test positive rate, ROC-AUC for reference, train and test sizes; run separately for next_1y and next_2y (folds censored per target). The bootstrap is a fast re-implementation (_artist_bootstrap_pr_auc: artist draw counts used as row weights on rows sorted by score once) instead of baselines._bootstrap_pr_auc, because that function scans the full array once per artist (artists x rows operations, 138k to 275k artists per fold) and does not finish at fold size. baselines.py unchanged. Historical WHEN baseline (rank by historical_rate, fallback = train median) per fold 1 to 5, next_1y: positive rate 0.446/0.436/0.447/0.492/0.512, PR-AUC 0.605/0.610/0.648/0.686/0.713 (CI half-width about 0.009), ROC-AUC 0.642/0.658/0.686/0.705/0.714; next_2y: positive rate 0.585/0.583/0.595/0.626/0.639, PR-AUC 0.698/0.708/0.741/0.768/0.787, ROC-AUC 0.616/0.635/0.666/0.686/0.696. The baseline improves in recent tranches, it does not degrade; part of the PR-AUC rise is the rising positive rate (lift over it is flat, 1.36 to 1.45 for next_1y), ROC-AUC rise is the cleaner signal; the cause cannot be isolated (population and train size change together). These are the per-fold floors a model must beat before the holdout is touched.
- Feature allowlist for WHEN modeling (next_1y and next_2y, 10-10-2026, decided after the feature point-in-time audit entry above): the only columns that may be model inputs are works_so_far_this_year, albums_so_far_this_year, singles_so_far_this_year, is_album_like, is_single_like, history_count, years_since_last, years_active_so_far, historical_rate, genres. Excluded, with reason: works_this_year, albums_this_year, singles_this_year (not point-in-time safe, whole-year counts include same-year Works that come later in the sort order); artist_undated_work_count and year_missing (not confirmed safe, whole-catalog artist-level values that identify the artist across train and test); work_source (not confirmed safe, a Work is synthetic only if the artist has 3 or more standalone releases anywhere in the dump, which uses later releases); year and cutoff_year (point-in-time safe but excluded on purpose, a calendar-time feature would absorb the 2020 to 2025 submission-lag pattern and the era drift instead of generalizing); artist_id and work_id are keys (grouping, bootstrap clustering, joins), never features; next_1y, next_2y and new_style are targets. The audit entry's caveat still applies: revisit this entry if artist_id or any non-time-respecting artist-level aggregate is introduced as a raw feature, or if any excluded column is later proven safe and brought back.
- Logistic Regression, untuned, first model in the Step 5 sequence (Logistic Regression, Naive Bayes, Random Forest, HistGradientBoostingClassifier, XGBoost last and the only one tuned; 10-10-2026, src/models.py make_logistic_regression, notebooks/03_when_backtesting_design.ipynb section 5.1). Features: exactly the allowlist entry above (NUMERIC_FEATURES plus 11 genre columns, genres multi-hot encoded against a fixed vocabulary so every fold has the same columns; the stray '1' genre tag is not encoded; it appears on 4 Works only (4 snapshots, years 2002, 2004, 2005 and 2006), each of which also carries a real genre, so no row ends up with all genre columns at zero). Imputation (one explicit rule for every non-native-NaN model in the sequence): NaN in years_since_last (an artist's first Work: 281,426 of 1,401,791 fold-1 train rows) and historical_rate (no years elapsed yet: 379,480) is filled with the median of the fold's train rows, fitted on train only, the same rule fit_when_baseline uses for its fallback so model and baseline treat first Works alike. No missing-value indicator columns: history_count and years_active_so_far, both in the allowlist, already identify exactly the imputed rows, and isna flags would go beyond the 10-column allowlist. Only StandardScaler on top, no log transform of the heavy-tailed counts (history_count reaches 1,095), a known limit of this untuned linear model. LogisticRegression(max_iter=1000), default L2, no tuning. Result: beats the historical baseline in every fold for both targets, 95% CIs never overlap. next_1y PR-AUC 0.659/0.660/0.683/0.724/0.754 (baseline 0.605/0.610/0.648/0.686/0.713), ROC-AUC 0.688/0.696/0.714/0.736/0.752 (baseline 0.642/0.658/0.686/0.705/0.714); next_2y PR-AUC 0.763/0.764/0.775/0.797/0.820 (baseline 0.698/0.708/0.741/0.768/0.787), ROC-AUC 0.685/0.687/0.696/0.712/0.732 (baseline 0.616/0.635/0.666/0.686/0.696). PR-AUC gain over the baseline is largest in folds 1 and 2 (+0.054/+0.050 next_1y, +0.066/+0.056 next_2y) and +0.029 to +0.042 from fold 3 on; as with the baseline, scores rise in recent folds partly because the positive rate rises, ROC-AUC is the cleaner trend. This is the reference the tree models are compared against. Models compared in the WHEN and WHAT comparisons: Logistic Regression, Naive Bayes, Random Forest, HistGradientBoosting and XGBoost (five models). K-nearest neighbours was not evaluated. Its prediction cost grows with the number of training rows, and the snapshot tables hold millions of rows, so it was judged impractical at this scale. Not tested, so no claim is made about its performance.
- Code and notebook structure for the model steps (10-10-2026, src/models.py, notebooks/03_when_backtesting_design.ipynb): every non-trivial hyperparameter carries an inline comment saying what it does and why that value; the notebook uses numbered '## Step N' headers with an Interpretation cell after each result in plain language; tuning stays manual and staged, never an upfront search loop. The Logistic Regression code and notebook section were retrofitted to this format without changing logic or results (spot-checked: fold 1 PR-AUC 0.6589 and fold 5 0.7545 before and after).
- Naive Bayes, untuned (10-10-2026, src/models.py make_naive_bayes): Pipeline of the same median SimpleImputer and GaussianNB with default var_smoothing=1e-9, no scaler. LIMITATION, not fixed on purpose: GaussianNB models every feature as an independent Gaussian, but 13 of the 20 columns after encoding are 0/1 (2 format flags is_album_like and is_single_like plus 11 genre columns) and the counts are skewed and correlated, so the model assumption does not hold and its probabilities are unreliable; kept as a cheap reference point, not a competitive candidate, so no mixed Gaussian/Bernoulli implementation. Result: next_1y PR-AUC 0.628/0.621/0.647/0.695/0.727 and ROC-AUC 0.651/0.649/0.679/0.707/0.726, about level with the historical baseline (0.605/0.610/0.648/0.686/0.713) and below Logistic Regression in every fold; next_2y PR-AUC 0.744/0.737/0.751/0.778/0.802 (baseline 0.698/0.708/0.741/0.768/0.787), ROC-AUC 0.659/0.648/0.665/0.686/0.711 level with the baseline from fold 3 on, below Logistic Regression in every fold (0.763/0.764/0.775/0.797/0.820).
- Random Forest, untuned (10-10-2026, src/models.py make_random_forest): same median imputation, RandomForestClassifier with n_estimators=200, max_depth=12, min_samples_leaf=50, max_features='sqrt', max_samples=0.25, random_state=1; common starting values for tabular data, no search. Result: best model so far in every fold for both targets, 95% intervals do not overlap with Logistic Regression. next_1y PR-AUC 0.696/0.701/0.719/0.746/0.771 (Logistic Regression 0.659/0.660/0.683/0.724/0.754), ROC-AUC 0.730/0.740/0.747/0.752/0.764; next_2y PR-AUC 0.796/0.798/0.805/0.816/0.836 (Logistic Regression 0.763/0.764/0.775/0.797/0.820), ROC-AUC 0.732/0.733/0.730/0.730/0.745. Gain over Logistic Regression shrinks in recent folds (next_1y PR-AUC +0.037/+0.041/+0.036/+0.022/+0.017) because Logistic Regression's ROC-AUC rises with fold while the forest's is flat for next_2y. Reference for HistGradientBoostingClassifier and XGBoost.
- HistGradientBoostingClassifier, untuned (10-10-2026, src/models.py make_hist_gradient_boosting): no imputer and no scaler (NaN in years_since_last and historical_rate handled natively), learning_rate=0.1, max_iter=200, max_leaf_nodes=31, min_samples_leaf=50 (same as the Random Forest), early_stopping=False (all train rows used, no internal validation split), random_state=1; common starting values, no search. Result: tied with the untuned Random Forest, differences at most 0.002 against 95% intervals 0.007 to 0.018 wide. next_1y PR-AUC 0.697/0.702/0.720/0.745/0.771 (Random Forest 0.696/0.701/0.719/0.746/0.771), ROC-AUC 0.730/0.741/0.749/0.753/0.764; next_2y PR-AUC 0.797/0.799/0.806/0.816/0.836 (Random Forest 0.796/0.798/0.805/0.816/0.836), ROC-AUC 0.732/0.734/0.732/0.731/0.746. A bagged and a boosted ensemble reach the same score on the same 10 features, so the limit is probably the information in the allowlisted features, not the model family; expect a small gain from XGBoost tuning.
- Notebook execution workflow (10-10-2026): no result cache and no AST-hash disk cache (more infrastructure than the project needs). New model cells are run on their own in the live kernel session, reusing the variables already computed by earlier cells; a clean top-to-bottom re-run is done only at checkpoints (for example once before closing Step 5), not after every edit. For the XGBoost tuning only: per-fold features, labels and DMatrix are built once and held in memory (not persisted), each finished trial is appended to results/trials/xgb_{target}.jsonl (tracked in git as the tuning record, resumable if interrupted), the bootstrap CI is skipped during the search and computed once for the final chosen configuration through evaluate_folds. Trials are ranked by mean PR-AUC across the 5 folds, but the per-fold table is always read before a change is accepted. next_1y and next_2y are tuned separately.
- XGBoost stage 0, untuned reference for the staged tuning (13-10-2026, src/models.py make_xgboost): XGBClassifier with no imputer and no scaler (native NaN), n_estimators=300, learning_rate=0.1, max_depth=6, min_child_weight=50 (hessian sum, at most 0.25 per row for the logistic loss, so at least 200 rows at p=0.5: the XGBoost counterpart of the 50-row leaf floor of the other trees, not an exact match), subsample=0.8, colsample_bytree=0.8, reg_lambda=1.0, tree_method='hist', eval_metric='aucpr' (no eval set is passed, so it does not change the fit), n_jobs=-1, random_state=1. Result: tied with HistGradientBoosting and the Random Forest, differences at most 0.002 against 95% intervals 0.007 to 0.018 wide. next_1y PR-AUC 0.698/0.702/0.720/0.747/0.771, ROC-AUC 0.731/0.741/0.749/0.754/0.764; next_2y PR-AUC 0.797/0.800/0.806/0.817/0.837, ROC-AUC 0.733/0.735/0.732/0.732/0.746. Tuning plan: three phases, each narrowing on the findings of the previous one, next_1y and next_2y tuned separately: (1) random search, about 35 random combinations per target over max_depth, min_child_weight, subsample, colsample_bytree, reg_lambda, reg_alpha and learning_rate at once (so interactions can show up), n_estimators fixed at 300; (2) grid search, a focused grid centered on the best region of the random search; (3) manual tuning, one or two hyperparameters changed one at a time (this phase decides the final configuration, learning_rate with n_estimators is settled here). Trials are ranked by mean PR-AUC across the 5 folds, the per-fold table is always read before a candidate is accepted, no bootstrap CI during the search (computed once for the final configuration through evaluate_folds), every finished trial is appended to results/trials/xgb_{target}.jsonl so an interrupted run resumes. Search ranges come from the reference table: max_depth 3-10, subsample 0.5-1.0, colsample_bytree 0.3-1.0, reg_alpha and reg_lambda 0.001-9; learning_rate is narrowed to 0.03-0.3 (the table's 0.00001-1 is far wider than 300 rounds can use) and min_child_weight to 5-500 (no table range, 50 was the stage 0 value); n_estimators 100-1000 is explored in the last phase. scale_pos_weight is tested explicitly alongside regularization and reported whether or not it changes anything (fold positive rates are 0.44-0.64, not severe imbalance, but not assumed irrelevant). early_stopping_rounds stays off, same reason as HistGradientBoosting early_stopping=False: it would carve a validation split out of each fold's train rows and break the fair comparison with every model that trains on the full fold.
- XGBoost tuning results, three phases (13-10-2026, src/tuning.py, scripts/run_when_xgb_random_search.py, run_when_xgb_grid_search.py, run_when_xgb_manual.py; every trial in results/trials/xgb_{target}.jsonl, 68 and 69 rows): the tuning confirmed a plateau, no meaningful gain is available. Stage 0 mean PR-AUC over the 5 folds: next_1y 0.7277, next_2y 0.8112. RANDOM SEARCH (35 combinations per target over max_depth 3-10, min_child_weight 5-500, subsample 0.5-1.0, colsample_bytree 0.3-1.0, reg_lambda and reg_alpha 0.001-9, learning_rate 0.03-0.3, n_estimators 300): best 0.7280 (trial 27) and 0.8115 (trial 5), median 0.7273 and 0.8110, worst 0.7250 and 0.8092; 10 of 35 beat stage 0 in each target, 23 and 28 of 35 within 0.0005 of it; rank correlation with the mean: max_depth +0.34/+0.40, reg_lambda +0.45/+0.45, learning_rate -0.38/-0.27, others weak; worst trials were shallow (depth 3-5) with low colsample_bytree. GRID SEARCH (27 combinations per target: max_depth 7/9/11, reg_lambda 2/5/15, learning_rate 0.03/0.05/0.08; fixed subsample 0.8, colsample_bytree 0.55, min_child_weight 50, reg_alpha 0.01, n_estimators 500; 0 failures): range 0.7264-0.7280 and 0.8104-0.8114, best g8 (depth 7, lambda 15, lr 0.05) 0.7280 and g10 (depth 9, lambda 2, lr 0.03) 0.8114; within the grid max_depth rank correlation -0.68/-0.69 and learning_rate -0.62/-0.58 (shallower and slower are better, effect about 0.0003 to 0.0008), reg_lambda +0.17/+0.10 (no real effect); next_2y's best sat at lr 0.03 so it was rerun with 1000 trees: 0.8112 vs 0.8114 at 500, more trees did not help; next_1y's best was not at an edge of depth or lr (lambda 15 is the top of its axis, but lambda 2 ties it). MANUAL PHASE (5 hand-picked trials per target, one fit each): depth 7 and depth 9 with their best grid lr and lambda reproduced the grid numbers exactly (0.7280/0.7279 and 0.8114/0.8114, fits are deterministic); learning_rate 0.02 with 750 trees (below the grid) 0.7279 and 0.8114, no gain, the plateau extends below the grid; learning_rate 0.12 (above the grid) 0.7274 and 0.8104, the only trial clearly worse (-0.0006 and -0.0010), the plateau ends there; scale_pos_weight set to negatives per positive (0.973 and 0.589) 0.7280 and 0.8113, no change (positive rates 0.44-0.64 are not severe imbalance); no fold moved by more than 0.0012 against the grid winner in any manual trial. Spread of the whole tuning (0.0016 and 0.0010) is about a tenth of the 95% interval width (mean 0.015 for next_1y and 0.010 for next_2y): a manual phase that confirms no further gain is the finding. early_stopping_rounds stayed off. LOCKED final configurations (13-10-2026, after the train versus test check below showed no extra overfitting; best grid combination per target, src/models.py FINAL_XGB_PARAMS): next_1y depth 7, lambda 15, lr 0.05 (trial g8); next_2y depth 9, lambda 2, lr 0.03 (trial g10); both n_estimators 500, subsample 0.8, colsample_bytree 0.55, min_child_weight 50, reg_alpha 0.01, every other value as in stage 0. They are no longer candidates and are not to be changed after the holdout is seen. Full bootstrap (1,000 artist-clustered draws, 95%, evaluate_folds, results/trials/xgb_final_{target}_ci95.csv) PR-AUC [CI]: next_1y 0.6983 [0.6898, 0.7062], 0.7024 [0.6932, 0.7110], 0.7208 [0.7124, 0.7287], 0.7470 [0.7409, 0.7530], 0.7714 [0.7657, 0.7770]; next_2y 0.7972 [0.7920, 0.8021], 0.7995 [0.7942, 0.8045], 0.8063 [0.8009, 0.8114], 0.8172 [0.8114, 0.8222], 0.8368 [0.8333, 0.8404]; ROC-AUC next_1y 0.731/0.741/0.749/0.754/0.765, next_2y 0.733/0.735/0.733/0.733/0.746.
- XGBoost train versus test check (13-10-2026, scripts/run_when_xgb_overfit_check.py, results/trials/xgb_overfit_{target}.csv): PR-AUC on each fold's own train rows against its test rows for stage 0, the best random trial, the final grid combination and the other tested depth, to see whether tuning bought its test score with more overfitting. Result: it did not, the gap is the same for every candidate. Mean train-test gap next_1y: stage 0 0.0946, best random 0.0950, final grid 0.0946, other depth 0.0953; next_2y: stage 0 0.0627, best random 0.0622, final grid 0.0633, other depth 0.0626. Per fold gap, stage 0 versus final grid: next_1y 0.1544/0.1342/0.0995/0.0599/0.0251 versus 0.1544/0.1342/0.0995/0.0598/0.0250; next_2y 0.0979/0.0843/0.0663/0.0462/0.0189 versus 0.0986/0.0851/0.0669/0.0465/0.0194. Train PR-AUC next_1y 0.8223 (stage 0) versus 0.8226 (final), next_2y 0.8739 versus 0.8747. Finding: no reason to prefer a less-tuned configuration. The gap itself is large in early folds (0.15 in fold 1 for next_1y) and shrinks with fold (0.025 in fold 5) as train grows and test positive rates rise, so it is a property of the model family on these features, shared by stage 0 and every tuned candidate, not something tuning added. Caveat: train and test PR-AUC have different positive rates, so the gap is a diagnostic, not a pure measure of overfitting; the gap of the other models (Random Forest, HistGradientBoosting) was not measured.
- FINAL holdout evaluation (13-10-2026, scripts/run_when_holdout.py, src/backtesting.py evaluate_holdout; notebooks/03_when_backtesting_design.ipynb Step 7), evaluated on 13-10-2026 (the script refuses a second run) and re-scored one more time on 14-10-2026, solely to obtain 95% intervals, with scripts/run_when_holdout_ci95.py (results/holdout_ci95/*.json): same final training rows, locked parameters, seed and bootstrap, no model, feature or setting change, and every point estimate, count and per-year value identical to the first evaluation: locked XGBoost (FINAL_XGB_PARAMS) and the historical WHEN baseline fitted on final_train (3,786,030 development rows, years up to 2015, the 2016-2017 gap rows removed) and scored on the sealed holdout, years 2018 to 2023, 1,020,151 snapshots, 291,481 artists, 0 rows dropped by censoring. next_1y (positive rate 0.542): XGBoost PR-AUC 0.7851 [0.7780, 0.7923], ROC-AUC 0.7653 [0.7623, 0.7684]; baseline PR-AUC 0.7350 [0.7267, 0.7438], ROC-AUC 0.7185 [0.7149, 0.7223]. next_2y (positive rate 0.643): XGBoost PR-AUC 0.8352 [0.8302, 0.8404], ROC-AUC 0.7494 [0.7464, 0.7525]; baseline PR-AUC 0.7894 [0.7830, 0.7959], ROC-AUC 0.6993 [0.6959, 0.7028] (95% artist-clustered bootstrap, 1,000 draws). The model beats the baseline for both targets with non-overlapping intervals. The holdout PR-AUC is above the last walk-forward fold (0.7714 and 0.8368) because the holdout positive rate is higher (0.542 against 0.512 for next_1y); the ROC-AUC, which does not depend on it, matches fold 5 (0.7653 against 0.7647 and 0.7494 against 0.7464), so the walk-forward estimate held on unseen years. By holdout year (descriptive only) next_1y PR-AUC 0.796/0.804/0.789/0.777/0.772/0.782 and ROC-AUC 0.774/0.775/0.759/0.758/0.763/0.771 for 2018 to 2023: a dip in 2020 to 2022 that recovers in 2023, and 2023 is not worse, so the submission lag of recent years does not visibly hurt here; next_2y PR-AUC declines slowly (0.853 to 0.825) with its positive rate (0.656 to 0.622). Precision and recall of the locked model at the default 0.5 threshold, next_1y: new work precision 0.7121, recall 0.7345, f1 0.7231 (support 552,877); no new work precision 0.6737, recall 0.6486, f1 0.6609 (support 467,274); accuracy 0.6952. In plain terms: of the snapshots the model flagged as followed by new work within a year, 71.2% were (base rate 54.2%), and of those that were, it caught 73.5%. The unit is a snapshot (an artist at the time of one of their works), not a whole artist. next_2y at 0.5: new work precision 0.7367, recall 0.8540; no new work precision 0.6306, recall 0.4495; accuracy 0.7097 (the model leans toward predicting new work, positive rate 0.643). Limits: calibration of the probabilities was not checked (0.5 is a convention, not a tuned operating point); the threshold report covers the locked model only (the baseline is a ranking score). The holdout is now used: no further tuning, feature or model changes are evaluated on it. Other models (Logistic Regression, Naive Bayes, Random Forest, HistGradientBoosting) were deliberately not scored on the holdout so that it was not used to choose between models; their walk-forward numbers stand as the comparison.
- XGBoost tuning notebook record (13-10-2026, notebooks/03_when_backtesting_design.ipynb Step 6): reads results/trials/ (trial logs and train-versus-test CSVs) with trials_frame in src/tuning.py, nothing refitted; the configurations in the XGBoost tuning results entry are LOCKED before the holdout evaluation.
- WHAT is modeled at artist-year level (13-10-2026, src/what_target.py build_artist_years): row = artist and calendar year; target new_style_year = 1 if the Works of the artist's next active year contain at least one style absent from every style seen through the end of the current year (styles of undated Works count as seen, same convention as the Work-level target), NaN when there is no next active year or the next year's Works carry no styles. Reason: the Work-level label depends on the seeded pseudo-random order inside a year, because the data only knows the year (57.0% of labelled Work-level transitions with history >= 3 go to a Work of the same year, 1,381,521 of 2,423,318). Validation on development years (year < 2018, src/what_target.py crosscheck_levels): 0 violations of the implication (Work-level 1 on the last Work of a year implies year-level 1, 524,587 rows checked) and 0 mismatches of the equality when the next year has exactly one Work (1,045,826 rows); the year-level label sees positives the Work-level label cannot in 11.7% (next year with 2 Works, 268,921 rows), 20.9% (3-4 Works, 162,882 rows) and 33.4% (5+ Works, 75,666 rows) of compared rows. Prevalence with history >= 3 (prevalence_by_fold): 30.9% over 763,371 labelled rows (1,067,817 eligible artist-years, 267,339 artists; NaN label share 28.5% overall, 27.5% to 31.4% by window), stable across the five WHEN test tranches (30.2% to 32.7%). No label statistic was computed on the holdout years (2018-2023), only row and artist counts (312,392 artist-years, 151,788 artists).
- Known limitation of the artist-year target (13-10-2026, src/what_target.py prevalence_by_next_year_volume): the label depends on next-year volume. Prevalence is 25.4% when the next active year has 1 Work, 33.5% with 2, 39.7% with 3-4 and 47.2% with 5+ (development years, history >= 3, 763,371 labelled rows). Next-year volume is not known at the cutoff and must never be a model input. The history gradient survives with volume held fixed (31.0% down to 11.7% across history buckets 3-5 to 51+ when the next year has one Work; 58.9% down to 34.4% when it has 5+). Volume is partly predictable from the current year's Work count (Spearman 0.43 between n_works_year and n_works_next_year; 71.5% of artist-years with 1 Work are followed by a single-Work year, against 15.2% for those with 5+). WHAT is therefore conditional on a next active year with style tags and measures a volume-dependent event; documentation and the demo must say so.
- History-bucket gradient at artist-year level is flatter than at Work level (13-10-2026, src/what_target.py prevalence_by_history_bucket): development years, history >= 3, artist-year prevalence 36.3%, 32.4%, 28.9%, 24.8%, 19.9% for buckets 3-5, 6-10, 11-20, 21-50, 51+ (1,067,817 eligible artist-years) against Work-level 28.7%, 22.7%, 17.6%, 12.8%, 7.3% (2,320,628 eligible rows). Both fall monotonically. The flatter artist-year slope is consistent with the volume effect in the previous entry (prolific artists release several Works per year).
- Config cleanup (13-10-2026, config.yaml): the key when.min_history_years (value 2) was provisional and was never read by any code (only config.yaml contained it), and the WHEN results (folds, sealed holdout, model comparison) were produced on all snapshots without a minimum-history filter (snapshots.parquet contains rows with history_count = 0). The key was removed from config.yaml on 13-10-2026. what.min_previous_works: 3 is kept (read by notebooks/02_eda_visualization.ipynb).
- Label loss of the artist-year target (13-10-2026, src/what_target.py label_loss_breakdown, development years, history >= 3): the 1,067,817 eligible artist-years split into 763,371 labelled (71.5%), 179,128 with no next active year (16.8%, intrinsic: WHAT is conditional on a future release, WHEN covers that case) and 125,318 whose next active year has no style tags (11.7%). Of those 125,318, 76,458 (61.0%) have a later active year with style tags (extra delay in years: p50 2, p75 5, p90 10, p99 29) and 48,860 (39.0%) have none. Of the 76,458 recoverable rows, 14,137 have their next styled year in 2018 or later, so their label would use holdout-year style tags: they are counted but left out of every rate. The other 62,321 would have a label prevalence of 40.0% against 30.9% for the currently labelled rows (31.6% for both together), because the horizon is longer and variable. Decision: keep the current definition (next active year) as the single primary target, so the label keeps one meaning; the tolerant definition is not used for training or evaluation of the main result.
- Loss from missing style tags is concentrated by primary genre, not by period or history (13-10-2026, src/what_target.py label_loss_breakdown, development years, history >= 3; primary genre = most frequent genre tag across dated Works, fake tag "1" excluded, 0 artists left without a genre). Share of eligible rows lost to missing style tags by genre: Electronic 0.6% (247,980 rows), Rock 9.6% (241,123), Pop 17.0% (183,242), Folk, World, & Country 17.9% (153,036), Jazz 20.5% (63,571), Latin 12.0% (54,553), Hip Hop 31.8% (39,030), Funk / Soul 9.1% (34,507), Reggae 11.5% (25,654), Stage & Screen 7.8% (13,743), Blues 26.7% (11,378); spread about 55x between highest and lowest. By period the share falls from 13.2% (before 1993) to 9.3% (2013-17), spread 1.42x; by history bucket it stays between 11.2% and 12.5%, spread 1.12x. Consequence: the labelled population over-represents genres whose Works carry style tags (Electronic, Rock); the limitation must be stated in the documentation and the demo, and every kill gate result must also be reported by primary genre.
- Exact label-resolution rule for the WHAT folds (13-10-2026, src/backtesting.py make_what_folds, same five tranches as WHEN: 1993-97, 1998-02, 2003-07, 2008-12, 2013-17): a train row is kept only if its next_active_year is earlier than the test start. Train rows per fold: 232,404 / 282,606 / 347,763 / 430,809 / 539,157; test rows 58,141 / 74,558 / 93,072 / 121,659 / 156,591; rows dropped by the rule 26,946 / 34,885 / 44,286 / 54,312 / 67,623. Against a fixed 2-year gap, 5.2% to 5.7% of the fixed-gap train rows (13,202 / 16,096 / 20,603 / 24,696 / 28,508) have a label that resolves at or after the test start; the exact rule closes that leak at a net loss of 2.5% to 3.5% of train rows (8,028 / 9,479 / 12,438 / 14,560 / 13,892). Between 39.4% and 44.0% of test rows per fold have a label that looks beyond the tranche end (43.8% / 44.0% / 43.9% / 43.4% / 39.4%, same situation as the WHEN test labels).
- Baseline ladder for the WHAT kill gate (13-10-2026, src/baselines.py fit_what_baseline and fit_what_lookup_baseline, evaluated per fold with evaluate_folds on the make_what_folds folds, fast artist-clustered bootstrap, 1,000 draws, 95% CI, development years only). PR-AUC per fold 1 to 5: constant 0.3064 / 0.3018 / 0.3161 / 0.3266 / 0.3228 (equal to the positive rate, ROC-AUC 0.500); lookup on history bucket 0.3381 / 0.3356 / 0.3533 / 0.3615 / 0.3581 (ROC-AUC 0.559 to 0.568); lookup on history bucket and current-year volume bucket 0.3474 / 0.3479 / 0.3625 / 0.3698 / 0.3597 (ROC-AUC 0.563 to 0.575). CI half-width is 0.26 to 0.57 points (0.35 to 0.57 for the last baseline). History adds 3.2 to 3.7 points over the constant; volume adds a further 0.15 to 1.23 points (+0.93 / +1.23 / +0.92 / +0.83 / +0.15 per fold): the intervals overlap in folds 1 and 5 and are separated in folds 2 to 4 by thin margins (+0.24, +0.09 and +0.09 points). The kill gate reference is the history and volume lookup baseline, the strongest information available at the cutoff. Cells under MIN_CELL_ROWS = 30 fall back to the global train rate (none did in fold 5).
- Kill gate pass rule for WHAT, fixed before any WHAT model result exists (13-10-2026): the model passes only if (a) in at least 4 of the 5 development folds the lower bound of its 95% artist-clustered bootstrap CI for PR-AUC is above the upper bound of the CI of the reference baseline (history bucket and current-year volume bucket lookup), AND the PR-AUC improvement over that baseline in those folds is at least 1.0 point (absolute PR-AUC), AND (b) in no fold the model's CI lies entirely below the baseline's CI. Reasons. Four of five: folds are expanding-window tranches that share artists, so they are not independent and the rule is a consistency criterion, not a probability statement; requiring all five would let a single atypical tranche decide (fold 1 trains on the least and oldest data, fold 5 already shows an anomaly where volume adds only 0.15 points), requiring only the latest tranche ignores stability over time, and clause (b) prevents the tolerated failure from being a clear regression; the value 4 is a convention fixed in advance. Margin of 1.0 point: with 58,141 to 156,591 test rows per fold the CI half-width is 0.26 to 0.57 points (0.35 to 0.57 for the reference baseline), so a pure CI separation rule could be passed by differences too small to matter; the margin separates statistically distinguishable from practically relevant. The value is anchored on the data (adding current-year volume to history improved PR-AUC by 0.15 to 1.23 points, about 0.8 on average, so style features are required to add at least as much as a simple Work count) and is about 1.8 to 2.8 times the half-width of the reference baseline CI; it is a judgment value, not derived. Reporting: results are shown fold by fold with the margin in each fold, overall and by primary genre; passing the gate only authorizes further work on WHAT, and the sealed holdout is evaluated once at the end, as for WHEN. Two 95% CIs separate only if the PR-AUC difference exceeds the sum of their half-widths (about 1.13, 1.07, 0.89, 0.77 and 0.71 points in folds 1 to 5, assuming model and reference baseline have similar CI widths), so the 1.0-point margin is stricter than the CI condition in folds 3 to 5 and weaker than it in folds 1 and 2. The rule was fixed at 90% before any WHAT model result existed; its confidence level, half-widths and margin rationale are restated at 95% afterwards (see the DECIDED entry "Confidence level moved from 90% to 95%").
- Confidence level moved from 90% to 95% (14-10-2026, src/baselines.py KILL_GATE_CONFIDENCE): 95% is the conventional level, and a non-standard level only adds something to defend. The original 90% was fixed before any result, on a premise recorded in a code comment (95% intervals would let almost nothing clear the gate) with no measurement behind it; the premise did not hold for the WHAT gate. The change was made after results, on a request about convention, not forced by any result. A sensitivity check came first (point estimates identical): the WHAT gate passes at both levels, smallest margin of the model's lower bound over the upper bound of (c) about 9.9 points at 95%. The WHAT pass rule, fixed at 90% before any result, is restated at 95% afterwards. Two WHEN results were recomputed at 95% from the same deterministic models (every point estimate identical, each interval containing the earlier one): the tuned final configuration (scripts/run_when_xgb_final_ci95.py, results/trials/xgb_final_next_*_ci95.csv) and the sealed holdout (scripts/run_when_holdout_ci95.py, results/holdout_ci95/*.json), re-scored once more on 14-10-2026 solely for the 95% bounds, with no model, feature or setting change. The one-off comparison of the two bootstrap implementations in "Per-fold evaluation" had no script behind it; its numbers were removed. Two interval readings changed at 95%: Naive Bayes against baseline for next_2y (separated above in folds 1, 2 and 5, no longer fold 4) and the baseline ladder (c) over (b) (overlap in folds 1 and 5, previously fold 5 only). All other non-overlap conclusions and the tree ties held at both levels (check figures not kept); the thinnest separations at 95% are Logistic Regression over Naive Bayes in WHAT fold 1 (0.48) and the trees over Logistic Regression in WHEN next_1y fold 5 (0.39 to 0.44). Also, 03_when_backtesting_design cell 46 gave folds 2 and 3 for the Naive Bayes overlap with baseline in next_1y; the table gives 2, 3 and 4 at both levels (corrected).
- Genre change measurement and decision to keep styles as the only primary target (13-10-2026, src/what_target.py genre_change_summary and build_artist_year_genres, development years, history >= 3, 1,067,817 eligible artist-years; fake genre tag "1" excluded): a new genre (the next active year brings a genre not seen so far, genres of undated Works count as seen) has prevalence 8.0% over 888,689 labelled rows, against 30.9% for a new style over 763,371. By tranche 1993-97 to 2013-17 the genre rate is 8.1 / 7.5 / 7.5 / 7.1 / 6.3% (style 30.6 / 30.2 / 31.6 / 32.7 / 32.3%); by history bucket 3-5 to 51+ it is 9.8 / 8.4 / 7.3 / 6.1 / 4.2% (style 36.3 / 32.4 / 28.9 / 24.8 / 19.9%); by next-year volume (1, 2, 3-4, 5+ Works) it is 6.2 / 9.1 / 11.5 / 14.5% (style 25.4 / 33.5 / 39.7 / 47.2%). Genre labels have no loss from missing tags (0 rows, against 125,318 for styles). On the 763,371 rows with both labels: no new style and no new genre 66.9%, new style without new genre 24.8%, new genre 8.3% (of which 2.2% new genre without new style); P(new genre | new style) = 19.8% and P(new style | new genre) = 73.9%. On the 125,318 rows without style tags the new genre rate is 6.5% against 8.3% on the rows where both labels exist (not a clean comparison: the two groups differ in genre mix). Decision: new genre is not used as a target (rare, falling over time, only 11 values, mostly implied by a style change); genre is used to stratify the kill gate results and the three-level split is available for the demo narrative.
- WHAT feature set and point-in-time audit (14-10-2026, src/what_target.py build_what_features, what_point_in_time_check, what_undated_check): one row per artist-year with the keys of build_artist_years (3,294,781 rows), 23 feature columns in 12 groups, each computed at the end of the year from dated Works up to that year only (undated Works enter no feature, unlike the label, where their styles count as seen; missing values stay NaN, nothing is imputed). Groups: (1) n_styles_seen, distinct styles used through the year; (2) n_styles_year, distinct styles in the year's Works; (3) n_new_styles_year, styles of the year absent from every earlier dated year; (4) styles_per_work, n_styles_seen over dated Works so far; (5) new_style_rate_so_far, mean of the novelty indicator over novelty-eligible years so far; (6) years_since_new_style, years since the last novelty-eligible year with a new style; (7) years_since_prev_active_year, gap to the previous active year; (8) years_active_so_far, years since the first active year; (9) top_style_share, share of style tag occurrences held by the most frequent style; (10) n_genres_seen, distinct genres so far (fake tag "1" removed) plus 11 genre_<name> flags (genres of models.GENRES carried by a Work of the year); (11) albums_share_year and (12) singles_share_year, share of the year's Works that are album-like or single-like (classification of the snapshots). history_count and n_works_year come from build_what_table; the label and n_works_next_year are never features. Novelty-eligible year rule: a year is eligible only if it has style tags and the dated vocabulary of earlier years is not empty, so the first active year and the first year with style tags are not novelty (n_new_styles_year is NaN there, and both are ignored by the rate and by years_since_new_style), because otherwise an artist's tagging habit would be read as novelty (every style is new by definition when the vocabulary is empty). Audit (30,000 random artists, seed 1; Works dated after the cutoff removed, undated kept; features and artist-years rebuilt and compared on rows with year <= cutoff, with the (artist_id, year) rows asserted identical first): cutoffs 1990, 2000, 2010 compare 21,825 / 34,781 / 54,068 rows and show 0 differing rows in all 23 columns at every cutoff. Positive controls look forward by design and are asserted to differ: new_style_year differs in 1,671 / 2,830 / 4,228 rows and n_works_next_year in 2,096 / 3,487 / 5,069 rows, all NaN versus value. Undated check (same sample, features with and without all undated Works): 95,346 rows compared, 0 differences in all 23 columns. Limits: the cut is at year level (not a blind spot here, because every feature is defined at the end of its year); the sample is about 3% of artists; new_style_rate_so_far passes by construction, since its indicator is computed inside the same pass from dated Works only.
- WHAT kill gate: PASSED (14-10-2026, src/what_target.py run_what_gate, src/models.py make_what_xgboost; development folds only, evaluated once, sealed holdout 2018-2023 untouched): model = XGBoost stage 0 with the same settings as the WHEN stage 0 (300 rounds, depth 6, min_child_weight 50, seed 1, no imputation, NaN handled natively, binary objective, no class weights; the full get_params of both models are identical), 25 inputs from the named constants WHAT_FEATURES (history_count, n_works_year and the 23 columns of build_what_features), single configuration, no tuning. Rule as logged in the entry "Kill gate pass rule for WHAT" (4 of 5 folds with separated 95% artist-clustered intervals and an improvement of at least 1.0 point over baseline (c), and no fold with the model interval entirely below that of (c)), applied on unrounded values with the same folds, bootstrap size, confidence and seed as the baseline ladder; baseline (c) reproduced the ladder in every fold (0.3474 / 0.3479 / 0.3625 / 0.3698 / 0.3597) and the model gave non-constant scores in every fold. Per fold 1 to 5 (train rows / test rows / test artists; model PR-AUC [95% CI]; baseline (c) PR-AUC [95% CI]; improvement in points; rule (a) met; rule (b) broken): fold 1 (1993-97) 232,404 / 58,141 / 30,253; 0.4607 [0.4525, 0.4681]; 0.3474 [0.3418, 0.3531]; +11.33; yes; no. Fold 2 (1998-02) 282,606 / 74,558 / 38,845; 0.4751 [0.4680, 0.4820]; 0.3479 [0.3428, 0.3534]; +12.72; yes; no. Fold 3 (2003-07) 347,763 / 93,072 / 48,911; 0.4946 [0.4881, 0.5006]; 0.3625 [0.3582, 0.3670]; +13.21; yes; no. Fold 4 (2008-12) 430,809 / 121,659 / 64,168; 0.4947 [0.4898, 0.4998]; 0.3698 [0.3660, 0.3737]; +12.50; yes; no. Fold 5 (2013-17) 539,157 / 156,591 / 79,182; 0.5002 [0.4953, 0.5053]; 0.3597 [0.3564, 0.3634]; +14.05; yes; no. Verdict: PASS, rule (a) met in 5 of 5 folds (4 required), rule (b) broken in 0 folds. Pooled ROC-AUC over the 504,021 test rows: 0.6732 for the model against 0.5712 for baseline (c). Genre view, descriptive only and no decision taken on genres (primary genre from the artist's full Works, used for stratification and never as a model input; scores pooled over the five disjoint test tranches, 504,021 rows): the model is above baseline (c) in all 11 primary genres, with pooled differences from +8.8 points (Funk / Soul) to +20.6 points (Hip Hop), and +12.7 points over all rows (0.4899 against 0.3628). Limits, stated as limits and not as findings: the improvement (11 to 14 points) is much larger than the contribution of history (3 to 4 points) and of the current-year volume (0.15 to 1.2 points); the audit shows the features are point-in-time safe; 43.2% to 46.8% of the test rows belong to artists that also have train rows, so part of the gain may be a stable per-artist habit of adding styles; tagging habit and next-year volume could also play a role. These are hypotheses to be diagnosed (see the DECIDED entry "WHAT gate diagnostics: results"), not conclusions.
- WHAT gate diagnostics: results (14-10-2026, src/what_target.py gate_diagnostic_returning_artists, gate_diagnostic_next_year_volume, gate_diagnostic_feature_ablation; development folds only, same fold dicts, bootstrap settings and seed as the gate, one configuration, no tuning, sealed holdout 2018-2023 untouched): post-hoc, run because the gain over baseline (c) (11.3 to 14.1 PR-AUC points) was large compared with the contribution of history (3 to 4 points) and of the current-year volume (0.15 to 1.2 points); they do not change the gate verdict, which stands as pre-registered. D1 to D3 use the row-level scores of the gate (504,021 test rows), whose per-fold PR-AUC reproduces the gate table to 6 decimals, with no refit.
