D1, new versus returning artists (new = no row of the artist in that fold's train table): new artists are 55.0% of the test rows (277,343, positive rate 36.6%) with model PR-AUC 0.5323 against 0.3788 for (c), +15.3 points (ROC-AUC +14.2 points); returning artists are 45.0% (226,678, positive rate 25.8%) with 0.3950 against 0.2904, +10.5 points (ROC-AUC +10.8 points); by fold the difference is +13.4 / +15.1 / +15.8 / +15.1 / +17.0 points for new artists and +10.6 / +11.0 / +11.3 / +10.0 / +11.1 for returning artists, so the gain is larger for artists with no train row and a per-artist memory explanation cannot account for it there (it does not exclude a contribution for returning artists). D2, within next-year Work count (used only to stratify the scores, never as an input): the model is above (c) in all 4 buckets (1, 2, 3-4 and 5+ Works; 61.2% / 19.5% / 13.3% / 6.0% of the rows, positive rates 26.3% / 35.8% / 42.6% / 50.0%) and in all 5 folds, with PR-AUC differences of +13.3 / +12.5 / +12.1 / +12.5 points pooled by bucket and from +9.2 to +14.6 points across fold and bucket, and ROC-AUC differences of +9.7 / +8.8 / +9.1 / +11.7 points pooled by bucket; the pooled ROC-AUC on all rows is 0.6732 for the model against 0.6694 as the row-weighted mean of the within-bucket values (0.6678 unweighted), a gap of 0.4 points, so almost none of the ranking comes from separating volume buckets (baseline (c): 0.5712 against 0.5737, gap -0.2 points). D3, ROC-AUC next to PR-AUC because PR-AUC depends on the base rate of each subset, is reported inside D1 and D2. D4, leave-one-group-out ablation with refits (groups TABLE_INPUTS 2 inputs, VOCABULARY 5, NOVELTY 3, TIMING_FORMAT 4, GENRE_FLAGS 11, partitioning the 25 inputs; same setup and folds as the gate, the full model and (c) reused from the gate): mean PR-AUC over the 5 folds (pooled ROC-AUC) is full 0.4851 (0.6732); minus VOCABULARY 0.4695, -1.56 points (0.6604); minus NOVELTY 0.4777, -0.73 (0.6681); minus TIMING_FORMAT 0.4829, -0.21 (0.6716); minus GENRE_FLAGS 0.4769, -0.82 (0.6649); TABLE_INPUTS only 0.3630, +0.56 over (c) (0.5739); baseline (c) 0.3575 (0.5712). All four ablated models stay above (c) in 5 of 5 folds; the table-inputs-only model is above (c) in 0 of 5. Against the full model, minus VOCABULARY is below its interval in 4 of 5 folds, minus GENRE_FLAGS in 1 of 5, and minus NOVELTY and minus TIMING_FORMAT in 0 of 5. Hypotheses, not conclusions: the gain needs the added columns and not the two table inputs; no single group carries it, the groups overlap in what they tell the model; novelty is not the main source; leave-one-group-out understates the value of each group under overlap and does not say which group would suffice alone (single-group runs were not run and are not needed for any pending decision).
- No feature change follows from the WHAT gate diagnostics (14-10-2026): the feature set stays at the 25 inputs of WHAT_FEATURES and the 5-model comparison uses them. No group removal is justified: TIMING_FORMAT costs 0.21 points when removed, within noise (below the full model's interval in 0 of 5 folds), but dropping it would reopen the point-in-time audit with no measurable gain; the other three groups cost between 0.73 and 1.56 points when removed.
- WHAT holdout label rule (14-10-2026, decided before any model touches the holdout; derived from tables only, counts and descriptive statistics of artist-years with history_count >= 3, no model fitted or scored on the holdout): training set = development rows with year < 2018 and next_active_year < 2018 (687,628 rows), the fold rule of make_what_folds at test_start = 2018, so no train label looks into 2018 or later. Cost: 75,743 of 763,371 labelled development rows (9.9%), in line with the folds (fold 5: 11.1%). The dropped rows are sparser artists (positive rate 0.3323 vs 0.3067 kept, mean history_count 13.7 vs 19.7), the same selection the folds already accept. A fixed 2-year gap (year <= 2015) was rejected: it keeps 697,878 rows, of which 29,158 have a label from 2018 or later. Test set = labelled rows with 2018 <= year <= 2023 and next_active_year <= 2023 (143,228 of 190,846 labelled holdout rows). Reason: labels with next_active_year 2024 to 2026 come from years with incomplete loading (mean works in the label year: 2.18 for 2023, 2.12 for 2024, 1.34 for 2025, 1.16 for 2026, which is also a partial year, dump dated 01-09-2026). Dropped: 47,618 labelled rows (25.0%; 31,181 with next year 2024, 12,391 with 2025, 4,046 with 2026). Kept vs dropped: positive rate 0.3128 vs 0.3137, mean history_count 17.28 vs 17.33, mean n_works_year 2.46 vs 2.02 (median 1 in both). Consequence: test year 2023 has zero rows (its 25,763 labelled rows all have a next year in 2024 or later), so the effective labelled test window is 2018-2022 (32,557 / 32,213 / 31,571 / 26,830 / 20,057 rows); the 2023 feature rows exist but have no resolved label. Limitation: the kept set is skewed to short gaps (gap 1 is 74.3% of kept rows vs 65.5% of all labelled rows) and prevalence rises with gap in the kept rows (0.3075 / 0.3212 / 0.3383 / 0.3443 for gap 1 / 2 / 3 / 4+, no clear trend in development), so the holdout base rate is slightly low: 0.09 points below the dropped rows, and 0.27 points below the kept rate reweighted to the all-labelled gap mix (0.3128 vs 0.3155). Rejected: next_active_year <= 2024 (would recover about 31k rows): the completeness evidence is weak, because the works count is measured only on artists that already have a work that year, and the cutoff must not be tuned on the data. The rule is fixed here before any model touches the holdout and is not changed after seeing results.
- WHAT holdout rule: expected effect on the metric (development check) (14-10-2026, scratch re-run of run_what_gate on the development folds, deterministic, reproduces the gate exactly: model 0.4607 / 0.4751 / 0.4946 / 0.4947 / 0.5002, baseline (c) 0.3474 / 0.3479 / 0.3625 / 0.3698 / 0.3597, pooled ROC-AUC 0.6732 vs 0.5712; development years only, sealed holdout untouched): the analogous rule (keep test rows with next_active_year <= test_end of the fold) applied to the gate scores drops 39.4% to 44.0% of test rows per fold (43.8% / 44.0% / 43.9% / 43.4% / 39.4%), against 25.0% of labelled rows under the holdout rule. Gap model minus (c) in PR-AUC points goes from 11.33 / 12.72 / 13.21 / 12.50 / 14.05 to 11.26 / 12.59 / 12.83 / 12.01 / 13.68 (mean 12.76 to 12.48), a shrink of 0.07 to 0.49 points. Each PR-AUC moves between about -0.7 and +0.7 points with no systematic sign (model: +0.48 / +0.21 / -0.54 / +0.22 / -0.66 points; (c): +0.55 / +0.35 / -0.16 / +0.69 / -0.29 points). Pooled ROC-AUC changes by 0.0005 for the model (0.6732 to 0.6727) and 0.0008 for (c) (0.5712 to 0.5720). The ordering model over (c) never changes (smallest kept gap 11.26 points). Purpose: reference to read the holdout result, fixed before the holdout is touched.
- WHAT Logistic Regression, untuned, first model of the WHAT comparison (14-10-2026, src/models.py make_what_logistic_regression, evaluate_folds on the make_what_folds folds, 1,000 bootstrap draws, 95% artist-clustered interval, seed 1; development years only, sealed holdout untouched): same Pipeline and settings as the WHEN Logistic Regression, unchanged: median SimpleImputer, StandardScaler, LogisticRegression(C=1.0, solver='lbfgs', max_iter=1000), default L2, no tuning, no class weights. Inputs: the 25 WHAT_FEATURES through _what_matrix, the 11 genre columns as 0.0 / 1.0. Imputation: NaN is filled with the median of the fold's train rows (fitted on train only, same rule as WHEN), no missing-value indicator columns; the only column with a large NaN share is years_since_new_style (17.07% of fold 5 train rows, an artist with no new style yet), then new_style_rate_so_far (1.70%), n_new_styles_year and top_style_share (0.36% each). Result, PR-AUC per fold 1 to 5: 0.4338 [0.4262, 0.4412] / 0.4346 [0.4276, 0.4419] / 0.4518 [0.4458, 0.4576] / 0.4508 [0.4461, 0.4562] / 0.4527 [0.4481, 0.4576]; ROC-AUC 0.6533 / 0.6580 / 0.6606 / 0.6442 / 0.6444. Against baseline (c) (0.3474 / 0.3479 / 0.3625 / 0.3698 / 0.3597; ROC-AUC 0.563 to 0.575): above in 5 of 5 folds, +8.63 / +8.67 / +8.93 / +8.10 / +9.31 points (mean 8.73), intervals never overlap. Against XGBoost stage 0 (0.4607 / 0.4751 / 0.4946 / 0.4947 / 0.5002; ROC-AUC 0.6690 / 0.6781 / 0.6825 / 0.6677 / 0.6708): below in 5 of 5 folds, -2.70 / -4.05 / -4.27 / -4.40 / -4.74 points, intervals never overlap; ROC-AUC lower by 0.016 / 0.020 / 0.022 / 0.024 / 0.026. It recovers about two thirds of the XGBoost gain over (c) (8.73 of 12.76 points, mean over the folds). Observations: the gap to XGBoost widens with the fold (2.70 to 4.74 points); the Logistic Regression ROC-AUC is flat to falling across folds (0.6533 to 0.6444, highest 0.6606 in fold 3) while the XGBoost PR-AUC rises (0.4607 to 0.5002). Hypothesis, not tested: the part of the gain the linear model does not reach comes from interactions and non-linear effects among the inputs. Limits: years_since_new_style is 17.07% NaN and was not checked as an exact identifier of the imputed rows (unlike history_count and years_active_so_far in WHEN), median imputation and no indicator columns; the heavy-tailed counts (history_count up to 1,089, n_works_year up to 250 in fold 5 train) are not log-transformed; evaluate_folds gives no interval for the ROC-AUC, so the ROC-AUC values are point estimates. Reference for the tree models of the WHAT comparison.
- WHAT Naive Bayes, untuned, second model of the WHAT comparison, kept as a reference point and not as a competitor (14-10-2026, src/models.py make_what_naive_bayes, evaluate_folds on the make_what_folds folds, 1,000 bootstrap draws, 95% artist-clustered interval, seed 1; development years only, sealed holdout untouched): same Pipeline and settings as the WHEN Naive Bayes, unchanged: median SimpleImputer and GaussianNB with the default var_smoothing=1e-9, no scaler, no tuning. Inputs: the 25 WHAT_FEATURES through _what_matrix, the 11 genre columns as 0.0 / 1.0. Imputation: same as the Logistic Regression entry (median of the fold's train rows, fitted on train only, no missing-value indicator columns; years_since_new_style is 17.07% NaN in fold 5 train rows). Result, PR-AUC per fold 1 to 5: 0.4140 [0.4066, 0.4214] / 0.4082 [0.4020, 0.4149] / 0.4267 [0.4211, 0.4325] / 0.4339 [0.4291, 0.4392] / 0.4362 [0.4316, 0.4410]; ROC-AUC 0.6407 / 0.6396 / 0.6410 / 0.6261 / 0.6287. Against baseline (c) (0.3474 / 0.3479 / 0.3625 / 0.3698 / 0.3597; ROC-AUC 0.563 to 0.575): above in 5 of 5 folds, +6.66 / +6.03 / +6.42 / +6.41 / +7.65 points (mean 6.63), intervals never overlap, ROC-AUC 0.626 to 0.641. Against Logistic Regression (0.4338 / 0.4346 / 0.4518 / 0.4508 / 0.4527): below in 5 of 5 folds, -1.97 / -2.64 / -2.52 / -1.69 / -1.65 points (mean -2.09), intervals never overlap; ROC-AUC lower by 0.013 / 0.018 / 0.020 / 0.018 / 0.016. Against XGBoost stage 0 (0.4607 / 0.4751 / 0.4946 / 0.4947 / 0.5002): below in 5 of 5 folds, -4.67 / -6.69 / -6.79 / -6.08 / -6.40 points (mean -6.13), intervals never overlap. It recovers about half of the XGBoost gain over (c) (6.63 of 12.76 points, 52%, against 8.73 points for Logistic Regression). Difference from WHEN: in WHEN the Naive Bayes sat at the level of the historical baseline, here it is above baseline (c); (c) is a different baseline from the WHEN one. Observations: the PR-AUC rises slightly across folds (0.4140 to 0.4362) while the ROC-AUC is flat to falling (0.6407 to 0.6287). Limits: the Gaussian assumption does not hold for the 11 genre 0/1 columns or for the skewed and bounded shares and counts; the scores are overconfident (fold 1 test scores span 0.0 to 1.0 with a standard deviation of 0.388, against 0.114 for Logistic Regression), which does not affect PR-AUC or ROC-AUC and was not corrected; years_since_new_style is 17.07% NaN and was not checked as an exact identifier of the imputed rows (median imputation, no indicator columns); no mixed Gaussian/Bernoulli implementation, as in WHEN (limitation not fixed on purpose); the ROC-AUC values are point estimates.
- WHAT Random Forest, untuned, third model of the WHAT comparison (14-10-2026, src/models.py make_what_random_forest, evaluate_folds on the make_what_folds folds, 1,000 bootstrap draws, 95% artist-clustered interval, seed 1; development years only, sealed holdout untouched): same Pipeline and settings as the WHEN Random Forest, unchanged: median SimpleImputer and RandomForestClassifier(n_estimators=200, max_depth=12, min_samples_leaf=50, max_features='sqrt', max_samples=0.25, n_jobs=-1, random_state=1); common starting values, no search. Inputs: the 25 WHAT_FEATURES through _what_matrix, the 11 genre columns as 0.0 / 1.0; max_features='sqrt' gives 5 of 25 features per split (about 4 of 20 in WHEN). Imputation: same as the Logistic Regression entry (median of the fold's train rows, fitted on train only, no missing-value indicator columns; years_since_new_style is 17.07% NaN in fold 5 train rows). Result, PR-AUC per fold 1 to 5: 0.4628 [0.4552, 0.4702] / 0.4722 [0.4652, 0.4792] / 0.4914 [0.4852, 0.4972] / 0.4900 [0.4847, 0.4954] / 0.4982 [0.4934, 0.5035]; ROC-AUC 0.6705 / 0.6774 / 0.6812 / 0.6655 / 0.6705. Against baseline (c) (0.3474 / 0.3479 / 0.3625 / 0.3698 / 0.3597): above in 5 of 5 folds, +11.54 / +12.42 / +12.89 / +12.02 / +13.85 points (mean 12.55), intervals never overlap. Against Logistic Regression (0.4338 / 0.4346 / 0.4518 / 0.4508 / 0.4527): above in 5 of 5 folds, +2.91 / +3.75 / +3.95 / +3.92 / +4.55 points (mean 3.82), intervals never overlap; ROC-AUC higher by 0.017 / 0.019 / 0.021 / 0.021 / 0.026. Against XGBoost stage 0 (0.4607 / 0.4751 / 0.4946 / 0.4947 / 0.5002): +0.21 / -0.30 / -0.32 / -0.47 / -0.20 points (mean -0.21), intervals overlap in 5 of 5 folds (interval widths 0.010 to 0.015), so a tie by the WHEN reading rule (a difference smaller than the interval width); the Random Forest is above XGBoost in fold 1 only and below in folds 2 to 5, always by less than half a point; ROC-AUC within 0.0022 of XGBoost in every fold (+0.0015 / -0.0007 / -0.0012 / -0.0022 / -0.0003). It recovers 12.55 of the 12.76 points of the XGBoost gain over (c) (98%, against 8.73 for Logistic Regression and 6.63 for Naive Bayes). Same pattern as WHEN, where the untuned Random Forest, HistGradientBoosting and XGBoost stage 0 were tied with differences of at most 0.002 (see the WHEN entries of those two models). Observation: the gap to Logistic Regression grows with the fold (2.91 to 4.55 points). Limits: years_since_new_style is 17.07% NaN and was not checked as an exact identifier of the imputed rows (median imputation, no indicator columns); the ROC-AUC values are point estimates; the settings are the untuned WHEN starting points and were not changed after the fold 5 glance (0.4982 then and now).
- WHAT HistGradientBoostingClassifier, untuned, fourth model of the WHAT comparison (14-10-2026, src/models.py make_what_hist_gradient_boosting, evaluate_folds on the make_what_folds folds, 1,000 bootstrap draws, 95% artist-clustered interval, seed 1; development years only, sealed holdout untouched): same settings as the WHEN HistGradientBoostingClassifier, unchanged: learning_rate=0.1, max_iter=200, max_leaf_nodes=31, min_samples_leaf=50 (same as the Random Forest), early_stopping=False (all train rows used, no internal validation split), random_state=1; common starting values, no search. Inputs: the 25 WHAT_FEATURES through _what_matrix, the 11 genre columns as 0.0 / 1.0. Imputation: none and no scaler, NaN is handled natively (years_since_new_style 17.07% NaN in fold 5 train rows, new_style_rate_so_far 1.70%, n_new_styles_year and top_style_share 0.36% each), no missing-value indicator columns. Result, PR-AUC per fold 1 to 5: 0.4623 [0.4540, 0.4703] / 0.4768 [0.4695, 0.4838] / 0.4962 [0.4899, 0.5019] / 0.4957 [0.4907, 0.5010] / 0.5013 [0.4964, 0.5065]; ROC-AUC 0.6692 / 0.6790 / 0.6830 / 0.6683 / 0.6715. Against baseline (c) (0.3474 / 0.3479 / 0.3625 / 0.3698 / 0.3597): above in 5 of 5 folds, +11.49 / +12.89 / +13.37 / +12.59 / +14.17 points (mean 12.90), intervals never overlap. Against Logistic Regression (0.4338 / 0.4346 / 0.4518 / 0.4508 / 0.4527): above in 5 of 5 folds, +2.86 / +4.22 / +4.44 / +4.49 / +4.86 points (mean 4.17), intervals never overlap; ROC-AUC higher by 0.016 / 0.021 / 0.022 / 0.024 / 0.027. Against the Random Forest (0.4628 / 0.4722 / 0.4914 / 0.4900 / 0.4982): -0.05 / +0.47 / +0.48 / +0.57 / +0.31 points (mean +0.36), intervals overlap in 5 of 5 folds, so a tie by the WHEN reading rule (a difference smaller than the interval width); ROC-AUC within 0.0028 (-0.0013 / +0.0015 / +0.0018 / +0.0028 / +0.0010). Against XGBoost stage 0 (0.4607 / 0.4751 / 0.4946 / 0.4947 / 0.5002): +0.16 / +0.17 / +0.16 / +0.10 / +0.12 points (mean +0.14), always above, intervals overlap in 5 of 5 folds, so a tie; ROC-AUC within 0.0008 (+0.0002 / +0.0008 / +0.0006 / +0.0006 / +0.0007). Mean gain over (c) 12.90 points against 12.76 for XGBoost stage 0 (101%), 12.55 for the Random Forest (98%), 8.73 for Logistic Regression and 6.63 for Naive Bayes. Observation: the three tree models are tied, with pairwise differences of at most 0.57 points against interval widths of 0.010 to 0.016. Same pattern as WHEN, where the untuned Random Forest, HistGradientBoosting and XGBoost stage 0 were tied (differences of at most 0.002 there, that is 0.2 points, and up to 0.57 points here). Limits: years_since_new_style is 17.07% NaN, handled natively, and was not checked as an exact identifier of the missing rows; the ROC-AUC values are point estimates; the settings are the untuned WHEN starting values and were not changed after the fold 5 glance (0.5013 then and now).
- WHAT XGBoost staged tuning protocol, fixed before phase 1 (14-10-2026, src/tuning.py prepare_what_fold_data, run_trial, run_random_search; folds from make_what_folds, development years only, sealed holdout 2018-2023 untouched): XGBoost goes to staged tuning following the WHEN protocol, a design choice; the tie of the Random Forest, HistGradientBoosting and XGBoost stage 0 is not a selection criterion. Three phases as in the WHEN entries "XGBoost stage 0, untuned reference" and "XGBoost tuning results": (1) random search, 35 combinations from sample_params unchanged (max_depth 3-10, min_child_weight 5-500, subsample 0.5-1.0, colsample_bytree 0.3-1.0, reg_lambda and reg_alpha 0.001-9, learning_rate 0.03-0.3, n_estimators 300) plus the stage0 reference row; seed 1, so the 35 draws are the same hyperparameter combinations as in WHEN (trial i uses the stream [1, i]), and N_TRIALS stays 35 for comparability; (2) focused grid and (3) manual trials, with values decided after the previous phase from its rank correlations, best region and search-space edge hits, and logged here before that phase runs; the 1000-tree rerun of the WHEN grid is copied only if the WHAT grid includes learning_rate 0.03. Inputs: the 25 WHAT_FEATURES, NaN handled natively, no imputation. Every finished trial is appended to results/trials/xgb_what_new_style_year.jsonl (parameters, per-fold PR-AUC and ROC-AUC, mean, seconds); the record has no seed field, the seed is 1 for the XGBoost fits (FIXED_PARAMS) and 1 for the random draws. Pipeline check before any search: run_trial with the stage 0 parameters on the five folds reproduced the logged WHAT stage 0 PR-AUC to 4 decimals in every fold (0.4607 / 0.4751 / 0.4946 / 0.4947 / 0.5002, mean 0.4851); the stage0 row of the trial log will carry the per-fold values as the reproducible source. Selection inside each phase: the highest mean PR-AUC over the five folds (unweighted, as in WHEN), no manual judgment; the per-fold table is descriptive only. If the best trials sit at a search-space edge, it is reported and the range is not extended.
- WHAT XGBoost adoption rule, fixed before phase 1 (14-10-2026), a deliberate difference from the WHEN practice (the WHEN entries are unchanged: there the best grid combination was locked although it tied with stage 0): the candidate is the best non-stage0 trial of the whole tuning by the selection rule (highest mean PR-AUC over the five folds), compared against stage 0; if a stage0 row has the highest mean, stage 0 stays and there is no comparison. The candidate is evaluated once through evaluate_folds (1,000 draws, 95%, seed 1) and replaces stage 0 only if its interval lower bound is above the stage 0 interval upper bound in at least 4 of the 5 folds; otherwise stage 0 stays as the WHAT XGBoost final. Reason: selection and comparison use the same five folds, so the tuned score carries a selection advantage, and a tie is resolved toward the configuration with no free choices. This is a conservative decision rule based on non-overlapping bootstrap intervals, not a significance test. The bootstrap unit is the test artist (artists drawn with replacement within each fold, row weights from the draw counts, the model not refitted per draw), so the intervals reflect test-sample variation only, not training-set or seed variation; folds share artists and are not independent; the threshold of 4 of 5 is a convention, as in the gate rule (a). Both models use seed 1, so their artist draws are identical within a fold, and non-overlap of the two intervals is expected to be stricter than an interval on the paired difference, which would be narrower; the rule therefore leans toward keeping stage 0. If the candidate is adopted, scripts/run_xgb_what_overfit_check.py is created and run; if not, it is not.
- WHAT XGBoost scale_pos_weight rate, for the phase 3 trial (14-10-2026): p = 0.3067 as measured (210,928 positives of 687,628 labelled development rows with year < 2018 and next_active_year < 2018, no row from 2018 onward), scale_pos_weight = (1 - p) / p = 2.260. The phase 3 script computes p from the data (labelled rows with year < 2018 and next_active_year < 2018) and prints and saves it in its own output; the value above is to be verified against that output when phase 3 runs. p is global: for each development fold it includes the labels of that fold's own test years (and later years), not only that fold's train rows. This is a documented limitation with negligible expected effect: the fold train rates are 0.2916 / 0.2944 / 0.2958 / 0.2983 / 0.3039 against 0.3067, and the value is one fixed class-weight constant, not a fitted quantity. It is not leak-free by construction. If a scale_pos_weight configuration were adopted, its outputs would not be calibrated probabilities. WHEN used the mean of the full development target, test years included.
- WHAT XGBoost phase 1 (random search) results and phase 2 (grid) design, fixed before phase 2 (14-10-2026, results/trials/xgb_what_new_style_year.jsonl, scripts/run_xgb_what_random_search.py, src/tuning.py run_random_search; development folds only, sealed holdout 2018-2023 untouched, no bootstrap in this phase): 36 rows in the log (the stage0 row plus trials 1 to 35), 345.6 s in total, 9.6 s mean per 5-fold trial (5.1 to 16.5 s, rank correlation with max_depth +0.913). The stage0 row carries phase "random" in the log; any ranking of trials excludes it by trial id. Stage 0 reproduction: the stage0 row gave PR-AUC 0.4607 / 0.4751 / 0.4946 / 0.4947 / 0.5002 (mean 0.48506), equal to the logged WHAT stage 0 at 4 decimals in every fold. Stage 0 parameters for reference (STAGE0 in src/tuning.py): max_depth 6, min_child_weight 50, subsample 0.8, colsample_bytree 0.8, reg_lambda 1.0, reg_alpha 0.0, learning_rate 0.1, n_estimators 300. Results over the 35 random trials, mean PR-AUC over the five folds: best 0.4887 (trial 30), median 0.4860, worst 0.4727; 22 of 35 are above stage 0 (0.4851) and 4 are within 0.0005 of it. Top 10 by mean (trial: mean): 30: 0.4887, 13: 0.4884, 1: 0.4883, 4: 0.4882, 24: 0.4882, 34: 0.4881, 19: 0.4881, 20: 0.4878, 35: 0.4877, 22: 0.4875, a spread of 0.0012; they use max_depth 3 to 7 (3, 3, 4, 4, 5, 5, 5, 5, 7, 7), reg_lambda 0.0013 to 2.02 and learning_rate 0.030 to 0.121. Best trial 30 (max_depth 5, min_child_weight 7.50, subsample 0.571, colsample_bytree 0.510, reg_lambda 0.0143, reg_alpha 1.37, learning_rate 0.0412) against stage 0 per fold 1 to 5: +0.67 / +0.43 / +0.26 / +0.23 / +0.22 points (mean +0.363). This gain is descriptive: it is a best-of-35 on the same five folds used for the selection, it is not a result of the adoption rule, and the adoption rule is unchanged. For scale, the 95% intervals of the WHAT stage 0 in the entry "WHAT kill gate: PASSED" are 1.56 / 1.40 / 1.25 / 1.00 / 1.00 points wide in folds 1 to 5 (from the recorded bounds). Spearman rank correlation of each parameter with the mean over the 35 trials: max_depth -0.503, min_child_weight +0.059, subsample +0.111, colsample_bytree -0.176, reg_lambda -0.027, reg_alpha +0.211, learning_rate -0.676 (shallower and slower are better; the other parameters are weak). Search-space edges among the top 5 trials (within 10% of the range, log scale for min_child_weight, reg_lambda, reg_alpha and learning_rate), reported and not acted on: max_depth 3 at the low edge (trial 24); learning_rate at the low edge (trial 13, 0.0300) and near it (trial 1, 0.0358); min_child_weight at the low edge (trial 30) and the high edge (trial 13); colsample_bytree at the low edge (trial 13); subsample at the high edge (trial 4); reg_lambda at the low edge (trial 24); reg_alpha at the high edge (trial 4) and the low edge (trial 1). The edge hits are spread over all seven parameters and no range is extended. Phase 2 design (grid, same shape as the WHEN grid: 3 parameters x 3 values = 27 combinations, n_estimators 500): max_depth 3 / 5 / 7, reg_lambda 0.01 / 0.3 / 2, learning_rate 0.03 / 0.05 / 0.08. The other four parameters (GRID_FIXED) are the median of the ten best non-stage0 phase 1 trials by mean PR-AUC (trials 30, 13, 1, 4, 24, 34, 19, 20, 35, 22; the median of ten values is the arithmetic mean of the two middle ones): min_child_weight 81.4, subsample 0.811, colsample_bytree 0.538, reg_alpha 0.343. For reg_alpha the two middle values are 0.0935 and 0.593, so the median depends on the averaging convention (the geometric mean of the two is 0.236). Among the ten best trials the values of min_child_weight are bimodal (four between 7.5 and 14.0, six between 79.2 and 340), so the median 81.4 falls at the lower end of the upper group, with no phase 1 trial below it between 14.0 and 79.2; the value is kept because the rule was fixed before computing it and the rank correlation is weak (+0.059). This fixed combination is a summary of the region and was not itself evaluated as a trial in phase 1. Rationale: the top 10 span max_depth 3 to 7 and reg_lambda 0.001 to 2, which is below the WHEN grid values (max_depth 7 / 9 / 11, reg_lambda 2 / 5 / 15), so only max_depth 7 and reg_lambda 2 overlap with it; the learning_rate values are kept from WHEN (0.03 / 0.05 / 0.08), and the low learning_rate edge is covered by the phase 3 trial with learning_rate 0.02 and 750 trees, so no range is extended. Selection inside the phase: the highest mean PR-AUC over the five folds, no manual judgment, stage0 excluded by trial id; the 1000-tree rerun (g_best_1000) runs only if the best grid combination has learning_rate 0.03, as in WHEN. Trial ids g1 to g27 in the same log, the naming of run_grid_search.
- WHAT XGBoost candidate pool and in-phase winner, clarification fixed before phase 2 (14-10-2026; it makes explicit the wording "best non-stage0 trial of the whole tuning" of the entry "WHAT XGBoost adoption rule", and the entries "WHAT XGBoost staged tuning protocol", "WHAT XGBoost adoption rule", "WHAT XGBoost scale_pos_weight rate" and "WHAT XGBoost phase 1 (random search) results and phase 2 (grid) design" stay unchanged): the candidate for the adoption comparison against stage 0 is chosen from a pool made of every logged non-stage0 trial of phases 1 to 3, that is the random trials 1 to 35, the grid combinations g1 to g27, the 1000-tree rerun g_best_1000 if it runs, and the manual trials of phase 3 (the scale_pos_weight trial included). The stage0 row is excluded by trial id. The candidate is the trial with the highest mean PR-AUC over the five folds in that pool, with no manual judgment. Inside phase 2, the winner among g1 to g27 (also by highest mean PR-AUC, g_best_1000 and the other phases excluded from that ranking, as in WHEN) only decides whether the 1000-tree rerun runs, which happens if its learning_rate is 0.03; it does not decide the final candidate, and g_best_1000 is eligible for the pool. The base configuration of the phase 3 trials is the best non-stage0 trial of phases 1 and 2 by the selection rule (highest mean PR-AUC over the five folds, g_best_1000 included if it ran); the five trial values are set in the phase 3 design entry, logged before phase 3 runs. Consequence: the pool holds 68 trials if the 1000-tree rerun runs, 67 otherwise (35 random, 27 grid, 5 manual, plus g_best_1000 if it runs), selected on the same five folds as the comparison, which is the selection advantage the adoption rule is built to absorb (4 of 5 folds with non-overlapping 95% intervals); if the candidate is a scale_pos_weight trial, its outputs are not calibrated probabilities (see the entry on the scale_pos_weight rate).
- WHAT XGBoost phase 2 (grid) results and phase 3 (manual) design, fixed before phase 3 (14-10-2026, results/trials/xgb_what_new_style_year.jsonl, scripts/run_xgb_what_grid_search.py, src/tuning.py run_what_grid_search; development folds only, sealed holdout 2018-2023 untouched, no bootstrap in this phase): 64 rows in the log (36 from phase 1, the 27 combinations g1 to g27 and g_best_1000), 349.3 s for the 27 combinations (8.2 to 19.1 s each, rank correlation with max_depth +0.945) and 28.8 s for g_best_1000. Grid fixed values as in the phase 2 design entry (min_child_weight 81.4, subsample 0.811, colsample_bytree 0.538, reg_alpha 0.343, n_estimators 500). Mean PR-AUC over the five folds across the 27 combinations: best 0.48888 (g13), median 0.48810, worst 0.48390; 24 of 27 are above stage 0 (0.48506) and 3 are above the phase 1 best (trial 30, 0.48870). Top 10 (trial: max_depth / reg_lambda / learning_rate: mean): g13: 5 / 0.3 / 0.03: 0.48888, g10: 5 / 0.01 / 0.03: 0.48887, g16: 5 / 2 / 0.03: 0.48887, g8: 3 / 2 / 0.05: 0.48851, g2: 3 / 0.01 / 0.05: 0.48836, g5: 3 / 0.3 / 0.05: 0.48835, g9: 3 / 2 / 0.08: 0.48832, g17: 5 / 2 / 0.05: 0.48826, g3: 3 / 0.01 / 0.08: 0.48824, g6: 3 / 0.3 / 0.08: 0.48823. The top three differ by less than 2e-5 (g13 0.488883, g10 0.488872, g16 0.488867), so the choice of g13 is mechanical by the selection rule and not a meaningful difference. Spearman rank correlation with the mean over the 27 combinations: max_depth -0.623, reg_lambda +0.047, learning_rate -0.379; mean by max_depth 3 / 5 / 7: 0.48824 / 0.48804 / 0.48618, by reg_lambda 0.01 / 0.3 / 2: 0.48746 / 0.48749 / 0.48751, by learning_rate 0.03 / 0.05 / 0.08: 0.48833 / 0.48766 / 0.48647 (depth 7 is worse, reg_lambda has no effect, slower is better). Search-space edges, reported and not acted on: the best combination has max_depth 5 (not the edge value 3) and learning_rate 0.03, the lowest grid value and the lower bound of the phase 1 range; the three best combinations all have learning_rate 0.03. The best has learning_rate 0.03, so the 1000-tree rerun ran: g_best_1000 (g13 with 1000 trees) gave 0.48805, 0.084 points below g13, so more trees did not help. Best grid combination g13 against stage 0 per fold 1 to 5: +0.61 / +0.51 / +0.29 / +0.21 / +0.28 points (mean +0.382; stage 0 0.48506, g13 0.48888). This gain is descriptive: it is a best-of-many on the same five folds used for the selection and it is not a result of the adoption rule, which is unchanged. The best non-stage0 trial of phases 1 and 2 is g13 (mean 0.48888, n_estimators 500); the pool ranking is g13, g10, g16, then trial 30 (0.48870, 300 trees), then g8. Limitations: phase 1 used n_estimators 300 and the grid 500, so the grid is not a direct continuation of the phase 1 learning_rate finding; the fixed parameters (81.4, 0.811, 0.538, 0.343) were a summary of the phase 1 region and were not evaluated as a trial in phase 1; the best grid combination is 0.00018 above the phase 1 best, so the surface is a plateau within the noise of the selection; every selection is made on the same five folds with no nested validation; n_estimators and learning_rate interact (a lower rate needs more trees), so a change of one is not separable from a change of the other in the trials that move both. Point-in-time confirmation (14-10-2026, scratch script outside the repository, not a reproducible result in the repository): features recomputed from Works with year <= t for 200 randomly drawn artists (seed 1, 1,225 Works, 150 undated) and cut years 1995, 2005 and 2015 matched the full-table values in all 25 inputs with 0 mismatches (9 to 15 rows per cut at year == t, 196 to 433 at year <= t), while the forward-looking controls new_style_year and n_works_next_year did differ; this confirms the earlier audit (what_point_in_time_check, 30,000 artists) on a small sample and does not replace it. Phase 3 design (five trials, one fit each): the base configuration is read by the script from the log by the selection rule (the best non-stage0 trial of phases 1 and 2 by highest mean PR-AUC over the five folds, g_best_1000 included if it ran), not typed in; it is g13 (max_depth 5, reg_lambda 0.3, learning_rate 0.03, n_estimators 500, min_child_weight 81.4, subsample 0.811, colsample_bytree 0.538, reg_alpha 0.343). m_depth4 (max_depth 4) and m_depth6 (max_depth 6), both depths outside the grid (the WHEN depth trials repeated grid rows and are not copied); m_lr_0.02 (learning_rate 0.02, n_estimators 750) and m_lr_0.012 (learning_rate 0.012, n_estimators 1250), two slow trials with learning_rate x n_estimators kept at 15 (0.03 x 500, 0.02 x 750, 0.012 x 1250); m_lr_0.012 replaces the WHEN trial with learning_rate 0.12, because the grid showed performance decreasing monotonically with learning_rate (0.03 > 0.05 > 0.08), so the open question is on the slow side; its rate is below the lower bound 0.03 of the phase 1 range, a deliberate choice of this phase, in which learning_rate and n_estimators are settled; m_spw (scale_pos_weight = (1 - p) / p with p computed from the labelled rows with year < 2018 and next_active_year < 2018, expected 2.260, see the entry on the scale_pos_weight rate). Ids m_depth4, m_depth6, m_lr_0.02, m_lr_0.012, m_spw.
- WHAT XGBoost phase 3 (manual) results and candidate, before the adoption evaluation (14-10-2026, results/trials/xgb_what_new_style_year.jsonl, scripts/run_xgb_what_manual.py; development folds only, sealed holdout 2018-2023 untouched, no bootstrap in this phase): 69 rows in the log (36 random including the stage0 row, 28 grid including g_best_1000, 5 manual), a single stage0 row and no repeated trial ids; the five manual trials took 83.2 s (10.1 / 13.2 / 18.0 / 29.8 / 12.1 s) and the whole log 806.9 s. The base was read by the script from the log by the selection rule and checked against the design: g13 (max_depth 5, reg_lambda 0.3, learning_rate 0.03, n_estimators 500, min_child_weight 81.4, subsample 0.811, colsample_bytree 0.538, reg_alpha 0.343). The positive rate p was computed from the data: 0.306747 (210,928 positives of 687,628 labelled rows with year < 2018 and next_active_year < 2018), equal to the 0.3067 of the entry on the scale_pos_weight rate, so scale_pos_weight = (1 - p) / p = 2.260013, used rounded to 2.26. Each trial changes one thing from the base, except the two slow trials, which change learning_rate and n_estimators together. Mean PR-AUC over the five folds (per fold 1 to 5): stage0 0.48506 (0.4607 / 0.4751 / 0.4946 / 0.4947 / 0.5002); g13 0.48888 (0.4669 / 0.4803 / 0.4975 / 0.4968 / 0.5030); m_depth4 (max_depth 4) 0.48879 (0.4675 / 0.4797 / 0.4973 / 0.4967 / 0.5027), -0.010 points against g13; m_depth6 (max_depth 6) 0.48853 (0.4660 / 0.4798 / 0.4974 / 0.4968 / 0.5027), -0.035; m_lr_0.02 (learning_rate 0.02, n_estimators 750) 0.48899 (0.4672 / 0.4804 / 0.4974 / 0.4970 / 0.5030), +0.011; m_lr_0.012 (learning_rate 0.012, n_estimators 1250) 0.48914 (0.4678 / 0.4800 / 0.4977 / 0.4971 / 0.5031), +0.026; m_spw (scale_pos_weight 2.26) 0.48895 (0.4672 / 0.4803 / 0.4975 / 0.4968 / 0.5029), +0.007. No fold of any manual trial differs from g13 by more than 0.0009. The plateau holds: the two depths are marginally below depth 5, scale_pos_weight changes nothing visible, and the slow trials are marginally above. Candidate by the pre-registered rule (highest mean PR-AUC over the five folds in the pool of all non-stage0 trials of phases 1 to 3, 68 trials: 35 random, 28 grid including g_best_1000, 5 manual): m_lr_0.012, max_depth 5, reg_lambda 0.3, learning_rate 0.012, n_estimators 1250, min_child_weight 81.4, subsample 0.811, colsample_bytree 0.538, reg_alpha 0.343, mean 0.489143, with 1.51e-4 over the second (m_lr_0.02, 0.488992); the top five are m_lr_0.012, m_lr_0.02, m_spw (0.48895), g13 (0.488883) and g10 (0.488872). m_spw ranks third, so the candidate is not a scale_pos_weight trial and its outputs are not affected by the calibration caveat of that entry. The candidate is +0.408 points over stage0 in mean PR-AUC (per fold 1 to 5: +0.71 / +0.49 / +0.31 / +0.24 / +0.30). This gain is descriptive: it is a best-of-68 on the same five folds used for the selection and it is not an adoption result; the adoption rule is unchanged and is applied next, once, through evaluate_folds. Edge: the candidate has the slowest learning_rate tried (0.012), and the improvements over g13 along the slow side are small and monotonic (+0.011 points at 0.02 and +0.026 at 0.012), so the search did not find a minimum on that side; the range is not extended. Limitations: every selection is made on the same five folds with no nested validation; the gap of the candidate to the second is 1.51e-4, within the noise of the selection, so the surface is a plateau; learning_rate and n_estimators interact (their product is kept at 15 in the two slow trials, so the effect of each is not separated); phase 1 used n_estimators 300 and the grid 500; the fixed parameters (81.4, 0.811, 0.538, 0.343) were not evaluated as a trial in phase 1; the stage 0 intervals are 1.00 to 1.56 points wide (entry "WHAT kill gate: PASSED"), against a gain of 0.408 points in the mean.
- WHAT threshold-metric report, fixed before any such metric is computed (14-10-2026; the entries on the WHAT XGBoost tuning and the adoption rule are unchanged): framing and scope. The report is orientative and descriptive, to show in plain terms how well the final model predicts; PR-AUC stays the only selection and adoption metric, and nothing in this entry changes a selection or a decision. It is computed only in the single final holdout evaluation, once, as in WHEN (entry "FINAL holdout evaluation"), on the holdout rows of the entry "WHAT holdout label rule" (test set: labelled rows with 2018 <= year <= 2023 and next_active_year <= 2023, so effectively 2018-2022); no such metric is computed on any development fold. Models: the final WHAT XGBoost, which is the stage 0 or the candidate m_lr_0.012 according to the adoption rule (nothing else is tried on the holdout), and baseline (c), because its score is a probability estimate (the train positive rate of the row's cell of history_bucket and volume_bucket, or the global train rate for an unseen or small cell), unlike the WHEN baseline whose score was a rate of Works per year and whose report at 0.5 was meaningless. Baseline (c) has few distinct score values, so at 0.5 it may flag no row (precision then undefined, recall 0, reported as such, not hidden). Thresholds, fixed now and not tuned on any data: 0.5, as in WHEN, and 0.3067, the positive rate p of the labelled rows with year < 2018 and next_active_year < 2018 (the same p as the entry on the scale_pos_weight rate; for the holdout this is exactly the training set of the holdout label rule, 687,628 rows, so it uses no holdout row). The threshold of each model is applied to its score unchanged, and no threshold is added or changed after the numbers are seen. Metrics, per model and threshold: accuracy, and for each class (new style, no new style) precision, recall, F1 and support, all in a visible table with accuracy as its own column, not only in prose. References printed next to them: the majority-class accuracy ("always no new style", equal to 1 minus the holdout positive rate) and the holdout positive rate as the precision of a score without skill. Ranking figure, fixed now and without a threshold: the precision among the 10% of holdout rows with the highest predicted score, shown next to the holdout positive rate and their ratio (lift); the top 10% is defined as the rows with a score at or above the 90th percentile of the holdout scores, and the share of rows actually selected is printed (10% up to ties, which matter for baseline (c) and not for the model). Plain-language reading to be written with the numbers: precision is the share of flagged artist-years that did introduce a new style, recall is the share of artist-years that did introduce one that were flagged, and lift is how many times more often the top 10% do so than a random 10%; the figures show a moderate signal, not case-by-case reliability. Unit and pooling: rows are artist-years pooled over the holdout years (effectively 2018-2022), not reported by year, with no confidence intervals unless asked. Limitations: the holdout label rule favors short gaps, so the holdout positive rate may differ from 0.3067 (0.3128 among the kept holdout rows in the entry "WHAT holdout label rule"), and a threshold at the training rate flags a different share of rows than it did in training; the 0.5 threshold is a convention, not a tuned operating point; calibration is not checked; baseline (c) is coarse. Differences from the WHEN report: the thresholds are logged before the run, the majority-class reference is included, accuracy is in a table, a second threshold (the training positive rate) and the top-10% lift are added, and baseline (c) is reported because its score is a probability.
- WHAT XGBoost adoption evaluation: stage 0 kept (14-10-2026, scripts/run_xgb_what_compare.py, results/trials/xgb_what_compare.csv, results/trials/xgb_what_compare_20261014_192152.out, src/models.py make_what_xgboost with overrides; development folds only (train rows and validation rows of each fold; the code and the CSV call the validation rows test_rows), sealed holdout 2018-2023 untouched, no threshold metric): the adoption rule is applied exactly as logged in the entry "WHAT XGBoost adoption rule", once, with evaluate_folds (1,000 artist draws, 95%, seed 1). Candidate: m_lr_0.012, confirmed by the script as the best non-stage0 trial of the log by the selection rule (pool of 68 trials). Both models were refitted with the parameters of their log row read by trial id (stage0: max_depth 6, min_child_weight 50, subsample 0.8, colsample_bytree 0.8, reg_lambda 1.0, reg_alpha 0.0, learning_rate 0.1, n_estimators 300; candidate: max_depth 5, min_child_weight 81.4, subsample 0.811, colsample_bytree 0.538, reg_lambda 0.3, reg_alpha 0.343, learning_rate 0.012, n_estimators 1250), checked to carry exactly the eight searched keys and no scale_pos_weight, and the get_params of each fitted model were checked against the log (candidate: n_estimators 1250, learning_rate 0.012). Reproduction check: PR-AUC per fold equals the logged trial row at 4 decimals for both models (stage0 0.4607 / 0.4751 / 0.4946 / 0.4947 / 0.5002; candidate 0.4678 / 0.4800 / 0.4977 / 0.4971 / 0.5031), the largest unrounded difference being 5.55e-17. Result per validation fold 1 to 5, PR-AUC [95% interval], stage 0 then candidate: 0.4607 [0.4525, 0.4681] and 0.4678 [0.4596, 0.4755]; 0.4751 [0.4680, 0.4820] and 0.4800 [0.4729, 0.4867]; 0.4946 [0.4881, 0.5006] and 0.4977 [0.4915, 0.5035]; 0.4947 [0.4898, 0.4998] and 0.4971 [0.4922, 0.5023]; 0.5002 [0.4953, 0.5053] and 0.5031 [0.4983, 0.5083]; difference +0.71 / +0.49 / +0.31 / +0.24 / +0.30 points (mean +0.408). The candidate lower bound is above the stage 0 upper bound in 0 of 5 folds (4 required); it is below it by 0.70 to 0.91 points in every fold. Verdict by the logged rule: the candidate is not adopted and stage 0 stays as the WHAT XGBoost final. Reading: the point estimate of the candidate is above stage 0 in all five validation folds, but the intervals overlap heavily, so the 68-trial tuning produced no gain that this rule can separate from the noise of the validation sample; the rule leans toward keeping stage 0 by construction (non-overlap of two intervals is expected to be stricter than an interval on the paired difference, which was not computed and is not part of the rule), so the verdict means "not distinguishable by this rule" and not "no gain". ROC-AUC for reference only (stage 0 / candidate): 0.6690 / 0.6737, 0.6781 / 0.6816, 0.6825 / 0.6845, 0.6677 / 0.6694, 0.6708 / 0.6729. Same pattern as the WHEN tuning, where the gain was a plateau within the interval width. Consequences: the WHAT XGBoost final model is stage 0 (the STAGE0 parameters above, 300 trees, learning_rate 0.1, max_depth 6), a design choice that the tie with the Random Forest and HistGradientBoosting does not change; the entry "WHAT threshold-metric report" therefore refers to stage 0; the adoption rule, the candidate and the pool are not changed after seeing this result, and the entry "WHAT XGBoost adoption rule" stays unchanged. The candidate m_lr_0.012 and the log of stage 0 plus 68 trials remain as the tuning record. Limitations: the adoption comparison uses the same five validation folds as the selection, with no nested validation, so it is not an unbiased estimate of future performance (the single holdout evaluation is that estimate); the intervals reflect validation-sample variation only (artist bootstrap, no refit per draw) and the folds are not independent.
- WHAT stage 0 train-versus-validation diagnostic, fixed before it is computed (14-10-2026; the WHEN counterpart is scripts/run_when_xgb_overfit_check.py and the entry "XGBoost train versus test check", whose "test" is the walk-forward fold, called validation fold here): descriptive only. Scope: development folds only, that is the train rows and the validation rows of each of the five folds (make_what_folds, development years only; the code names the validation rows test_rows); no holdout row is read and no holdout script is run. Model: XGBoost stage 0 only, the final WHAT XGBoost after the adoption evaluation, with the parameters of the log row stage0 (STAGE0 in src/tuning.py), refitted on each fold with xgb.train and FIXED_PARAMS exactly as the trials, so that the validation PR-AUC reproduces the logged stage0 row (0.4607 / 0.4751 / 0.4946 / 0.4947 / 0.5002); the script stops if it does not at 4 decimals. Quantities per fold: PR-AUC and ROC-AUC on the fold's train rows (the rows the model was fitted on) and on its validation rows, the gap train minus validation in points for each metric, and the positive rate of the train and the validation rows next to them. No threshold for "overfitting" is set, no decision depends on the result, and PR-AUC stays the only selection and adoption metric; the adoption verdict is not reopened. Reading, fixed now: the stability of the gap across the five folds (the WHEN gap was large in early folds and shrank with the fold) and the level of the validation PR-AUC against baseline (c) (0.3474 / 0.3479 / 0.3625 / 0.3698 / 0.3597), not the gap alone. PR-AUC depends on the positive rate and the train and validation rates differ (train 0.2916 to 0.3039, validation 0.3018 to 0.3266 in the folds), so the gap is a diagnostic and not a pure measure of overfitting; ROC-AUC is printed because it does not depend on the rate. Because the validation folds were used for model selection (tuning, the adoption comparison and the five-model comparison), the train-versus-validation gap is descriptive and is not an unbiased estimate of future performance; the single holdout evaluation is that estimate. The WHEN figures (mean gap 0.0946 and 0.0627 for stage 0) are a different task and prevalence and are not used as a reference value. Output: results/trials/xgb_what_overfit.csv, a new file that the script refuses to overwrite. Differences from the WHEN script: stage 0 only (WHEN compared four candidates per target), ROC-AUC added, positive rates added, the gap also in points, a reproduction check against the log, a refusal to start if the output file exists, and the WHAT folds. The entry "WHAT XGBoost adoption rule" ties the creation of scripts/run_xgb_what_overfit_check.py to the adoption of the candidate; it stays unchanged, and this entry adds a descriptive check of the final model (stage 0) that the candidate branch did not cover, so the script is created although the candidate was not adopted.
- WHAT final holdout evaluation of stage 0, single evaluation, now spent (14-10-2026, scripts/run_xgb_what_holdout.py, results/holdout_what/ with 8 files (stage0_new_style_year.json, baseline_c_new_style_year.json, headline.csv, by_year.csv, threshold_summary.csv, threshold_by_class.csv, top10_lift.csv, row_scores.csv), results/xgb_what_holdout_20261014_194207.out, src/backtesting.py evaluate_holdout with return_scores; the entries "WHAT holdout label rule", "WHAT XGBoost adoption evaluation: stage 0 kept" and "WHAT threshold-metric report" fixed everything below before the run): stage 0 (parameters of the log row stage0, STAGE0) was fitted once on the training rows and scored once on the holdout rows, nothing was tuned or chosen after seeing the results, and the holdout is now spent: no re-run, no change and no further evaluation on it (the script refuses a second run, the folder results/holdout_what exists). Counts and checks printed by the script and asserted before the fit: 687,628 training rows (year < 2018 and next_active_year < 2018, p = 0.306747 = 210,928 positives, equal to 0.3067), 190,846 labelled holdout rows, 143,228 holdout rows (2018 <= year <= 2023 and next_active_year <= 2023) from 71,523 artists with positive rate 0.3128, 47,618 rows dropped by the label rule, no training year or label year from 2018 onward and no holdout year before 2018; the get_params of the fitted model equal the stage0 log row; the cutoff rule of every report is score >= threshold and the accuracy and new-style recall at 0.5 built by the script equal the internal report of evaluate_holdout (check passed). PR-AUC and ROC-AUC with 95% artist-clustered intervals (1,000 draws, seed 1): stage 0 PR-AUC 0.4986 [0.4932, 0.5036], ROC-AUC 0.6781 [0.6748, 0.6813]; baseline (c) PR-AUC 0.3464 [0.3429, 0.3502], ROC-AUC 0.5586 [0.5553, 0.5620]; the intervals do not overlap (the stage 0 PR-AUC lower bound is 14.30 points above the baseline (c) upper bound), the PR-AUC difference is +15.22 points and the ROC-AUC difference +11.96 points (from the unrounded values 0.678146 and 0.558563; the 4-decimal table values give 11.95). By year (descriptive, rows, PR-AUC, ROC-AUC), stage 0: 2018 32,557, 0.5000, 0.6803; 2019 32,213, 0.4971, 0.6718; 2020 31,571, 0.4949, 0.6726; 2021 26,830, 0.4969, 0.6834; 2022 20,057, 0.5080, 0.6868; baseline (c): 2018 0.3470, 0.5597; 2019 0.3478, 0.5555; 2020 0.3451, 0.5540; 2021 0.3423, 0.5607; 2022 0.3510, 0.5653; positive rates by year 0.3134 / 0.3155 / 0.3144 / 0.3065 / 0.3136; year 2023 has no rows (all its labelled rows have a next active year after 2023). Threshold report, accuracy with its references (flagged share, accuracy, majority-class accuracy "always no new style" 0.6872, holdout positive rate 0.3128 as the precision of a score without skill): stage 0 at 0.5 flags 9.40%, accuracy 0.7065; stage 0 at 0.3067 flags 48.52%, accuracy 0.6144; baseline (c) at 0.5 flags 0.00% (no row), accuracy 0.6872; baseline (c) at 0.3067 flags 65.19%, accuracy 0.4814. Per class (precision, recall, F1; support 98,423 for no new style and 44,805 for new style): stage 0 at 0.5: no new style 0.7172, 0.9457, 0.8158; new style 0.6028, 0.1811, 0.2785; stage 0 at 0.3067: no new style 0.7929, 0.5940, 0.6792; new style 0.4250, 0.6592, 0.5168; baseline (c) at 0.5: no new style 0.6872, 1.0000, 0.8146; new style precision and F1 undefined (no row flagged), recall 0.0000; baseline (c) at 0.3067: no new style 0.7422, 0.3759, 0.4991; new style 0.3422, 0.7132, 0.4625. Top 10% by score (rows with a score at or above the 90th percentile of the holdout scores): stage 0 cutoff 0.4943 selects exactly 10.00% of rows (14,323), precision 0.5980 against the positive rate 0.3128, lift 1.91; baseline (c) cutoff 0.3636 selects 16.19% (23,183 rows, because its scores tie), precision 0.3498, lift 1.12. Reading, plain language and no more than the numbers show: the signal is moderate and consistent with the validation folds, where stage 0 had PR-AUC 0.4607 to 0.5002 and ROC-AUC 0.6677 to 0.6825 (pooled 0.6732) and baseline (c) had PR-AUC 0.3474 to 0.3698 and pooled ROC-AUC 0.5712; the holdout PR-AUC of stage 0 (0.4986) and ROC-AUC (0.6781) fall inside those ranges, the holdout PR-AUC of baseline (c) (0.3464) is 0.1 points below its validation range and its ROC-AUC (0.5586) is below its pooled value. The PR-AUC difference to baseline (c) on the holdout (15.22 points) is larger than in any validation fold (11.33 to 14.05 points) and than the 11.26 to 13.68 points of the development check of the label rule (entry "WHAT holdout rule: expected effect on the metric"); no explanation is claimed for it. Accuracy is not informative here: at 0.5 it is 0.7065 against 0.6872 for always predicting no new style, and at 0.3067 it is 0.6144, below that reference. The orientative figures are precision, recall and lift: at 0.5 stage 0 flags 9.4% of the artist-years and 60.3% of those flagged do introduce a new style (against 31.3% overall) but it finds only 18.1% of the artist-years that do; at 0.3067 it finds 65.9% of them but 57.5% of the flagged rows do not introduce one; in the top 10% by score 59.8% do, a lift of 1.91. These figures show a moderate signal, not case-by-case reliability. Limitations: the label rule favors short gaps, so the holdout positive rate (0.3128) differs from the training rate (0.3067) and a threshold at the training rate flags a different share of rows; the effective window is 2018-2022; calibration was not checked and the thresholds are conventions (0.3067 is the training positive rate), not tuned operating points; the threshold metrics have no confidence intervals; artists recur between train and holdout (the split is temporal and not by artist, and the share of holdout artists that also have training rows was not computed here); the rows are artist-years pooled over 2018-2022; baseline (c) is coarse, with ties at its cell rates; there is a single evaluation, so no repeat or variability across fits is available; row_scores.csv stores artist_id as a float (for example 3.0) and is not overwritten. Differences from the WHEN holdout report: the thresholds were logged before the run, the majority-class reference and the positive rate are printed next to accuracy, accuracy is in a table, a second threshold (the training positive rate) and the top-10% lift are added, baseline (c) is reported because its score is a probability (the WHEN baseline score was a rate of Works per year and its report at 0.5 was meaningless), the 95% intervals come from the single run (WHEN needed a second pass over the holdout), the files were written into a temporary folder and renamed only after all were complete, and row-level scores were saved.
- WHAT stage 0 train-versus-validation diagnostic, results (15-10-2026, scripts/run_xgb_what_overfit_check.py, results/trials/xgb_what_overfit.csv, results/trials/xgb_what_overfit_20261015_122658.out; the entry "WHAT stage 0 train-versus-validation diagnostic" fixed everything below before the run; development folds only (train rows and validation rows of each fold), sealed holdout untouched, descriptive only, no decision depends on it): stage 0 (parameters of the log row stage0) was refitted once per fold with xgb.train and FIXED_PARAMS, and the same fitted model scored the train and the validation rows. Reproduction: the validation PR-AUC equals the log row and the gate values at 4 decimals in every fold (0.4607 / 0.4751 / 0.4946 / 0.4947 / 0.5002), the largest unrounded difference to the log row being 0.00e+00. Per fold 1 to 5 (train rows 232,404 / 282,606 / 347,763 / 430,809 / 539,157; validation rows 58,141 / 74,558 / 93,072 / 121,659 / 156,591): train PR-AUC 0.5063 / 0.5054 / 0.5028 / 0.5013 / 0.5039, validation PR-AUC 0.4607 / 0.4751 / 0.4946 / 0.4947 / 0.5002, gap train minus validation 4.56 / 3.03 / 0.83 / 0.65 / 0.38 points (mean 1.89); train ROC-AUC 0.7138 / 0.7106 / 0.7065 / 0.7027 / 0.6993, validation ROC-AUC 0.6690 / 0.6781 / 0.6825 / 0.6677 / 0.6708, gap 4.48 / 3.24 / 2.41 / 3.50 / 2.85 points (mean 3.30); positive rate of the train rows 0.2916 / 0.2944 / 0.2958 / 0.2983 / 0.3039 and of the validation rows 0.3064 / 0.3018 / 0.3161 / 0.3266 / 0.3228. The PR-AUC gap shrinks with the fold while the train PR-AUC stays between 0.5013 and 0.5063, so the shrinking comes from the validation PR-AUC rising; the ROC-AUC gap does not shrink steadily (fold 4 is above fold 3). The validation PR-AUC is above baseline (c) (0.3474 / 0.3479 / 0.3625 / 0.3698 / 0.3597) in all five folds, by 11.33 / 12.72 / 13.21 / 12.49 / 14.05 points. Reading, as fixed in the entry of the diagnostic: the gaps are small (at most 4.56 points for PR-AUC and 4.48 for ROC-AUC, the PR-AUC gap falling to 0.38 points) and there is no collapse of the validation score; the model looks limited by its inputs more than overfitted, which is a hypothesis and not tested, and is consistent with the plateau of the tuning (entries "WHAT XGBoost phase 2 (grid) results and phase 3 (manual) design" and "WHAT XGBoost adoption evaluation: stage 0 kept"). The validation positive rate is above the train rate in every fold (by 0.7 to 2.8 points), which is expected to raise the validation PR-AUC and so make the PR-AUC gap somewhat smaller than at equal rates (not quantified); the ROC-AUC gap does not depend on the rate and is the rate-free figure. The WHEN gap (mean 0.0946 and 0.0627 for stage 0) is a different task and prevalence and is not used as a reference. Limitations: the validation folds were used for model selection (tuning, the adoption comparison and the five-model comparison), so the gaps are descriptive and not an unbiased estimate of future performance, and the single holdout evaluation is that estimate; the check is on five folds with no repetition across fits; no threshold for overfitting was set.

- Style trend forecast, design fixed before any model result exists (15-10-2026, src/style_series.py, src/style_checks.py): goal = forecast the 10 styles with the highest growth in Works next year, with the expected Works per style and a range, and let a demo user query the models for chosen styles; counts are Works catalogued in Discogs (community-dependent), not sales, listens or real-world output. Series: dated Works with at least one style tag, a Work counts once in each of its styles (510,364 undated and 851,725 untagged Works dropped, 731 styles, 1894 to 2026). Growth for target year t+1 = c_{t+1} / mean(c_t, c_{t-1}, c_{t-2}) - 1; a style is eligible when that 3-year mean base is at least 100 (a convention; sensitivity with 50 and 200 is descriptive only, never shipped). Reliable years: last reliable year 2023 (ratio of the year's Works to the 2017-2019 median at least 0.85, a judgment rule and not a test; 2023 = 0.877, 2024 = 0.795); 2024 to 2026 are not used anywhere (submission lag, partial year). Folds: walk-forward with an expanding window, one target year at a time, each year predicted only with targets already known at its origin (train rows = origins whose target year is at most t); development target years 1993 to 2017, reported in the five project tranches (1993-97, 1998-02, 2003-07, 2008-12, 2013-17) plus pooled; final test = target years 2018 to 2022, scored once, no pass rule applied there; 2023 is excluded from the test (anomalous, close to the loading lag) and is used only as the base year of the 2024 projection. Baselines (ranked among styles eligible for the target year): random (analytic 100/E for top_10_styles), repeat last year's ranking (growth of year t), mean growth of years t, t-1, t-2. Models (one fit per method and fold gives both ranking and count): BayesianRidge, XGBoost (max_depth 3, 200 rounds, other settings default, seed 1) and Random Forest (200 trees, max_depth 6, min_samples_leaf 10, max_features sqrt, seed 1), none tuned. Rows: (style, origin year t) with base at least 100. Features: log1p of c_t, c_{t-1}, c_{t-2}; lagged log growth at 1, 2 and 3 years; style age (t minus first year with at least 10 Works); primary genre (most frequent genre among the style's Works dated up to t, one-hot over the 11 genres); share of the year's total. Target: log(max(c_{t+1}, 1) / mean base), not demeaned; ranking = predicted value among eligible styles; expected Works = mean base x exp(prediction). Decision metrics: top_10_styles (overlap of the predicted and actual top 10 of the target year) and Spearman between predicted and actual growth over the styles scored by every method; top_5_styles is reported next to it. 95% intervals: bootstrap over years (2,000 draws, seed 1) on paired differences; bootstrap over styles within year for Spearman. Pass rule on the development years, all five must hold: the candidate is the model with the highest mean top_10_styles; (1) its mean top_10_styles is at least 0.5 above the best baseline; (2) at least as many hits as the best baseline in at least 15 of 25 years; (3) lower bound of the year-bootstrap interval of the paired difference above 0; (4) its mean exceeds the random expectation by at least 1.0 hit with the lower bound above 0; (5) its mean Spearman is not below the best baseline's. Consequence: if the rule passes, the candidate is labelled the recommended ranking; if not, the best baseline is. All three models stay available in the demo either way, labelled with their validation results. Count range: expected Works x exp of the 10th and 90th percentile of the log residuals of earlier folds only, all eligible styles pooled; the first target year has no earlier residuals and is excluded from count metrics; metrics: median absolute percentage error and share of actual counts inside the range (nominal 80%), over all eligible styles and over the predicted top 10, each model against the count baselines (repeat c_t and the 3-year mean base, ranges built the same way). The BayesianRidge predictive standard deviation is not used for the range; its coverage is not assumed. Projection: base year 2023, target 2024, illustrative with a visible warning (2023 spikes in Noise Rock, Plunderphonics, Alt-Pop and Future House, counts at the 2023 catalog loading level, no truth exists); training rows include target year 2023, no 2024 to 2026 count is used. Demo: user picks a style and a year or up to 5 styles from a dropdown; for a past year X the model is refitted with data up to X only (backtest, truth known up to 2022), for 2024 it is the projection; fitted models are saved to files read by the app; WHEN and WHAT models are unchanged. Context signals, descriptive only, shown for the projected top 10 in base year 2023, never model inputs, never used to select or re-rank, no composite index, no thresholds or labels: (a) distinct labels and top-3 label share of the style's Works, each Work counted once per label of its linked release (main release for masters, own release for synthetic Works); (b) share of the style's Works whose release has no label or only "Not On Label" names, with real-label versions of (a) excluding those names; (c) synthetic share of the style's Works, next to the overall synthetic share of the year; each shown with its percentile among the eligible styles of the year. Limitations: Discogs measures catalogued releases; master versus synthetic is defined by the artist's standalone releases anywhere in the dump (uses later releases); the dump has no upload date, so a bulk catalog load cannot be ruled out; 2023 growth of the top styles is present in both master and synthetic Works and is not concentrated by label or by date for Noise Rock and Alt-Pop, but Russian Pop, Future House, Latin and Ethno-pop sit at the 91st to 100th percentile of top-3 label share; test is 5 years, so test intervals are wide; the first dev target years have few eligible styles and little training history. The label figures of the context signals (a) and (b) and the master versus synthetic comparison come from src/style_checks.py and were reproduced on 15-10-2026 by scripts/run_style_design_checks.py, with the outputs in results/style_design_checks/ (base year 2023, the Works of each style dated 2023, percentile among the styles eligible for the 2024 projection; the parameters this entry does not describe were chosen in the script and are printed by it). The four percentile figures match: Russian Pop 99.6, Future House 94.3, Latin 91.5 and Ethno-pop 94.7 are inside the 91st to 100th range, and Noise Rock 44.5 and Alt-Pop 28.3 are not concentrated. One figure differs: the statement that the 2023 growth of the top styles is present in both master and synthetic Works holds for 10 of 10 top styles in synthetic Works but for 9 of 10 in master Works, where Latin has a growth of -0.21 (present read as growth above 0, the entry gives no figure).

- Style trend forecast, clarifications to the design entry, fixed before any model is fitted (15-10-2026, src/style_forecast.py; the design entry is unchanged and these points settle what it left open): (1) genres: the primary-genre one-hot uses the 11 real genres of the Works table; the literal tag "1" (8 of 4,587,128 kept Works, never the most frequent genre of any style) is ignored. (2) training rows: for target year T the origin is t = T - 1, and training rows are the (style, origin) pairs with origin at most T - 2 and base at least 100, so every training label is known at the origin and the target year T is never seen; "origins whose target year is at most t" in the design entry is read this way. (3) lagged log growth at 1, 2 and 3 years: log((c_k + 1) / (c_{k-1} + 1)) evaluated at k = t, t - 1 and t - 2 (one-year growth at three successive years, not growth over 1, 2 and 3 years), which avoids repeating the information of the log1p counts; a convention, not a unique answer. (4) share of the year's total: c_t over the sum of the style-year cells of year t (a Work counted once per style), the same universe as the style counts. (5) BayesianRidge: features standardized with mean and standard deviation computed on the training rows of each fold only, and applied unchanged to the rows being predicted; XGBoost and Random Forest receive unscaled features. (6) best baseline: the one with the higher mean top_10_styles over the 25 development target years among repeat last year and mean growth of t, t - 1 and t - 2, a tie going to repeat last year; random is reported separately as a reference and is never the best baseline. First development target year stays 1993 (136 eligible styles and 3,058 training rows in the Step 1 report: 136 to 223 eligible styles and 3,058 to 7,516 training rows across 1993 to 2017); the first origin with any eligible style is 1904, so the 1993 fold trains on rows from 1904 on.

- Style trend forecast, Random Forest tuning protocol and adoption rule, fixed before phase 1 (15-10-2026, src/style_forecast.py, src/tuning.py, scripts/run_rf_style_random_search.py, run_rf_style_grid_search.py, run_rf_style_manual.py, results/trials/rf_style_forecast.jsonl; development target years 1993 to 2017 only, the final test years 2018 to 2022 play no role in any tuning decision and are scored at the end with the locked configuration; this entry supersedes "none tuned" of the design entry for the Random Forest only, every other design and clarification point is unchanged): model to tune = Random Forest, the model with the highest mean top_10_styles (6.16 against 6.12 BayesianRidge and 6.00 XGBoost) and the highest mean Spearman (0.712 against 0.699 and 0.695) on the 25 development years (scripts/run_style_forecast_dev.py, results/style_forecast/dev_hits.csv); the intervals overlap, so this is selection by the highest mean as in the candidate definition of the pass rule, not a significant difference. BayesianRidge and XGBoost stay untuned as references. Stage 0 = the design settings (n_estimators 200, max_depth 6, min_samples_leaf 10, max_features sqrt, seed 1), same rows, features, target and walk-forward as the design and clarification entries. Folds: the 25 walk-forward development years grouped in the five project tranches (1993-97, 1998-02, 2003-07, 2008-12, 2013-17). Selection metric: mean Spearman between the predicted and the actual growth over all styles eligible in each target year, averaged over the 25 years; each tranche has 5 years, so the mean of the five tranche means is the same number and tranches weigh equally, as in the WHEN and WHAT protocols; top_10_styles and top_5_styles per year are recorded in the log and are descriptive during tuning, because integer hits over 25 years tie often and the best of many combinations picked on them would pick noise; Spearman measures the whole ranking and not only the top 10, a known limit. Three phases as in the WHEN and WHAT protocols: (1) random search, 35 combinations plus the stage 0 row, seed 1 with trial i drawn from the stream [1, i], over max_depth 3 to 14 (integer), min_samples_leaf 1 to 50 (log-uniform), max_features among sqrt, 0.3, 0.5 and 0.8 (equal probability), max_samples 0.3 to 1.0 (uniform, bootstrap on), n_estimators 300 fixed, random_state 1; (2) focused grid and (3) manual trials, with values decided after the previous phase from its rank correlations, best region and search-space edge hits, and logged here before that phase runs; if the best trials sit at a search-space edge it is reported and the range is not extended. Selection inside each phase: highest mean Spearman, no manual judgment. Every finished trial is appended to results/trials/rf_style_forecast.jsonl (parameters, Spearman and hits per year, tranche means, mean, seconds), console logs next to it with a timestamp in the name. Pipeline check before any search: run_trial with the stage 0 parameters must reproduce the development Spearman of scripts/run_style_forecast_dev.py (0.712 mean) and its top_10_styles per year exactly. Adoption rule: the candidate is the best non-stage0 trial of the whole tuning by the selection rule, compared with stage 0; if a stage 0 row has the highest mean, stage 0 stays and there is no comparison. The candidate replaces stage 0 only if all hold: (1) the lower bound of the paired year-bootstrap interval (2,000 draws over the 25 years, seed 1, percentiles 2.5 and 97.5) of the mean Spearman difference candidate minus stage 0 is above 0; (2) its mean Spearman is at least that of stage 0 in at least 4 of the 5 tranches; (3) its mean top_10_styles is not lower than that of stage 0. Reason: selection and comparison use the same development years, so the tuned score carries a selection advantage, and a tie is resolved toward the configuration with no free choices; the paired bootstrap is the adaptation of the non-overlapping interval rule of the WHAT adoption rule, because both configurations score the same years; it is a conservative decision rule and not a significance test, the 4 of 5 threshold is a convention, and the rule leans toward keeping stage 0. If the candidate is adopted, a train versus validation diagnostic (the Spearman on each fold's own training rows against the target year) is created and run, as in WHEN and WHAT; if not, it is not. After the tuning the pass rule of the design entry is applied once more on the development years with the locked Random Forest in place of the untuned one, its five conditions unchanged; if it fails, the shipped ranking stays repeat last year. The test procedure, the 2024 projection and the output files are fixed in a later entry before the test years are scored. Limitations: 35 random draws and a small grid explore a small part of the space; selection and comparison share the same 25 development years and there is no nested validation; Spearman is not the top 10 metric of the pass rule; the paired bootstrap resamples years only and ignores variation between styles and seeds.

- Style trend forecast, Random Forest tuning phase 1 (random search) results and phase 2 (grid) design, fixed before phase 2 (15-10-2026, results/trials/rf_style_forecast.jsonl, scripts/run_rf_style_random_search.py, src/tuning.py run_rf_random_search; development target years 1993 to 2017 only, the final test years 2018 to 2022 are not touched; the entry "Random Forest tuning protocol and adoption rule" is unchanged): 36 rows in the log (the stage 0 row and 35 random combinations). The pipeline check reproduced scripts/run_style_forecast_dev.py exactly: top_10_styles and top_5_styles equal in all 25 years, mean Spearman 0.7117, mean top_10_styles 6.16. Mean Spearman over the 25 years: stage 0 0.7117, best 0.7283 (trial 12: max_depth 13, min_samples_leaf 7, max_features 0.5, max_samples 0.325), median 0.7212, worst 0.6924; 29 of 35 trials are above stage 0 and none is within 0.002 of it, so the whole region around the stage 0 depth is below the median of the search. Top 10 by mean Spearman (trial: max_depth / min_samples_leaf / max_features / max_samples: mean): 12: 13 / 7 / 0.5 / 0.325: 0.7283, 14: 13 / 20 / 0.5 / 0.654: 0.7276, 27: 13 / 5 / 0.8 / 0.347: 0.7272, 28: 10 / 13 / 0.5 / 0.890: 0.7270, 21: 10 / 6 / 0.8 / 0.910: 0.7253, 17: 11 / 3 / 0.5 / 0.789: 0.7252, 31: 11 / 4 / 0.3 / 0.693: 0.7251, 7: 12 / 11 / 0.3 / 0.751: 0.7251, 25: 9 / 6 / 0.3 / 0.521: 0.7242, 2: 10 / 5 / 0.3 / 0.594: 0.7241. Spearman rank correlation of each hyperparameter with the mean over the 35 trials: max_depth +0.819, min_samples_leaf -0.103, max_features +0.037 (sqrt coded as 0, an arbitrary order, read loosely), max_samples +0.070; mean by max_features sqrt / 0.3 / 0.5 / 0.8: 0.7203 / 0.7176 / 0.7212 / 0.7165, so max_features hardly separates trials (differences at most 0.005). Only max_depth shows a clear trend and the best trials sit in its upper part. Search-space edges, reported and not acted on: the sampled maximum of max_depth was 13 (the range allows 14) and the three best trials use 13; min_samples_leaf of the top 10 is 3 to 20 and max_samples 0.325 to 0.910, both interior. Spread of the search (best minus worst) 0.0358, against a yearly standard deviation of the Spearman of about 0.060 (mean across trials) and a standard error of a 25-year mean of about 0.012 if years are treated as independent. Phase 2 design (27 combinations, one fit set each): max_depth 9, 11, 13 (the range of the top 10 is 9 to 13); min_samples_leaf 3, 7, 15 (the top 10 use 3 to 20, median 6); max_features 0.3, 0.5, 0.8 (the weakest axis, kept to see whether it matters at high depth). Fixed values: max_samples = the median of the 10 best phase 1 trials (read from the log by the script by this rule, about 0.67, not typed in), n_estimators 300, bootstrap on, random_state 1. Trial ids g1 to g27, in the order of the product of max_depth, min_samples_leaf, max_features (first axis slowest). Selection inside the phase: highest mean Spearman over the 25 years, no manual judgment; the candidate pool for the adoption rule is the best non-stage0 trial of all phases. The grid axes were chosen after seeing the phase 1 result from its rank correlations and best region, and are logged here before the grid runs; the 1000-tree rerun of the XGBoost grids has no counterpart for the Random Forest (more trees only reduce variance) and is not copied. Limitations: the grid is 27 points around the best region of a 35-draw search; selection and comparison share the same 25 development years; max_samples is fixed at a summary of the phase 1 region and was not varied in the grid.

- Style trend forecast, Random Forest tuning phase 2 (grid) results and phase 3 (manual) design, fixed before phase 3 (15-10-2026, results/trials/rf_style_forecast.jsonl, scripts/run_rf_style_grid_search.py, src/tuning.py run_rf_grid_search; development target years 1993 to 2017 only, the final test years 2018 to 2022 are not touched; the entries "Random Forest tuning protocol and adoption rule" and "phase 1 results and phase 2 design" are unchanged): 63 rows in the log (36 from phase 1 and the 27 combinations g1 to g27, no duplicate ids), fixed max_samples 0.673514 read from the log as the median of the 10 best phase 1 trials. Mean Spearman over the 25 years across the 27 combinations: best 0.7277 (g26: max_depth 13, min_samples_leaf 15, max_features 0.5), median 0.7259, worst 0.7224; all 27 are above stage 0 (0.7117), none is above the phase 1 best (0.7283) and 11 are within 0.002 of it. Top 10 (id: max_depth / min_samples_leaf / max_features: mean): g26: 13 / 15 / 0.5: 0.7277, g14: 11 / 7 / 0.5: 0.7276, g18: 11 / 15 / 0.8: 0.7275, g17: 11 / 15 / 0.5: 0.7275, g24: 13 / 7 / 0.8: 0.7271, g27: 13 / 15 / 0.8: 0.7271, g9: 9 / 15 / 0.8: 0.7270, g23: 13 / 7 / 0.5: 0.7269, g8: 9 / 15 / 0.5: 0.7268, g15: 11 / 7 / 0.8: 0.7265. Spearman rank correlation of each axis with the mean within the grid: max_depth +0.268, min_samples_leaf +0.355, max_features +0.530; mean by axis value: max_depth 9 / 11 / 13: 0.7249 / 0.7262 / 0.7261, min_samples_leaf 3 / 7 / 15: 0.7251 / 0.7260 / 0.7261, max_features 0.3 / 0.5 / 0.8: 0.7243 / 0.7266 / 0.7262; each axis moves the mean by 0.0012 to 0.0023, and depth above 11 and leaf above 7 are flat. Grid-axis edges, reported and not acted on: the best combination g26 sits at the edge of two axes (max_depth 13, min_samples_leaf 15); max_features 0.5 is interior. Spread of the grid (best minus worst) 0.0053 against a yearly standard deviation of the Spearman of about 0.060 and a standard error of a 25-year mean of about 0.012 if years are treated as independent: the grid shows plateau-level differences and not a resolved optimum. Pool ranking of the best non-stage0 trials of phases 1 and 2 (mean Spearman, top_10_styles): trial 12 0.7283 (6.40), g26 0.7277 (6.28), g14 0.7276 (6.28), trial 14 0.7276 (6.20), g18 0.7275 (6.28); the candidate by the selection rule is phase 1 trial 12 (max_depth 13, min_samples_leaf 7, max_features 0.5, max_samples 0.325, n_estimators 300), and its lead over g26 (0.0006) is far below the noise, so the choice is mechanical and not a meaningful difference. Phase 3 design (seven trials, one fit set each, each changing one setting of the candidate, base configuration read by the script from the log by the selection rule and not typed in): m_depth16 (max_depth 16, above the phase 1 range, where the best trials sat at its upper part); m_depth_none (max_depth unlimited); m_leaf30 (min_samples_leaf 30, above the grid edge 15, with trial 14 at 20 among the best); m_trees600 (n_estimators 600, to check that 300 trees are enough; more trees only reduce variance); m_samples1.0 (max_samples 1.0, because the candidate uses a low 0.325); m_nobootstrap (bootstrap off, every tree sees all rows, max_samples not used); m_absolute_error (criterion absolute_error, robust to the spikes in the heavy-tailed log growth target; much slower than squared_error, so it runs last and its seconds are reported).
