Selection: highest mean Spearman over the 25 years, no manual judgment; a manual trial enters the candidate pool of the adoption rule only through the same selection rule, so it replaces trial 12 only if its mean is higher. Families not explored, reported as a limitation: ccp_alpha, min_impurity_decrease, max_leaf_nodes and min_samples_split, which regularize like max_depth and min_samples_leaf (already covered), and whose scale depends on the variance of the target; the inputs, the target definition and the minimum 100 are fixed by the design; BayesianRidge and XGBoost stay untuned references. Limitations: seven single-setting trials around one candidate do not map interactions; selection and comparison share the same 25 development years.

- Style trend forecast, Random Forest tuning phase 3 (manual) results and phase 4 (adoption evaluation) design, fixed before phase 4 (15-10-2026, results/trials/rf_style_forecast.jsonl, scripts/run_rf_style_manual.py, src/tuning.py run_rf_manual_search; development target years 1993 to 2017 only, the final test years 2018 to 2022 are not touched; the earlier tuning entries are unchanged except the one protocol change stated below): 70 rows in the log (63 from phases 1 and 2 plus the 7 manual trials, 70 distinct ids). The defaults check passed: the stage 0 parameters re-run in memory reproduced the existing log row in every yearly Spearman, top_10_styles and top_5_styles (mean Spearman 0.711716). Base configuration read from the log by the selection rule: phase 1 trial 12 (max_depth 13, min_samples_leaf 7, max_features 0.5, bootstrap on, max_samples 0.325369, n_estimators 300, random_state 1), mean Spearman 0.728281, mean top_10_styles 6.40. Manual trials, one setting changed each (id: setting: mean Spearman, difference against the base, mean top_10_styles, seconds): m_depth16: max_depth 16: 0.728262, -0.00002, 6.44, 15.9; m_depth_none: max_depth unlimited: 0.728324, +0.00004, 6.36, 15.9; m_leaf30: min_samples_leaf 30: 0.7248, -0.0035, 6.12, 12.1; m_trees600: n_estimators 600: 0.729665, +0.0014, 6.28, 31.2; m_samples1.0: max_samples 1.0: 0.7268, -0.0015, 6.28, 38.6; m_nobootstrap: bootstrap off: 0.7227, -0.0056, 6.32, 60.3; m_absolute_error: criterion absolute_error: 0.728211, -0.00007, 6.28, 98.7 (about 6.3 times the base time, ranking quality unchanged). Spread of the seven manual trials 0.0069 (0.0049 without m_nobootstrap) against a yearly standard deviation of the Spearman of about 0.060 and a standard error of a 25-year mean of about 0.012 if years are treated as independent: only m_nobootstrap and m_leaf30 move by more than 0.003, both downward, so the manual phase confirms the plateau and no further gain is available. By the selection rule the candidate of the whole tuning is m_trees600 (mean Spearman 0.729665, mean top_10_styles 6.28), the only trial above the base besides m_depth_none (+0.00004, nothing); its lead over trial 12 is 0.0014 (per-year paired difference mean 0.0014, sd 0.0020), below the noise, so the choice is mechanical; doubling the trees reduces the variance of the forest and adds no capacity; its top_10_styles is lower than that of trial 12 (6.28 against 6.40), which is descriptive because the selection uses the Spearman only. Candidate configuration: max_depth 13, min_samples_leaf 7, max_features 0.5, bootstrap on, max_samples 0.325369, n_estimators 600, random_state 1. Phase 4 design, three parts on the development years only, console output only: (1) adoption evaluation exactly as in the protocol entry, candidate m_trees600 against stage 0, computed from the yearly values of the trial log: the paired year-bootstrap (2,000 draws over the 25 years, seed 1, percentiles 2.5 and 97.5) of the mean Spearman difference, the number of tranches (of 5) where the candidate mean is at least that of stage 0, and the mean top_10_styles of both; the candidate replaces stage 0 only if all three conditions hold, otherwise stage 0 is the locked Random Forest. (2) Protocol change, fixed before the diagnostic runs: the train versus validation diagnostic is run for stage 0 and for the candidate whether or not the candidate is adopted, so both gaps are on record (the protocol entry ran it only on adoption): for each of the 25 walk-forward years, the Spearman between prediction and actual target on the fold's own training rows (the forest scores the rows it was fitted on) against the Spearman on the target year, reported per year, per tranche and as a mean, with the gap; a deeper forest is expected to show a larger gap because each tree reproduces its training rows, so the gap is not by itself a rejection criterion; what makes extra overfitting harmful is a validation score below that of stage 0, which conditions (1) and (2) of the adoption rule already exclude, so the diagnostic adds no condition and is reported as it is. (3) The pass rule of the design entry is applied once more on the development years with the locked Random Forest in place of the untuned one, BayesianRidge and XGBoost untuned, its five conditions unchanged (the candidate is the model with the highest mean top_10_styles; (1) at least 0.5 hits above the best baseline, repeat last year at 6.00 on the development years; (2) at least as many hits as the best baseline in at least 15 of 25 years; (3) lower bound of the paired year-bootstrap interval above 0; (4) at least 1.0 above the random expectation with the lower bound above 0; (5) mean Spearman on the common styles not below the best baseline's), all methods recomputed in one consistent walk-forward run with the locked parameters; the untuned models must reproduce results/style_forecast/dev_hits.csv and the Random Forest must reproduce its log row; if the rule fails the shipped ranking stays repeat last year and the tuned Random Forest remains available in the demo labelled with its validation results. The locked configuration is not changed after the test years are seen. Limitations: the tuned gain is measured on the same 25 development years used to select it; the adoption bootstrap resamples years only; the Spearman is a whole-ranking metric and the pass rule uses top_10_styles of the top 10; the families ccp_alpha, min_impurity_decrease, max_leaf_nodes and min_samples_split were not explored (redundant with depth and leaf, scale depends on the target variance).

- Style trend forecast, adoption and pass rule results, final test, 2024 projection and output procedure, fixed before the test years are scored (15-10-2026, src/style_forecast.py, src/tuning.py, scripts/run_rf_style_adoption.py, scripts/run_style_forecast_test.py, scripts/run_style_forecast_spearman.py, results/trials/rf_style_forecast.jsonl, results/style_forecast/; the earlier entries of this forecast are unchanged): (1) Adoption, from the trial log (results/trials/rf_style_forecast_adoption_20261015_171211.out): the candidate m_trees600 replaces stage 0, the three conditions hold: mean Spearman difference +0.01795 with paired year-bootstrap interval 0.01204 to 0.02429, mean Spearman at least that of stage 0 in 5 of 5 tranches, mean top_10_styles 6.28 against 6.16. The locked Random Forest is n_estimators 600, max_depth 13, min_samples_leaf 7, max_features 0.5, bootstrap on, max_samples 0.3253691715004556, random_state 1; BayesianRidge and XGBoost stay untuned with the design settings. (2) Train versus validation diagnostic on the 25 development years, stage 0 against the locked forest: Spearman on the fold's own training rows 0.7699 against 0.8063, on the target year 0.7117 against 0.7297, gap 0.0582 against 0.0767, worst single-year gap 0.1711 against 0.1775; per tranche gap (stage 0 / locked) 1993-97 0.0257 / 0.0341, 1998-02 0.0981 / 0.1148, 2003-07 0.0490 / 0.0729, 2008-12 0.0058 / 0.0347, 2013-17 0.1121 / 0.1268. The locked forest gains 0.0364 on its training rows and 0.0180 on validation, so about half of its training gain is a larger gap; a deeper forest shows a larger gap by construction, and the validation score is higher in all 5 tranches, which is what the adoption rule requires; overfitting is measured and not eliminated. (3) Pass rule re-applied on the 25 development years with the locked forest (untuned models and baselines reproduce results/style_forecast/dev_hits.csv exactly): mean top_10_styles random_forest 6.28 [5.80, 6.84], bayes_ridge 6.12, repeat_last 6.00 [5.48, 6.52], xgboost 6.00, mean_growth 4.52, random 0.54; candidate random_forest, best baseline repeat_last; condition (1) margin at least 0.5 False (+0.28), (2) at least as many hits in at least 15 of 25 years True (21), (3) lower bound of the paired year-bootstrap interval above 0 False (-0.12, interval -0.12 to 0.68), (4) at least 1.0 above random with the lower bound above 0 True (+5.74, lower bound 5.25), (5) mean Spearman not below the best baseline True (0.7297 against 0.7069). Two of five conditions fail, so the recommended ranking is repeat last year, as fixed in the design entry; the tuned Random Forest stays available in the demo labelled with its validation results. (4) Choice of the selection metric: the tuning selected on the mean Spearman between predicted and actual growth because the target is a continuous quantity and the task is to order styles, so the rank correlation over all eligible styles of each year (the rank information coefficient used for ranking signals) is the natural metric and uses about 230 styles a year; WHEN and WHAT select on PR-AUC because their targets are binary, and a PR-AUC here would need a binary label built from an arbitrary top-k cut with about 10 positives a year; the decision metric is top_10_styles because the product is a top 10, and optimizing a smooth metric while deciding on the product metric is standard practice; the two do not contradict on the development years (the locked forest improves both over stage 0, Spearman +0.018 and top_10_styles +0.12, and the top_10_styles of the 70 configurations stays between about 6.1 and 6.4); the remaining difference is that the Spearman weights the whole ranking and not only its head, a known limit. (5) Spearman interval on the development years: bootstrap over styles within each year (2,000 draws, seed 1, 95% percentile interval) per method, mean over the 25 years, computed by scripts/run_style_forecast_spearman.py with the locked forest in place of the untuned one, console only. (6) Test: target years 2018 to 2022 are scored once with the same walk-forward procedure and settings as development (expanding window, training rows from origins at most T - 2 and base at least 100, features and target as in the design and clarification entries), the locked forest, BayesianRidge and XGBoost untuned and the same baselines; no pass rule is applied on the test; count ranges use the residuals of all earlier folds (development, then earlier test years); reported: top_10_styles and top_5_styles per year and their mean with a 95% bootstrap interval over the 5 years (2,000 draws, seed 1), Spearman on the styles scored by every method per year and mean, count metrics (median absolute percentage error and share inside the range, nominal 0.80) over all eligible styles and over the predicted top 10 for each model and the two count baselines; descriptive and outside the rule: the paired difference of each model minus repeat last year in top_10_styles and in Spearman, with a year-bootstrap interval (2,000 draws, seed 1), on the 25 development years and on the 5 test years; no setting, feature, seed or rule changes after the result is seen, and the script refuses a second run, writes to a temporary folder and renames it only when everything is complete. (7) Projection: base year 2023, target 2024, ranking by repeat last year (the recommended ranking), top 10 listed with its growth, the 2023 Works, the base and the expected Works with the 10th to 90th percentile range of each of the three models side by side; the models are fitted on origins at most 2022 (target year 2023 included as a training label) with the locked parameters and predict from origin 2023; illustrative with the warning of the design entry; the repeat last year ranking uses the growth of 2023 on the 2020 to 2022 base while eligibility for 2024 uses the 2021 to 2023 base, so a style whose own 2020 to 2022 base is below 100 can enter the top 10 through a small denominator; this is reported and not corrected. (8) Outputs: one table results/style_forecast/predictions.csv (style, target_year, method, expected_growth, expected_works, range_low, range_high, rank) for the test years 2018 to 2022 and the 2024 projection, and one fitted model file per model fitted for the projection in results/style_forecast/models/ (bayes_ridge, xgboost, random_forest, each with its input columns and the scaling statistics for bayes_ridge); no model per year is saved; the app reads the table and writes nothing; the integration with the demo is decided separately. Known limits carried: the ranges of the predicted top 10 under-cover (observed 0.62 to 0.64 on development for each model's own top 10 against 0.80 nominal) because the residual quantiles pool all eligible styles, reported and not corrected; the test has 5 years, so its intervals are wide; the tuned gain is measured on the same development years that selected it; the Spearman used for selection differs from the top_10_styles of the decision rule; the tuning families ccp_alpha, min_impurity_decrease, max_leaf_nodes and min_samples_split were not explored.

- Style trend forecast, notebook and reporting rules, fixed before the notebook is built (15-10-2026, notebooks/06_style_forecast.ipynb, src/style_forecast.py, src/style_report.py; the earlier entries of this forecast are unchanged): (1) scope: the notebook records the whole forecast in the project order of question, code, result, interpretation and decision, with the logic in src/ and the cells only importing and calling; it reads the saved files (results/style_forecast/dev_hits.csv, results/style_forecast/predictions.csv, results/trials/rf_style_forecast.jsonl) and refits nothing that was already fitted for a result file. (2) Quantities that were only printed to the console are recomputed with the existing functions and no new fit of the test: the development Spearman per method and the pass rule with the locked forest by walk_forward_tuned and pass_rule, and the train versus validation diagnostic by rf_overfit_table, all on target years up to 2017; the test top_10_styles, top_5_styles, Spearman, count metrics and paired differences are recomputed from results/style_forecast/predictions.csv and the style by year counts of the series, a read of the saved predictions and not a new scoring, and they are compared with the values printed in results/style_forecast/test_20261015_173215.out; any difference is reported and the printed values stay the reference. The test script is not run again. (3) Projection table: for each style of the top 10 of the 2024 projection the table shows the growth of 2023, the Works of 2023, the base used by the growth (the mean of 2020 to 2022, equal to the Works of 2023 over one plus the growth, computed from the series and not from the projection file), the base used by the expected Works (the mean of 2021 to 2023) and the expected Works with the range of each of the three models; a style whose 2020 to 2022 base is below 100 is flagged as a small-denominator entry, descriptive only: no style is removed and the ranking is not changed (the entry on the final test and projection states that such a style can enter the top 10 through a small denominator; it applies to the styles flagged in the table). (4) Warnings shown next to the projection: counts are Works catalogued in Discogs and expressed at the 2023 loading level, 2023 has spikes that are not explained, the three models disagree for the largest spikes, the ranges of the predicted top 10 under-cover (0.62 to 0.64 observed on development for each model's own top 10, 0.48 on the test for the locked forest's own top 10, against 0.80 nominal), and no truth exists for 2024. (5) The recommended ranking is stated as repeat last year, with the locked Random Forest shown as the model that orders the whole list better (mean Spearman difference against repeat last year, development and test, with the year-bootstrap intervals) and not as better at the top 10, which the pass rule did not support. (6) Charts: at most three (the tuning trials by mean Spearman with the stage 0 line, top_10_styles by method on development and test, the Works of the flagged top 10 styles over the last years), built by functions in src/style_report.py.

- Style trend forecast, metric names and test reporting scope (15-10-2026, notebooks/06_style_forecast.ipynb, src/style_report.py; the earlier entries of this forecast are unchanged): (1) the notebook, its charts and its text name the metrics top_10_styles and top_5_styles (number of styles of the predicted top 10 or top 5 that are in the real top 10 or top 5), as the entries above do, and not hits@10 and hits@5; the names inside code, results/style_forecast/ and results/trials/ files stay as generated (hits10, hits5) and the notebook relabels them when displayed. (2) Reporting on the test years follows the WHEN and WHAT holdout procedure, where only the locked winner and the baselines were scored on the holdout: the test tables, intervals and chart of the notebook show the locked Random Forest, repeat last year, 3-year mean growth and random only. The untuned BayesianRidge and XGBoost are compared on development years only. Their test columns exist in results/style_forecast/predictions.csv because the single test run produced all methods, they were not used in any decision and are not reported. The 2024 projection keeps the three models, because the ranges shown in the demo come from them and no truth exists for 2024.
- ~~Final WHAT minimum history threshold~~ → **RESOLVED** in EDA section: sensitivity check at min_previous_works=[2,3,5] confirms cutoff 3 is stable (20.18% → 18.06% → 15.45%, linear trend, see EDA entry "WHAT minimum-history-threshold sensitivity check"). Repeated at artist-year level on development years (13-10-2026, src/what_target.py threshold_sensitivity) and keeps 3: prevalence 39.7% (threshold 0), 34.7% (1), 32.4% (2), 30.9% (3), 29.8% (4), 28.9% (5), 27.6% (7); no cliff, a smooth fall of about 1 to 1.5 points per step from threshold 2 on, and the same tranche profile at every threshold (lowest 1998-2002, highest 2008-2012, spread 2 to 3 points). Threshold 3 keeps 267,339 of 844,705 development artists and 1,067,817 of 2,555,349 development artist-years, so WHAT covers a smaller population (about 32% of development artists) than WHEN.
- Final temporal cutoffs. For WHAT, a train row is valid for a fold only if its next_active_year is earlier than the test start, because the label can resolve more than 2 years later (Work-level transitions: 9.4% take over 2 years, 3.3% over 5, 1% over 12), so the fixed 2-year gap used for WHEN does not close the leak. Implemented for the development folds (see the DECIDED entry on the exact label-resolution rule). ~~Still pending: the rule to build the training set for the final holdout evaluation of WHAT~~ → **RESOLVED** (14-10-2026): development rows with year < 2018 and next_active_year < 2018 (687,628 rows), see the DECIDED entry "WHAT holdout label rule".
- Whether WHAT survives formal validation: EDA base-rate check (02_eda_visualization.ipynb, 5,948,995-row checkpoint, 07-10-2026) is a positive early signal, not yet a resolution. Across 4,413,298 consecutive same-artist transitions (656,953 artists with 2+ known-year works, ordered by year, missing-year works excluded from ordering), 23.5% introduce a style never seen before in that artist's history; restricted to transitions with 3+ prior known-year works (the min_previous_works=3 modeling population), the rate is 18.1% over 3,227,980 transitions. Neither near 0% nor near 100%, so WHAT has a real positive class and is worth carrying into formal validation. Distinct-styles-per-artist (same 656,953-artist population): median 3, mean 3.69, p10 1, p90 8, p99 17, max 127. The baseline and the pass rule are now fixed (see the DECIDED entries on the baseline ladder and the kill gate pass rule; a plain constant rate would be passed by any model that learns history_count, so the reference is the history and volume lookup baseline), and the kill gate uses the fast per-fold bootstrap (evaluate_folds in src/backtesting.py), not evaluate_kill_gate, which does not finish at fold size. RESOLVED 14-10-2026: the verdict is PASS (see the DECIDED entry "WHAT kill gate: PASSED").
- WHAT gate diagnostics (post-hoc, development folds only, same fold dicts, bootstrap settings and seed as the gate, no tuning): none of them can rescue or overturn the gate verdict, which stands as pre-registered. D1, new versus returning artists: test rows split by whether the artist has any row in the fold's train table, with rows, share, positive rate, model and baseline (c) PR-AUC and the difference, per fold and pooled. D2, within next-year volume: test rows stratified by the number of Works in the next active year (diagnostic only, never a feature), pooled over folds, with the same measures. D3, the same two views with ROC-AUC next to PR-AUC, since PR-AUC depends on the base rate of each subset (skipped if it complicates the code). D4, feature-group ablation: the same model refitted on the history and Work count inputs only, and on the full set minus each of the groups vocabulary, novelty, timing and format, and genre flags, with PR-AUC, the difference versus baseline (c) and versus the full model per fold. Rule: any change to the feature set caused by these diagnostics is logged as a new decision before the 5-model comparison. RESOLVED 14-10-2026: results are in the DECIDED entry "WHAT gate diagnostics: results"; no feature change follows (see "No feature change follows from the WHAT gate diagnostics").
- src/snapshots.py was fixed to read the `styles` column (plural, a list of tags) instead of a `style` (singular, pipe-separated string) column that notebook 01's real pipeline never produced. Verified against data/processed/works_checkpoint.parquet (5,948,995 rows): `styles` exists, holds a list/array per row (1,018,461 rows with an empty array, 0 rows with a None/NaN scalar), no `style` column present. `build_snapshots()` run on a 500-artist, 16,073-row sample of the real checkpoint produced 15,800 snapshots with no errors, `new_style` taking 0 (11,334), 1 (3,900), and NaN (566) as expected; manually traced one artist's genuinely-new-style transition (new_style=1), one with no new styles (new_style=0), and one with no next work at all (new_style=NaN).
- snapshots.py gap-honesty fix implemented (08-10-2026): two changes to build_snapshots(), both addressing the same root issue, undated works previously vanished completely before any artist timeline was built, hiding that gap rather than reporting it. (1) `artist_undated_work_count`: the total count of an artist's works with no year at all, computed once per artist and attached to every snapshot row for that artist, reported at the artist level rather than localized to one specific gap, because there is no reliable signal to place an undated work inside one gap (release_id was already rejected elsewhere in this project as too noisy for real chronology). (2) `new_style` false-positive fix: an artist's undated works' styles are now folded into `styles_seen_so_far` as baseline vocabulary from that artist's very first snapshot onward, instead of only accumulating from dated anchors, so a style used on an undated work and reused later on a dated work is no longer wrongly flagged as "new". This is a deliberate, conservative tradeoff: it cannot know whether an undated work actually came before or after the dated work being evaluated, so treating its styles as always-already-seen avoids the common false "new style" positive at the cost of possibly missing a rare true one (an undated work that really did come later). Verified on a 500-artist real-checkpoint sample (3,269 rows, 219 dropped for missing year, 3,050 snapshots, no exceptions): `artist_undated_work_count` cross-checked exactly against a manual count for 5 hand-picked artists with undated works (reported vs manual: 1/1, 1/1, 6/6, 2/2, 2/2, all matching). The `new_style` fix was confirmed to change real behavior: artist_id 31 has an undated work tagged [Deep House, Tech House]; before the fix, work_id 214441 (1995) was flagged new_style=1 for introducing "Deep House" on its following work, after the fix it correctly reads 0, since Deep House was already part of this artist's known vocabulary via the undated work.
- Missing year at the feature engineering stage (drop, impute or flag): DECIDED, the implemented handling is at the end of this entry. History of the analysis: Partially resolved by the Master missing-year recovery above (DECIDED): of the original 9.19% missing-year rate, recovery closed the recoverable Master-side portion, leaving overall 8.58% (510,364 of 5,949,217 rows) still missing. Updated via EDA (02_eda_visualization.ipynb, re-run on the current 5,949,217-row checkpoint on 08-10-2026): split by work_source, 3.15% for masters (61,388 of 1,951,057, was 5.1%) and 11.23% for synthetic standalone-derived works (448,976 of 3,998,160, was 11.2%, essentially unchanged since the recovery only applies to masters), so synthetic works are now over 3.5x as likely to be missing year as masters, up from ~2.2x before recovery, the gap widened because masters improved and synthetic did not. The histogram shows missing year is still not era-concentrated (missing rows can't be placed on a year axis). The earlier 6.7% figure was measured on the original masters_df before the various exclusion filters, not directly comparable. The multivariate step of the same notebook shows missing-year rate still varies sharply by genre after recovery (the "1" fake-genre tag excluded from this breakdown), from Latin 25.7% and Reggae 23.0% down to Electronic 1.5%, now an ~18x spread (was 27.0%/25.0%/1.6%, ~17x, before recovery). Spearman correlation between missing_share and median year across the 11 genres is r=-0.721, p=0.012 (significant); min year r=-0.487, p=0.128 and p10 year r=-0.436, p=0.180 remain directionally the same but not significant at n=11 genres. The recovery does not close this genre gap, and if anything widens it slightly: recovery rate is strongly negatively correlated with a genre's original missing-year rate (Spearman r=-0.809, p=0.0026, see DECIDED above), so Latin/Reggae/Folk,World,&Country remain disproportionately under-covered even after recovery, an accepted, unresolved limitation, not a resolution of the equity gap. Style-level check confirms the same pattern, more sharply: missing_share now spans 14.1% (Country) down to 0.40% (Tech House) across the top 15 styles, a ~35.6x spread versus genre's ~18x (was 15.5%/0.44%, ~35x). All three year-distribution stats correlate significantly with missing_share at n=15 styles: min r=-0.689 (p=0.0045), p10 r=-0.736 (p=0.0017), median r=-0.666 (p=0.0067); at the genre level only median is significant (p10 p=0.18, min p=0.13). Handling strategy for the remaining missing years: an era/age-first handling was considered and is not implemented in the code (see the implemented handling at the end of this entry). Hypothesis 5 (02_eda_visualization.ipynb, 08-10-2026) checked whether missing year changes the WHAT target itself, using an artist-level year_missing flag (True if the artist has at least one undated work; 35% of the 3,254,604 modeling-population transitions): new-style rate is 14.6% with undated works vs 20.0% without (chi-square 14,600.77, Cramer's V 0.067), but the gap is mostly catalog size (23.7% of year_missing=True transitions are in the 51+ prior-work bucket vs 5.6% for the rest), and inside prior-work buckets it shrinks to -1.7, -1.4, -0.8, 0.0 and +1.0 points (3-5 to 51+). Artists with undated works do not behave like a different population, which supports the keep-and-flag approach in the DECIDED missing-year dropout entry above; year_missing was kept out of the model inputs (see the implemented handling at the end of this entry), and no evaluation split by year_missing exists in the repo. Update 10-10-2026: REOPENED after the Master-only filter was reverted (see CUT). All three imputation attempts failed or proved immaterial (docs/abandoned_approaches.md), so the 510,364 works (8.58%) missing a year, across master and synthetic, stay in the dataset with year NaN. Practical answer: do not drop these works, flag year_missing explicitly, and handle it at the feature/snapshot level (any feature or label computation that strictly needs a year excludes or special-cases those rows, not the whole work). Implemented handling (15-10-2026, verified in src/snapshots.py, src/models.py and src/what_target.py): (1) Works with a missing year stay in the Works table but get no snapshot row: build_snapshots drops them, counts them in result.attrs["dropped_missing_year"] (510,357 on the full checkpoint, after 95 Works without artist), and treats the styles of an artist's undated Works as already seen when computing new_style. (2) The snapshots carry artist_undated_work_count and year_missing (True when the artist has at least one undated Work) as pass-through columns for analysis only. (3) Neither column is a model input: NUMERIC_FEATURES (WHEN) and WHAT_FEATURES (WHAT) do not contain them, and the entry "Feature allowlist for WHEN modeling" gives the reason (not confirmed safe, whole-catalog artist-level values). (4) For WHAT, undated Works enter no feature, and their styles count as already seen in the label (build_artist_years, build_what_features). Not supported by the code: no imputation was adopted (docs/abandoned_approaches.md), no era-first handling exists, and no evaluation split by year_missing was run.
- Exact final feature set (decided, 15-10-2026): the WHAT feature set stays at 25 inputs (WHAT_FEATURES in src/models.py: history_count and n_works_year from build_what_table, plus 12 numeric or share columns and 11 genre columns from build_what_features), as recorded in the entry "WHAT feature set and point-in-time audit". build_what_features builds the features directly from the Works table, one row per artist-year, computed at the end of each year with only Works dated that year or earlier, and undated Works enter no feature. This replaces the earlier plan to take the last Work of each artist-year from snapshots.parquet. n_works_next_year and any label-derived quantity are diagnostics only and never features (WHAT_FORBIDDEN_INPUTS in src/models.py).
- Minimal config.yaml (15-10-2026, config.yaml, src/snapshots.py load_config and CONFIG): config.yaml holds the data and results paths, the single random seed (project.SEED = 1, also the bootstrap seed), the year bounds (last complete year 2025, partial year 2026, development limit 2017, holdout 2018 to 2023, label gap 2, first style column 1890, first style development target 1993), the bootstrap settings (1,000 draws, 95% confidence, 2,000 draws over years for the style forecast) and what.min_previous_works. Every value equals the literal it replaced, checked one by one; no behaviour changed. Intentionally left in code: locked hyperparameters, search grids and design settings (for example FINAL_XGB_PARAMS, LOCKED_RF_PARAMS, MIN_BASE, TOP_K, the tranches), because each is recorded in the entry that fixed it. The scripts whose outputs are cited in this file or that refuse to run twice keep their own literals (paths, 2017 and 2018, seeds), so that a spent script is never edited; the modules they import read the same values from config.yaml. Dead keys removed: data.* and when.horizons (nothing read them).
- Source of results/demo/live_scores.csv (15-10-2026, notebooks/03_when_backtesting_design.ipynb Step 8, cells 82 to 84): the file was written by a one-off scoring script that is not in the repository, as the notebook states ("a one-off scoring script outside the project; this section only reads its saved output"), and it was first committed in 535816e (13-10-2026). As far as the repo shows: ten artists scored as of 01-09-2026 with the two locked XGBoost configurations (FINAL_XGB_PARAMS) fitted on the same 3,786,030 training rows used for the sealed WHEN holdout (development years up to 2015), with a "live" feature row built from the full dated history and the current year as reference, plus a cross-check score for each artist's last real snapshot. The saved columns follow regular rules in all ten rows (history_count equals dated_works, years_since_last equals 2026 minus last_year, years_active_so_far equals 2026 minus first_year, historical_rate equals history_count over years_active_so_far). Not recorded anywhere in the repo: how the most recent Work is chosen when a year has several, how its genre is encoded, and how the current-year counts are defined. The file is therefore not regenerable from the repo, and no script was written for it, because an unverified reconstruction could not be shown to give the same values. It is a demonstration, not evidence, and no number of the project depends on it (entry in Limitations on demos).
- Transformation and correlation analysis (15-10-2026, notebooks/02_eda_visualization.ipynb section 8, src/eda.py transformation_check and spearman_table): the three right-skewed count columns of the transitions table (prior_work_count, year_gap, prior_style_count; 3,254,604 transitions of the modeling population) were checked before and after log1p. Skewness: 7.34 to 0.92, 7.77 to 1.52 and 3.35 to 0.15; excess kurtosis before: 80.75, 96.49 and 23.18. The D'Agostino-Pearson test (fixed-seed sample of 5,000 rows per column, seed 0, the same rows before and after) rejects normality for all three after the transform (p 1.04e-101, below the floating point range and 3.84e-06), so log1p reduces the skew but does not reach normality: these are whole-number counts with many ties and year_gap has a median of 0. The Spearman table covers the six pairs of the three columns and is_new_style (all rank correlations, n = 3,254,604, all below the Bonferroni threshold of 0.05 / 6 = 0.0083): prior_work_count with prior_style_count 0.589, with is_new_style -0.188 and with year_gap -0.256; year_gap with is_new_style 0.124 and with prior_style_count -0.127; prior_style_count with is_new_style 0.012 (p 4.6e-100, practically null pooled, as in Hypothesis 4). With this n every non-zero correlation is significant, so r is the quantity to read, and the transitions of one artist are not independent, so the p-values are optimistic. Conclusion: no transformation is added to the pipeline. The adopted models (XGBoost for WHEN and WHAT, Random Forest for the style forecast) split on thresholds and are invariant to a monotonic transform, and the tests of the notebook rest on ranks (chi-square on buckets, Mann-Whitney, Spearman). Logistic Regression and Naive Bayes are fitted without a log transform (documented limit of the untuned reference models); the effect of log1p on them was not tested and no claim is made about it. The columns analysed are the EDA transition columns, not the model inputs themselves.

### Leakage in model selection: label look-ahead in development fold 5 (17-10-2026)
Test rows of development fold 5 (origin years 2013-2017) have labels that reach into 2018-2019, the holdout period: 21.32% of rows for WHEN next_1y, 41.70% for next_2y and 39.38% for WHAT (upper bound for WHEN, since some labels are settled by data up to 2017). This is leakage in model selection: the fold 5 score entered the mean used to choose hyperparameters. Features, training labels (ending in 2011-2012) and holdout scores are not affected. Dropping the affected rows lowers the fold 5 PR-AUC of both compared configurations by 0.005 to 0.0065, with a base-rate shift of about 0.004 and a change of population (origin years 2016-2017 removed). The difference between configurations is stable: 0.0004 vs 0.0004 (next_1y), 0.0003 vs 0.0005 (next_2y) and 0.0030 vs 0.0037 (WHAT), with the same sign in the fold 5 difference and in the 5-fold mean difference. The choice between the final configuration and stage 0 does not change. Only two configurations per target were compared, not all trials. WHAT candidate m_lr_0.012 shows a paired fold 5 gain of +0.0030 [0.0022, 0.0037]; it was not adopted under the unpaired rule and the holdout was scored with stage 0, so it is reported here and not applied. Style forecast does not depend on data from 2018 or later in selection. Scripts: scripts/run_label_lookahead_check.py and scripts/run_label_lookahead_ranking_check.py. Outputs: results/label_lookahead/.

## Demo artist selection: eligibility rule (17-10-2026)

Context: the Time Machine demo needs well-known artists across genres, chosen before any score is viewed.

Decision: an artist is a candidate if at least one of these holds for ceremony years 2019 to 2026:
- a Grammy Album of the Year nomination;
- a Primetime Emmy nomination in Outstanding Variety Special (Live), Outstanding Variety Special (Pre-Recorded), Outstanding Music Direction or Outstanding Original Music and Lyrics.
Popularity filler chosen by the author is also admitted, backed where possible by Billboard year-end 2022, the Billboard Latin Music Awards 2019 to 2025, Pitchfork 2022 or the Latin Grammy. One nomination is enough, there is no minimum count.

Limitation: selecting by nominations favours artists whose nominated work coincides with a stylistic change. The demo list is therefore not a random sample and says nothing about global performance. Global metrics come only from the holdout.

Sources (accessed 17-10-2026): en.wikipedia.org pages for the Grammy Album of the Year and for the four Emmy categories above; grammy.com category pages; billboard.com year-end and Latin charts.

## Demo artist selection: cap per genre (17-10-2026)

Decision: at most 10 candidates per genre. Cut order: Album of the Year nominations, then Emmy nominations, then presence in Billboard year-end 2022.
Left out by the cap: Ariana Grande, Miley Cyrus, Rihanna, J Balvin, Maluma, Daddy Yankee, Jennifer Lopez, Billy Joel, Bruce Springsteen, Labrinth.

## Demo artist selection: manual exclusions (17-10-2026)

Excluded by editorial criterion of the author, no reason recorded: Dr. Dre, Snoop Dogg, 50 Cent, Kanye West.
Excluded for data reasons: Elton John, whose catalogue keeps repeated titles across years after cleaning.
These exclusions are manual and were not produced by the eligibility rule.

## Demo artist selection: data-quality criterion (17-10-2026)

Decision: a candidate is kept if, in works_checkpoint.parquet:
- it has works in at least 4 distinct years within 2018 to 2022;
- it has at least 3 rows in the WHAT table for 2018 to 2023, from build_what_table(build_artist_years(works));
- it appears in results/holdout_what/row_scores.csv.
The criterion uses only data coverage, never model output or labels.
Candidates that did not pass: Daft Punk, Adele, boygenius, Vampire Weekend, Brandi Carlile, Peso Pluma, Romeo Santos, Kendrick Lamar, Bon Iver, Arctic Monkeys, Justin Bieber, Beyonce, Usher, A$AP Rocky, Kacey Musgraves.
Limitation: the criterion favours prolific artists. Pool B (entry "Demo pool B: candidates and coverage rule (19-10-2026)") re-admits the candidates named above under a looser coverage rule; this entry and the pool A list are unchanged.

Fixed before any score is viewed. 40 artists, Discogs ids in brackets:
Pop: Taylor Swift (1124645), Billie Eilish (5590213), Lady Gaga (1103159), Olivia Rodrigo (7592689), Sabrina Carpenter (4378284), Dua Lipa (4357593), Harry Styles (2508414), Ed Sheeran (2184482).

Latino: Bad Bunny (5894905), Karol G (4452209), Rauw Alejandro (7153566), Farruko (2929902), Anuel AA (5794658), Ozuna (5395551), Shakira (5530), Rosalia (5648334).

## Duplicate audit: sensitivity analysis (17-10-2026)

Context: same-artist, same-title works in different years survive cleaning because the fuzzy filter skips pairs with a score of 100 and the exact dedup requires the same year.

Decision: a sensitivity analysis on the development partition only, with duplicates collapsed to the earliest work under definition A (same artist_id and normalized title). The result is written to new files with exclusive mode. The rule is fixed before running: the effect is material if the paired change falls outside its 95% confidence interval, or if it changes the choice between the stage 0 and final configurations.
The holdout is not re-evaluated. If a correction is adopted later, the frozen result and the corrected result are both reported, with no choice of the better one.
Definition B is not used: stripping "mono" and "stereo" as whole words changes titles such as "Stereo Love".

## Demo: re-recordings (17-10-2026)

Decision: titles containing "taylor's version" (case-insensitive, matching both the straight apostrophe and the typographic one, which is normalized to the straight one before matching) are flagged as re-recordings in the demo (is_rerecording). Flagged re-recordings are hidden from the timeline cards of tab 6 as noise, controlled by the config key hide_rerecordings in app/demo_config.yaml and based on the existing is_rerecording column. Scores, labels, models and metrics are unchanged because they are computed with all Works. The hiding is display-only. The screen keeps a short notice under each year with hidden re-recordings, with their count; a year whose Works are all re-recordings appears as a gap card with that line.
Limitation: only re-recordings that carry the marker in the title are flagged (see also the Limitations entry "Re-recordings in the demo (19-10-2026)"). They are flagged by title only and are not removed by the cleaning, unlike radio edits and reissues; removing them upstream would require recomputing results whose holdout is spent.

## Demo: WHEN regeneration control (17-10-2026)

Decision: the new script writes with exclusive mode, fits the final WHEN configuration with training up to 2015 and scores only the demo artists. It first reproduces the saved holdout aggregate PR-AUC of 0.7851 to 4 decimals and stops if it does not match. No alternative tolerance is defined.

## Demo: timeline rule per artist and year, and baseline label (17-10-2026)

Decision: the demo timeline shows one row per artist and year. Each artist-year is represented by its last Work in the within-year order of the WHEN snapshots table (src/snapshots.py: sorted by artist_id, year, within_year_rank, work_id, where within_year_rank is a hash of work_id plus a seed from config.yaml), that is, the Work whose works_so_far_this_year equals works_this_year, shown with its own score and its own label. Reproducibility: the order does not depend on the row order of any file, and the CSV file order is not used to pick the Work. Reason: that Work has every earlier Work of its year already counted in history_count, so it has the most history behind it. Limitation: Discogs only gives the year, so the order inside a year is a reproducible pseudo-random convention and not the real release order; the chosen Work is not necessarily the last one released, and the screen must label it as the representative Work of the year, never as the last release (see entry "WHAT is modeled at artist-year level", 57.0% of labelled Work-level transitions with history >= 3 go to a Work of the same year, 1,381,521 of 2,423,318). results/demo/when_demo_scores.csv is not regenerated and does not carry the position columns; the small Works file of the demo artists carries works_so_far_this_year and works_this_year and is joined to the demo rows by work_id, because the app never reads snapshots.parquet (entry "Demo: live scoring with the final models (17-10-2026)"). The baseline shown next to the model and its always-visible label were reversed on 19-10-2026 (see CUT entry "Demo: baseline always visible in the demo screens (reversed 19-10-2026)"). Reference only, kept because they are not cited elsewhere in this file: the holdout figures of baseline (c), the history bucket and current-year volume bucket lookup, are WHAT PR-AUC 0.3464 and ROC-AUC 0.5586, from entry "WHAT final holdout evaluation of stage 0" (14-10-2026).

## Demo: live scoring with the final models (17-10-2026)

Decision: the demo app scores live with the final models: WHEN XGBoost final for next_1y and next_2y (FINAL_XGB_PARAMS in src/models.py, entries "XGBoost tuning results, three phases" and "FINAL holdout evaluation"), WHAT stage 0 (entries "WHAT XGBoost adoption evaluation: stage 0 kept" and "WHAT final holdout evaluation of stage 0"), and, for the style forecast, the Random Forest with the locked configuration (the one adopted in the entry "Style trend forecast, adoption and pass rule results, final test, 2024 projection and output procedure") and repeat_last (no model needed; it stays in the files and in the controls and is not displayed in the app, see entry "Demo: tab 7 shows the Random Forest (19-10-2026)"). The demo includes only final models: the models that were only comparison models in the style forecast (Bayesian Ridge and XGBoost) are not scored in the app, and their results are shown only from the saved result files, in the method tab. Hyperparameters and seeds are those logged in those entries and in "Style trend forecast, adoption and pass rule results, final test, 2024 projection and output procedure", and are not restated here.
Refit: the WHEN and WHAT models are refitted once with the logged hyperparameters and seed on the same training rows as their holdout evaluations (years before 2018 only, as logged in those entries) and saved in a small folder. No holdout evaluation is repeated and no new holdout metric is computed. The metrics shown in the app are the ones already saved in results/holdout_ci95, results/holdout_what and results/style_forecast. The final WHEN and WHAT models were not saved by the scripts that produced the holdout results, which is why they are refitted and checked against the stored scores.
Control: before saving or using any model, live scores must reproduce the stored ones to 4 decimals: results/demo/when_demo_scores.csv for WHEN and results/holdout_what/row_scores.csv for WHAT (stage0_score), on the rows of the demo artists, and all rows of results/style_forecast/predictions.csv for the Random Forest and repeat_last (2018 to 2022 and 2024), growth predictions and ranges. The step stops if any comparison fails.
Style forecast scope: the app scores the style forecast live for every target year of predictions.csv (2018 to 2022 and 2024; tab 7 also scores 2023 live from an extra model, entry "Demo: live style forecast for 2023 and later years (19-10-2026)"). For each target year T of 2018 to 2022, one Random Forest is refitted with the locked configuration and seed (LOCKED_RF_PARAMS in src/style_forecast.py; entry "Style trend forecast, adoption and pass rule results, final test, 2024 projection and output procedure", which also fixes the walk-forward procedure) on the training rows of that target year (origins at most T - 2, labels known), following the walk_forward procedure of scripts/run_style_forecast_test.py and predicting the styles eligible at origin T - 1. The 2024 rows use the existing Random Forest bundle of results/style_forecast/models. repeat_last needs no model. The bundles in results/style_forecast/models stay untouched and are not used by the app. The per-year models are saved in a small folder together with what the ranges need. From count_table and count_bounds in src/style_forecast.py, the range of a row is the expected Works multiplied by exp of the 10th and 90th percentile of the log residuals log(actual / expected) of earlier target years, pooled (all folds scored before T for 2018 to 2022; every scored fold for 2024), where actual is c_next clipped at 1 and expected is base x exp(prediction) for the Random Forest and c_t clipped at 1 for repeat_last. These residuals come from the Random Forest fits of the development years, which are not saved. Limitation: these residuals cannot be recomputed from the saved models alone, so the two percentiles per target year and method are computed once by the refit step and stored as a small table, and the control checks that the ranges built from that table match predictions.csv. Size cap: the folder of per-year models must stay below 150 MB in total (compressed joblib); if it does not, the step stops and reports instead of dropping anything silently.
Inputs: small files only. For WHEN and WHAT, one file with the feature rows of the demo artists (the 40 fixed ids in app/demo_config.yaml), built from the existing snapshots and artist-year tables. For the style forecast, the style-by-origin rows of every origin needed by the per-year models and by the 2024 projection (not only origin 2023), built by build_panel in src/style_forecast.py and saved as one small file. The small Works file of the demo artists carries works_so_far_this_year and works_this_year. The app never reads snapshots.parquet or any large file and does not compute features from raw data.
Limitation: live scoring of WHEN and WHAT works only for the 40 demo artists and the 21 artists of pool B (entry "Demo pool B: final list (19-10-2026)"), not for arbitrary artists. The range of target years is not the same in every tab (WHAT 2018 to the last data year for the artist-years with enough earlier Works, see the entry "Demo: WHAT live assets, 2018 to the last data year (19-10-2026)"; WHEN 2018 to 2023 with 2024 to 2026 as projection; style 2018 to 2024 with 2023 and 2024 flagged and no forecast for 2025 and 2026), and the app must label it on screen. Baselines (for example baseline (c) in WHAT) are not models; the demo screens show only final models, and baselines appear in the method and results tabs, labelled as baselines.

## Demo: live scoring assets, results (17-10-2026)

Files: src/live_assets.py (logic) and scripts/run_live_assets.py (calls it; refuses to run when results/demo/live/ exists and writes with exclusive creation). Models: the ones built by make_xgboost (WHEN next_1y and next_2y, FINAL_XGB_PARAMS) and make_what_xgboost (WHAT stage 0, parameters read from the stage0 row of results/trials/xgb_what_new_style_year.jsonl), saved in the native XGBoost JSON format. Training rows: WHEN, holdout_gap of the development split (the training rows of the holdout run); WHAT, year and next_active_year both before 2018.
Folder results/demo/live/ (sizes from disk): when_next_1y.json 4,013.3 KB, when_next_2y.json 11,629.3 KB, what_stage0.json 1,478.4 KB, when_features.csv 103.3 KB (947 rows), what_features.csv 23.9 KB (160 rows), columns.json 1.4 KB (ordered column list of each model, read from the fitted model), control.csv 0.1 KB. Runtime of the run 114 s.
Control before saving (results/demo/live/control.csv): maximum absolute difference against the stored scores 2.98e-08 for next_1y (947 rows), 2.97e-08 for next_2y (947 rows) and 2.80e-08 for WHAT stage 0 (160 rows), against the limit of 0.00005. Reload check after saving: the three files loaded with XGBClassifier.load_model and the feature files scored with the columns of columns.json give 947, 947 and 160 matched rows, no unmatched row on either side, and maximum absolute differences 2.98e-08, 2.97e-08 and 2.80e-08 against results/demo/when_demo_scores.csv (joined on artist_id, snapshot_year, work_id and target) and results/holdout_what/row_scores.csv (stage0_score, joined on artist_id and year).
Limitation: the feature files cover only the 40 demo artists, so live scoring of WHEN and WHAT works only for them. The style forecast assets are not part of this step.

## Demo: live scoring assets for the style forecast, results (17-10-2026)

Files: src/live_style_assets.py (logic) and scripts/run_live_style_assets.py (calls it; refuses to run when results/demo/live/style/ exists and writes with exclusive creation). Three keys were added to paths in config.yaml: style_predictions (results/style_forecast/predictions.csv), style_bundle (results/style_forecast/models/random_forest.joblib, read only) and style_live (results/demo/live/style). No existing function of src/style_forecast.py was changed.
Procedure: the five per-year Random Forests (target years 2018 to 2022) are refitted with LOCKED_RF_PARAMS on the same training rows as walk_forward (origin at most T - 2, label known). The 30 walk-forward fits (target years 1993 to 2022) produce the residuals for the percentiles; only the 2018 to 2022 models are kept. The 2024 rows use the existing bundle in results/style_forecast/models, loaded read-only and not copied. Percentiles per target year and method come from count_bounds on the folds scored before the year (every scored fold for 2024), pooled.
Folder results/demo/live/style/ (sizes from disk): random_forest_2018.joblib 6,373.3 KB, random_forest_2019.joblib 6,486.9 KB, random_forest_2020.joblib 6,664.6 KB, random_forest_2021.joblib 6,810.0 KB, random_forest_2022.joblib 6,962.9 KB (five models 33,297.7 KB in total, cap 150 MB), panel_rows.csv 338.4 KB (1,444 rows, origins 2017 to 2021 and 2023), percentiles.csv 0.7 KB (12 rows), columns.json 0.3 KB (ordered input columns, read from the fitted model), control.csv 0.6 KB.
Control before saving (results/demo/live/style/control.csv): 1,444 rows per method and 2,888 in total matched against results/style_forecast/predictions.csv, no unmatched row on either side, row counts per target year equal to the stored ones. Maximum absolute difference against the limit of 0.00005: random_forest expected_growth 2.22e-16, expected_works 1.82e-12, range_low 1.82e-12, range_high 1.82e-12; repeat_last expected_growth 4.44e-16, expected_works 0, range_low 1.82e-12, range_high 1.82e-12. Rank, reported apart and not part of the pass rule, matches in all rows (maximum difference 0) for both methods.
Reload check after saving (models loaded from disk, rows rescored from panel_rows.csv and percentiles.csv): same 1,444 matched rows per method, no unmatched row. Maximum absolute difference: random_forest expected_growth 2.22e-16, expected_works 1.82e-12, range_low 3.64e-12, range_high 1.82e-12, rank 0; repeat_last expected_growth 0, expected_works 0, range_low 1.82e-12, range_high 1.82e-12, rank 0.
Limitations: only the Random Forest with the locked configuration and repeat_last are scored live (Bayesian Ridge and XGBoost are not). The ranges depend on the stored percentile table, which is not recomputed in the app.

## Demo: works file of the demo artists (17-10-2026)

Decision: a small file results/demo/works_demo.csv holds the Works of the 40 demo artists (the ids of app/demo_config.yaml) and feeds the timeline cards, so the app never reads works_checkpoint.parquet or snapshots.parquet. It complements the entries "Demo: timeline rule per artist and year, and baseline label (17-10-2026)" and "Demo: re-recordings (17-10-2026)" without changing their rules; the hiding of flagged rows in tab 6 is recorded in the second one.
Rows: every dated Work from 2018 onward; for 2010 to 2017 only the representative Work of each artist-year (works_so_far_this_year equals works_this_year); nothing before 2010. Reason: 2018 is the first year of the demo scores (holdout start) and 2010 to 2017 gives readable context in the timeline. Limitation: years before 2010 are not in the file and the 2010 cut is a display choice.
Columns: work_id, artist_id, title, year, genres, styles, is_rerecording, works_so_far_this_year, works_this_year, is_representative. The per-artist training row count is not in this file, it stays in results/demo/when_demo_scores.csv (artist_train_rows).
Re-recordings: the rule of the entry "Demo: re-recordings (17-10-2026)" is applied unchanged (is_rerecording, both apostrophe forms matched). Rows are kept and flagged, not deleted, because scores are per Work and the representative Work is defined over all Works of the year; the flagged rows are hidden from the timeline cards of tab 6 (entry "Demo: re-recordings (17-10-2026)"). Reason for the flag: a re-recording repeats the styles of the original, so by the definition of the WHAT label (a style absent from every earlier style) it is not expected to add a new style; this was not checked case by case.

## Demo: tab 6 flow and scoring (19-10-2026)

Decision: the question on screen is "Will the artist release something next year?" (WHEN next_1y). The word is release (entry "Text rules for the audience (20-10-2026)"), because the target counts any next release: the year-level activity of the artist, defined in the entry "WHEN target is measured at year level, snapshots stay one per dated work (10-10-2026)". The user prediction is optional: a checkbox "My prediction" (off by default, kept per artist and changeable between years) adds a yes or no question and a lock button before step A. Step A shows the model result, step B the reality (the stored label). A "Go to {year}" button moves to the next playable year (a year with a Work and a score, or a year without a Work that has a year-end score, see below); in this mode (one artist) there is no year selector, and the timeline is the window of the entry "Demo: tab 6 mode 1 layout: compact bar, windowed timeline, two-column panel (20-10-2026)", with the future years as "?". Tab 6 has a second mode, several artists in one year (entry "Demo: tab 6 mode 2, several artists in one year (19-10-2026)"), which opens first and has a year selector. Each year card draws up to poc.titles_per_card (3) titles from results/demo/works_demo.csv, with a "+N more" line when the year has more, and a short line with the count of hidden re-recordings (entry "Demo: re-recordings (17-10-2026)"); the score, verdict and label of a year are those of its representative Work.
Scoring: the scoreboard is model vs reality, with an optional user column (user vs reality and user vs model) when the checkbox is on; years already played keep the columns they had. The model says yes when its live score is at least 0.5, the default threshold of the entry "FINAL holdout evaluation (13-10-2026)" (precision and recall of next_1y reported at 0.5, a convention and not a tuned operating point). The WHEN side panel is the cascade of mode 2, built by the same functions (multi_rows of src/demo_multi.py, side_panel_html of src/poc_view.py): the "Next year" pill and, only when the next-year verdict or the 2-year verdict is No, an arrow and the "Within {n} years" pill; after the reveal the reality cascade follows in the same structure, with the release title in the Yes cells (entry "Demo: release title of the reality cells (20-10-2026)"). The mode 1 panel is two question blocks, Timing and Style, with the wording of the entry "Tab 6 panel wording, independent Timing and Style questions, new styles in the WHAT reality (20-10-2026)"; the Timing reality is "Reality: Yes" or "Reality: No" with the release title and year on the line below (rule B). The user prediction applies only to the next-year question, and the scoreboard counts only the next-year result, by the stored counted_1y flag (a result shown for reference only is not counted); the 2-year result is never scored. WHAT is shown below WHEN in the right panel, under the title "Style of the next release" (game.what_title), which answers a different question than WHEN: the style of the next release, whenever it comes. Step A: a verdict pill, Likely when the WHAT score is at or above score_cutoff and Not likely otherwise (poc.texts.likely and not_likely, the pills of mode 2). The cutoff is the one of the top 10% rule already registered (rows with a score at or above the 90th percentile of the holdout scores; entries "WHAT threshold-metric report (14-10-2026)" and "WHAT final holdout evaluation of stage 0 (14-10-2026)"), not a new threshold, and it is read at load time from results/holdout_what/top10_lift.csv (row stage 0: score_cutoff 0.4943, precision_top 0.5980, holdout_positive_rate 0.3128). The reliability lines sit in the closed "How to read this" expander. For a Yes verdict the line reads "In past tests, about {x} in 10 artists in this group did bring a new style, against about {y} in 10 for any artist.", with x and y the precision_top and the holdout_positive_rate of that row rounded to the nearest integer out of 10 (6 and 3 with those figures). For a No verdict the line reads "In past tests, about {z} in 10 artists outside this group also brought a new style, almost the same as for any artist ({y} in 10).", with z the positive rate of the holdout rows whose stage0_score is below score_cutoff in results/holdout_what/row_scores.csv, rounded out of 10 (128,905 rows below the cutoff, 36,240 positives, rate 0.2811, so z is 3 against y 3); the words "almost the same" are used only when the rounded z and y differ by at most one, otherwise the neutral wording "compared with {y} in 10 for any artist" is used, and with the real values the first wording applies. The scores are not on screen: each is the tooltip of its prediction word, never a percentage. The audience is not familiar with ML, so no calibration warning is shown; the verdict is a ranking statement about the top 10% group and not a probability. Step B: the stored WHAT label as "Reality: Yes, new style: <styles>" or "Reality: No new style", with the release title and its year (the next active year of results/demo/live/what_labels.csv, not necessarily the year after the shown one) on the line below; a line saying there is no WHAT reality when the label is empty; and the same cataloguing-delay note as WHEN for labels after the last holdout year (in the expander). A Work row without a WHAT score (an artist-year without enough earlier Works) shows a grey dash with the tooltip "No style prediction for this year" (poc.texts.what_na and what_na_tip) and its grey dot mark has the tooltip "No prediction" (poc.texts.mark_not_forecast); the cell follows what_available. A year-end row with a stored WHAT score shows the verdict like any row, with the asterisk when it has a label and a grey dot when it has none (entry "WHAT year-end rows (20-10-2026)"); one without a score shows the dash with its own tooltip. The dash replaces the former text "No WHAT score: no release in this year.", which read as no release and contradicted WHEN. WHAT is never counted and never changes the scoreboard. No baseline is shown.
All years with a WHEN score are playable, including 2024, 2025 and 2026. Years are counted by the year of their reality, not by the stored holdout flag (entry "Counting rule by reality year and the 2027 forecast (20-10-2026)"): a next_1y label whose reality year (year + 1) is 2025 or earlier counts, one in 2026 is shown with the asterisk and stays out of the model-vs-reality count. The last data year (2026) is the forecast for 2027: it shows the model prediction and "Not yet", has no reveal step and does not change the scoreboard. Years where the artist has no dated Work are playable from the holdout start (2018) on when the artist has a stored year-end score (entry "Demo: WHEN scores for artist-years without a Work (20-10-2026)": 91 artist-years, 2018 to 2025, none for the last data year): their card carries the grey tag route.texts.year_end_tag ("No release in {year} · prediction from earlier releases"), the WHEN cascade of the panel shows the same tag and the WHAT cell follows the entry "WHAT year-end rows (20-10-2026)" (verdict with a plain mark or a grey dot, or the grey dash). They enter the scoreboard by the rule of the entry "Counting rule by reality year and the 2027 forecast (20-10-2026)": the reality year is the year after the row (the 2-year item as shown), so the rows up to 2024 count (74 of the 91 WHEN rows with a next_1y label, 2018 to 2024, by the stored labels) and the 7 of the 17 rows of 2025 that have a label carry the asterisk and change nothing in the board. Years with no Work and no year-end score stay as gaps.
Warnings on screen: the line that the model has not seen the artist in training (artist_train_rows is 0) is one short visible line above the bar (game.unseen_warning); the other notes sit in the closed "How to read this" expander: the style of the next release is shown for every artist-year from 2018 to the last data year with at least min_previous_works earlier Works (config.yaml) (entry "Demo: WHAT live assets, 2018 to the last data year (19-10-2026)").

## Demo: tab 7 shows the Random Forest (19-10-2026)

Decision: tab 7 shows the Random Forest with the locked configuration, scored live from the saved per-year models (no model is fitted or trained in the app), with the ranges from the stored percentile tables. The year selector lists every target year, none hidden: 2018 to 2022 are the final test years and are counted; 2023 and 2024 show the forecast and the reality with green or red marks followed by an asterisk (a result shown for reference only, not included in the results), with a reason, and have no summary (2023 is excluded from the test, entry "Demo: live style forecast for 2023 and later years (19-10-2026)"; the 2024 reality is display only and incomplete because of cataloguing delay); 2025 and 2026 have no forecast and show a text (style_tab.none_text of app/demo_config.yaml). The table has the top style_tab.top_n (10) styles of the forecast, in rank order, with rank, style, expected growth, expected Works as a pill and the range. The rank is by predicted growth over the base (the mean of the three latest years): expected growth is expected_growth of results/style_forecast/predictions.csv, exp(prediction) - 1 as in long_table of src/style_forecast.py (growth over the base, not a log), shown as a percentage (style_tab.texts.percent_format, for example +103%), and expected Works is the base times exp(prediction); a small style with a high growth can rank above a large one, and the expander says so (style_tab.growth_note). Two steps as in mode 2 of tab 6 (entry "Demo: tab 6 mode 2, several artists in one year (19-10-2026)"): the primary button Predict shows the forecast table (placeholders before), the primary button Show reality adds the actual Works, the actual growth and a mark for an actual inside the range (green check or red cross, followed by an asterisk for 2023 and 2024); the state resets on a change of year and is not shared with tab 6. After the reveal, for the counted years only, two summary lines: styles of the real top 10 (by actual growth among the eligible styles, the registered top_hits definition) present in the forecast top 10, and forecast styles with the actual inside the range, each as k of N. The actual Works come from results/demo/live/style/actuals.csv. The actual growth is in the units of the expected growth: the label of build_panel is log(max(c_next, 1) / base), so the actual growth is exp(label) - 1, which equals max(actual Works, 1) / base - 1; the app computes it from the actual Works and the stored base of the panel rows, and the test checks it against exp(label) - 1 of actuals.csv. The 2024 rows have no label, so their actual growth is computed from the actual Works and the base in the same way; the flagged years (2023 and 2024) keep the normal color of the mark, green or red, followed by an asterisk.
Texts: the page shows the style notice (notices.style_notice: "Which music styles will have the most new releases in Discogs next year?") and one neutral visible line (style_tab.headline: "Tested on {first} to {last}: on average {k} of the top {n} styles in reality are in the forecast top {n}." (figures computed from the data), the mean of the hits 6, 8, 5, 6 and 5 of the stored test log). The closed expander "How reliable is this?" (style_tab.texts.validation_label), on the page from the start and not only after Predict, holds one text, style_tab.validation_text: "Tested on {first} to {last}, the forecast gets {k} of the top {n} styles right. Repeating last year's ranking gets {b} of {n}, so the two are about equal. The ranges are a guide, and only {years} years were tested, so figures can change." (all figures computed; the recommendation to prefer repeating last year is kept in this entry, not on screen) The figure "about 5 of the 10" is the mean of the stored in-range counts 7, 5, 5, 4 and 3 (4.8), computed from the stored predictions and actuals by mean_in_range of src/style_tab.py and checked by scripts/run_style_apptest.py against the yaml wording, against the live summary of each year and, for the hits, against the headline mean of 6.0. The legend ("An asterisk marks a result shown for reference only, not included in the results."), the growth note and the range note are in a second collapsed expander after Predict. The actual cells of 2023 and 2024 use the normal cell style; the asterisk is only on the result mark. repeat_last stays in the files and is not displayed. The tuned Random Forest stays available in the demo labelled with its validation results.
Test figures behind the text (target years 2018 to 2022, scored once; source results/style_forecast/test_20261015_173215.out, also quoted in the Limitations entry on the 5-year test): mean top_10_styles random_forest 6.00 [5.20, 7.00] against repeat_last 5.40 [4.00, 6.80] (95% bootstrap interval over the 5 years), so the intervals overlap; descriptive paired difference random_forest minus repeat_last +0.600 [-0.400, 1.800]. No pass rule is applied on the test years (entry "Style trend forecast, adoption and pass rule results, final test, 2024 projection and output procedure"). The five-condition pass rule was applied on the development years 1993 to 2017 only: mean top_10_styles random_forest 6.28 [5.80, 6.84] against repeat_last 6.00 [5.48, 6.52], two of the five conditions fail (margin of +0.28 against at least 0.5, and a paired interval lower bound of -0.12, not above 0), so the recommended ranking is repeat last year. The ranges under-cover on the development years (0.62 to 0.64 observed against 0.80 nominal, same Limitations entry).
Base years: the 3 behind the growth_note placeholder is typed in style_tab.base_years and hard-coded in src/style_forecast.py (build_panel, the mean of the three latest years), and a check in scripts/run_style_apptest.py fails if the two disagree.
Tests (print-only, 20-10-2026, scripts/run_style_apptest.py): every year in the selector; the validation text in the closed expander, its computed figures against the stored data; the order, ranks, expected growth (ordered descending) and expected Works of 2018 to 2022 equal results/style_forecast/predictions.csv, and the actual growth equals exp(label) - 1 of actuals.csv; the actual Works and marks equal a recount from actuals.csv; hits 6, 8, 5, 6 and 5 for 2018 to 2022 equal the stored test log (in range 7, 5, 5, 4 and 3 of 10); 2023 and 2024 with green or red asterisk marks, no amber class and no summary; 2025 and 2026 with the text and no table; the reset on a year change with tab 6 untouched. Live scoring takes about 0.02 s per year.
Limitation: the tab was not seen rendered in a browser; only five years were tested.

## Demo: WHAT labels after the last holdout year (19-10-2026)

Decision: results/demo/live/what_labels.csv stores, for every row of what_features_all_years.csv, label (new_style_year of build_artist_years: 1 when the next active year has a style absent from every earlier style, 0 when not, empty when there is no next active year or its Works carry no style tags), next_active_year and label_after_holdout. label_after_holdout is true when the label exists and the row is outside the holdout rule (year after the last holdout year, or next_active_year after it; holdout_end in config.yaml), the rule used by the entry "WHAT final holdout evaluation of stage 0". The labels of the 160 holdout rows equal the stored ones in results/holdout_what/row_scores.csv and none is flagged (control 6 of the step). Counts from the step output: flagged labels 3 in 2022, 32 in 2023, 26 in 2024 and 18 in 2025 (79 in total); 2026 has 24 rows and no label; 2025 has 29 rows and 18 labels.
Reason: the label needs the next active year, which lies after the holdout for these rows and is partial because of the cataloguing delay (same reasoning as label_after_holdout in the entry "Demo: WHEN scores file (17-10-2026)"). The flagged labels are shown as information with the cataloguing-delay note of tab 6 and never enter a score, as WHAT is not scored in tab 6.

## Duplicate audit: sensitivity result (17-10-2026)

Script: scripts/run_duplicate_sensitivity.py. Outputs: results/duplicate_sensitivity/results.csv and verdicts.csv.
Reproduction check: all 30 stored development per-fold PR-AUC values were reproduced on the original data to 4 decimals before the batch.
Collapse (definition A, same artist_id and normalized title): 78,300 clusters, 90,076 works removed out of 5,949,217. 11,117 clusters include a work dated 2018 or later. 1,299 titles normalize to an empty string, which groups all such works of one artist, so the removal count is slightly overstated.
Primary rule: not material for WHEN next_1y, WHEN next_2y and WHAT. The collapsed pooled PR-AUC stays inside the 95% interval of the original in every case, and the preferred configuration by mean development PR-AUC is the same on both versions (the candidate m_lr_0.012 for WHAT).
Secondary reading: the paired PR-AUC difference excludes zero in all cases, with magnitudes between 0.0009 and 0.0026 (down for WHEN, up for WHAT). Both readings are reported and neither was chosen after the fact.
Gain over baseline: pooled paired differences between +0.0002 and +0.0014, and the interval for the WHAT candidate includes zero. The positive rate of the baseline is lower after collapsing for WHEN (next_1y 0.4753 to 0.4716, next_2y 0.6123 to 0.6090) and higher for WHAT (0.3175 to 0.3188).
The holdout was not re-evaluated and no correction is adopted. The frozen results stand. The candidate m_lr_0.012 stays not adopted: it fails the unpaired adoption rule and the holdout was already viewed with stage 0.
Limitations: definition A treats same-title works as duplicates, so it is an upper bound of true duplicates. The style forecast series were not audited at the time of this entry.

## Demo pool B: candidates and coverage rule (19-10-2026)

Context: a second artist pool B lets the demo also show well-known artists that the first list (pool A, the 40 artists of the entry "Demo artist selection: final list (17-10-2026)") does not hold. Nothing is retrained and pool A is unchanged. The rule and the list of this entry and of the entry "Demo pool B: final list (19-10-2026)" were fixed before any pool B score or label was produced; no pool B file exists at this date.
Candidates: every candidate named in the entries "Demo artist selection: data-quality criterion (17-10-2026)" (Daft Punk, Adele, boygenius, Vampire Weekend, Brandi Carlile, Peso Pluma, Romeo Santos, Kendrick Lamar, Bon Iver, Arctic Monkeys, Justin Bieber, Beyonce, Usher, A$AP Rocky, Kacey Musgraves: the 15 that did not pass that criterion) and "Demo artist selection: cap per genre (17-10-2026)" (Ariana Grande, Miley Cyrus, Rihanna, J Balvin, Maluma, Daddy Yankee, Jennifer Lopez, Billy Joel, Bruce Springsteen, Labrinth: the 10 left out by the cap), 25 names in total. The manual exclusions of the entry "Demo artist selection: manual exclusions (17-10-2026)" are not candidates.
Rule: an artist is in pool B if its Discogs id resolves from the data and it has dated Works in at least 2 distinct years within 2018 to 2023 (pool_b.min_distinct_years and pool_b.coverage_years in app/demo_config.yaml). The window is the one of the counted WHEN labels (holdout 2018 to 2023, config.yaml). The rule uses only data coverage, never a score, a label or a model output, and no artist is added or dropped for any other reason.
Id resolution: by the artist name in data/processed/works_checkpoint.parquet, accent-insensitive, taking the id with the most Works; the same method returns the registered id for 36 of the 40 pool A artists (the other four, H.E.R., SZA, Rosalia and BTS, differ by name variant). Adele has no exact name match and its Works sit under "Adele (3)" (id 819015, checked by titles); A$AP Rocky is stored as "ASAP Rocky" (id 2409771, checked by titles). Both are resolved by hand and are the only manual resolutions; Adele fails the rule.
Counts (distinct years with dated Works, source data/processed/works_checkpoint.parquet): Daft Punk 1289: 1 in 2018 to 2023, 1 from 2018 to 2026; Adele 819015: 1 in 2018 to 2023, 1 from 2018 to 2026; boygenius 6774153: 3 in 2018 to 2023, 3 from 2018 to 2026; Vampire Weekend 861083: 1 in 2018 to 2023, 3 from 2018 to 2026; Brandi Carlile 502784: 4 in 2018 to 2023, 5 from 2018 to 2026; Peso Pluma 13200183: 4 in 2018 to 2023, 7 from 2018 to 2026; Romeo Santos 3094075: 2 in 2018 to 2023, 3 from 2018 to 2026; Kendrick Lamar 1778977: 2 in 2018 to 2023, 3 from 2018 to 2026; Bon Iver 1042739: 2 in 2018 to 2023, 4 from 2018 to 2026; Arctic Monkeys 391170: 3 in 2018 to 2023, 4 from 2018 to 2026; Justin Bieber 1602787: 3 in 2018 to 2023, 4 from 2018 to 2026; Beyonce 52835: 4 in 2018 to 2023, 6 from 2018 to 2026; Usher 97139: 4 in 2018 to 2023, 5 from 2018 to 2026; A$AP Rocky 2409771: 4 in 2018 to 2023, 7 from 2018 to 2026; Kacey Musgraves 2866928: 3 in 2018 to 2023, 6 from 2018 to 2026; Ariana Grande 3310737: 4 in 2018 to 2023, 7 from 2018 to 2026; Miley Cyrus 676432: 5 in 2018 to 2023, 7 from 2018 to 2026; Rihanna 321128: 1 in 2018 to 2023, 1 from 2018 to 2026; J Balvin 3460330: 6 in 2018 to 2023, 9 from 2018 to 2026; Maluma 3913668: 6 in 2018 to 2023, 7 from 2018 to 2026; Daddy Yankee 256399: 6 in 2018 to 2023, 9 from 2018 to 2026; Jennifer Lopez 51984: 5 in 2018 to 2023, 8 from 2018 to 2026; Billy Joel 137418: 2 in 2018 to 2023, 4 from 2018 to 2026; Bruce Springsteen 219986: 6 in 2018 to 2023, 9 from 2018 to 2026; Labrinth 1154116: 4 in 2018 to 2023, 6 from 2018 to 2026.
Result: 21 of the 25 pass; Daft Punk, Adele, Vampire Weekend and Rihanna fail (1 year inside 2018 to 2023 each).
Limitation: coverage, not irregularity, decides membership, so a very irregular artist with Works in fewer than 2 years of the window is excluded, and some failures of 17-10-2026 may owe to the coverage alone. Pool B is not a random sample and says nothing about global performance.

Fixed before any pool B score or label was produced.
