Reason: tab 7 now lists every target year from 2018 to 2026 with a two-step table, 2023 and 2024 flagged in amber, 2025 and 2026 without a forecast; the projection of 2024 is shown as a flagged year.
- Demo: tab 6 mode 2 presentation of 19-10-2026 (replaced 19-10-2026): the wordings "a grey placeholder (route.texts.placeholder)", the verdicts as plain text, "the two reliability lines of mode 1 ... shown once under the table", "the legend line" and the buttons and pickers shown in the page body, of the entry "Demo: tab 6 mode 2, several artists in one year (19-10-2026)". Reason: presentation change for a clearer screen (dark dashed placeholder cells, verdict pills in one accent color, large primary buttons, pickers and reading notes in collapsed expanders); no score, stage, state or count changed.
- Demo: tab 6 mode 2 as a one-step read-only table (replaced 19-10-2026): the wording "Mode 2 is a read-only table: no question, no lock, no reveal, no scoreboard and no game state" of the entry "Demo: tab 6 mode 2, several artists in one year (19-10-2026)", with the summary and the reliability lines shown at once. Reason: the table now has two steps (Predict, then Show reality), so the audience sees the model verdicts before the realities; the summary appears only after the second step.
- Demo: top padding of the page container in the proof of concept style (removed 19-10-2026): the rule that set the padding of the main container, from poc.sizes.page_top_padding. Reason: the fixed Streamlit header hid the tab bar of the app on every tab; the key is removed from app/demo_config.yaml.
- Demo: tab 6 without a year selector (narrowed 19-10-2026): the wording "there is no year selector" of the entry "Demo: tab 6 flow and scoring (19-10-2026)" as a statement about tab 6. Reason: tab 6 now has a second mode with a year selector (entry "Demo: tab 6 mode 2, several artists in one year (19-10-2026)"); it holds only for the one-artist mode.
- Demo: pool selector in tab 6 and the proof of concept (reversed 19-10-2026): the wording "tab 6 and the proof of concept page get a pool selector above the genre filter. Labels come from pool_b.pool_labels and pool_b.pool_selector_label ... the default is pool A (pool_b.default_pool) ... the genre and artist selectors have one widget key per pool, so changing the pool shows that pool's own genre and artist choice" of the entry "Demo: pool switch in tab 6 and the proof of concept (19-10-2026)", and the neutral pool labels "Most active artists" and "Other well-known artists" of the entry "Demo pool B: final list (19-10-2026)". Reason: the pools are mixed in one list, so the audience does not see a split between them.
- Demo: live WHEN and WHAT only for the 40 demo artists (reversed 19-10-2026): the wordings "live scoring of WHEN and WHAT works only for the 40 demo artists" of the entry "Demo: live scoring with the final models (17-10-2026)" and "live WHAT works only for the 40 demo artists" of the entry "Demo: WHAT live assets, 2018 to the last data year (19-10-2026)", and "pool B has no WHAT assets yet" of the entry "Demo pool B: WHEN assets (19-10-2026)". Reason: the 21 artists of pool B now have WHEN and WHAT assets (entries "Demo pool B: WHEN assets (19-10-2026)" and "Demo pool B: WHAT assets (19-10-2026)").
- Demo: tab 6 WHAT without verdict and the calibration warning (reversed 19-10-2026): the wordings "WHAT is shown next to WHEN in two columns, in both steps, without a Yes or No verdict and without a threshold: the WHAT score of the artist-year with the existing notice that WHAT scores are not calibrated and only rank" and, in the warnings on screen, "scores are not calibrated and only rank" of the entry "Demo: tab 6 flow and scoring (19-10-2026)". Reason: the audience is not familiar with ML, and the cutoff of the new verdict is the top 10% rule already registered (entries "WHAT threshold-metric report" and "WHAT final holdout evaluation of stage 0"), not a new threshold; the calibration limit stays in Limitations.
- Demo: tab 6 question, mandatory prediction and WHAT as an information line (reversed 19-10-2026): the wordings "In year XX, will this artist release a new Work next year?", "The user predicts yes or no first, then sees the model result, then the reality", "the scoreboard compares the user and the model only" and "After the reveal, WHEN next_2y and WHAT are shown as information and are not scored" of the entry "Demo: tab 6 flow and scoring (19-10-2026)". Reason: the question text changed, the user prediction became optional, the board is model vs reality with an optional user column, and WHAT is now shown in a column next to WHEN in both steps (still without verdict and not counted).
- Demo: hidden-count line only when the reality depends on hidden re-recordings (19-10-2026): the wording "When the reality of a year depends on hidden re-recordings, the screen shows a line with the count of hidden re-recordings" of the entry "Demo: re-recordings (17-10-2026)". Reason: the screen shows the count under every year that has hidden re-recordings, a simpler rule that needs no per-year judgement; the hiding stays display-only.
- Demo: WHAT live range 2018 to 2022 (reversed 19-10-2026): the wording "WHAT 2018 to 2022" in the Limitation of the entry "Demo: live scoring with the final models (17-10-2026)" and "WHAT exists only for 2018 to 2022" in the entry "Demo: tab 6 flow and scoring (19-10-2026)". Reason: WHAT is now scored for every eligible artist-year from 2018 to the last data year (entry "Demo: WHAT live assets, 2018 to the last data year (19-10-2026)").
- Demo: tab 6 years without a stored label shown as partial truth (reversed 19-10-2026): the sentence "Years without a stored label (is_projection or label_after_holdout per target) are shown as "partial truth, not part of any metric" and do not enter the scoreboard" of the entry "Demo: tab 6 flow and scoring (19-10-2026)". Reason: years with a label after the last holdout year are now shown with their stored label and kept out of the scoreboard, and only the last data year, which has no label, shows no reality.
- Demo: baseline always visible in the demo screens (reversed 19-10-2026): the sentences "The baseline shown next to the model is baseline (c)... it is always visible and always labelled as baseline (c)" of the entry "Demo: timeline rule per artist and year, and baseline label (17-10-2026)". Reason: the demo shows only the final models; baselines stay in the method and results tabs, labelled as baselines.
- Demo: re-recordings not collapsed in the artist timeline (reversed 19-10-2026): the wording "are not collapsed in the artist timeline" of the entry "Demo: re-recordings (17-10-2026)". Reason: noise in the cards; flagged re-recordings are now hidden from the timeline cards of tab 6.

- Work definition is Master-only (decided and REVERSED 10-10-2026): filtering works_df to work_source == "master" took it from 5,949,217 to 1,951,057 rows. Reversed because the trade was disproportionate: 3,998,160 works (67.2%) and 403,897 of 1,034,756 artists (39.0%) were dropped to address an 8.58% field-level gap (510,364 missing years), and losing most of an artist's discography destroys the sequences a next-work model learns from. works_checkpoint.parquet restored to all 5,949,217 rows (master and synthetic); the synthetic-Works rule is back in DECIDED.
- Merging artist_id for 21 normalized-identical artist names that differ only in case ("Zoë"/"ZOË", "Høst"/"HØST", mostly noise/black metal project names): confirmed NOT a Unicode-normalization artifact (NFC normalization doesn't collapse them), the artist_ids are genuinely distinct per Discogs' own ID system. Too small (21 names) and too ambiguous (could be the same artist entered twice, or two different artists sharing a stylized name, common in that scene) to safely merge. Left as is.
- Excluding works by "session" alone in the title (13,419 matches): too generic, mostly original recording-session albums ("Tutorial Sessions", "In Session", "The Blackacre Sessions"), not re-performances of existing material. The specific sub-patterns "radio edit"/"radio version"/"bbc session" were kept and removed instead (see DECIDED).
- Excluding works by "karaoke" in the title (426 matches): too noisy. Real karaoke-product compilations exist ("De Grootste Karaoke Hits") but roughly 20-25% of a 20-row sample read as original song/album titles using "karaoke" thematically or as wordplay ("Drunk At The Karaoke Bar" by Blanks 77, "Karaokejack" by Takkyu Ishino), same noise level as "collection"/"complete", and the population is too small to justify a narrower disambiguation rule. Left in works_df.
- Excluding works by "complete" in the title (1,614 matches): same problem as "collection", too generic. Real compilations exist ("The Complete JATP Performances", "Complete Recordings 1995") but so do clear original titles ("Complete Metamorphosis" reads as a creative album title, not a recording archive), roughly 20-25% of a 20-row sample was ambiguous or a clear original. Left in works_df.
- Excluding works by "acoustic" in the title without the parenthetical qualifier (3,824 matches): too ambiguous, "acoustic" is often a genre/style label for original work ("Acoustic Soul" by Fink is a real debut studio album), not a signal of redoing an existing song. Replaced by the narrower "(Acoustic)" parenthetical pattern instead (see DECIDED).
- Excluding works by "collection" in the title (5,362 matches): too noisy. The regex itself has a bug (no word boundary, so it also matches "Recollection"/"Recollections", an unrelated word), and even past that, "collection" doesn't reliably mean a retrospective compilation (several matches, e.g. "Intergalactic Fungi Collection", read as original album titles). Roughly a quarter of a 20-row sample was a false match or genuinely ambiguous, far above the noise level accepted for Live/Greatest Hits/best-of-anthology. Left in works_df.
- Fuzzy-match dedup pass (rapidfuzz token_sort_ratio threshold, same artist_id) on top of the existing exact-match title_normalized dedup: initially found promising on a capped sample (Bing Crosby's titles surfaced minor-wording near-duplicates like "Drifting And Dreaming" vs "Drifting Dreaming"), but a full-scale EDA re-check (notebooks/01_cleaning_eda.ipynb) across all 699,079 multi-work artists / 5,774,455 works showed every band from 85 to 99 dominated by numbered/episodic series false positives (radio show episodes, Vol./Partie/roman-numeral sequences), with true duplicates too rare and scattered to separate from the noise by score alone (see DECIDED). This CUT applied only to the unguarded version: a guarded version (excluding pairs where the only difference is a number/roman numeral) was then built and checked the same way, found clean, and is the one actually implemented and applied to works_df (see DECIDED).
- Optional sensitivity analysis with the tolerant WHAT target (next active year that has style tags), to be run only after the kill gate verdict and only if time allows, reported as a robustness check and never mixed into the main training set. Recoverable rows: 76,458 (+10.0% of the 763,371 labelled rows, development years, history >= 3); caveat: the extra delay to the styled year is variable (p50 2, p75 5, p90 10, p99 29 years) and the recovered label prevalence is 40.0% (62,321 rows whose next styled year is a development year; the other 14,137 are excluded because their label would use holdout-year style tags) against 30.9% for the primary target, so the two targets measure different horizons (see the DECIDED entry on label loss). CLOSED 15-10-2026: not pursued within the scope of the project and recorded as a limitation (section Limitations).
- Whether to cap works per artist or down-weight high-volume artists before generating snapshots in snapshots.py: quantified via EDA (02_eda_visualization.ipynb, re-run on the current 5,948,995-row checkpoint on 07-10-2026, superseding the figures below from an earlier stale checkpoint) that the top 1% of artists (10,348 of 1,034,756) hold 17.15% of all works and 20.56% of all potential snapshots (work_count - 1 per artist), more than the bottom 50% of artists (517,378) combined (12.65% of works), while a single median artist is a negligible 0.00004% share of potential snapshots. This concentration persists even after the mass-upload exclusion removed the one confirmed artificial outlier, so it reflects genuinely high-volume (legitimate) artists, not upload noise. No cap or weighting applied yet. CLOSED 15-10-2026: not pursued within the scope of the project and recorded as a limitation (section Limitations).
- Whether to down-weight snapshots belonging to artists with incomplete history (using artist_undated_work_count, added in the snapshots.py gap-honesty fix) during model training. The naive approach, sample_weight inversely proportional to artist_undated_work_count in model.fit(), was considered and rejected as a default, because missing year is not random, it is concentrated in specific genres (Latin, Reggae, Folk/World/& Country, per the missing-year-by-genre entries above), so down-weighting by completeness would effectively down-weight those genres further during training, compounding the representation problem already documented above rather than fixing it. Four alternatives were considered, in increasing order of complexity, to be chosen from once the modeling stage starts and actual performance can be measured: (1) feed artist_undated_work_count and genre directly as model input features, letting tree-based models (RF/XGBoost) learn any appropriate discount themselves through splits, rather than imposing a hand-chosen weighting formula, simplest and lowest risk of introducing new bias; (2) two-level weighting, first equalizing each genre’s total weight in the loss function so genre representation is not affected by row count or completeness, then within each genre down-weighting only the artists unusually incomplete relative to their own genre’s baseline, separating “this is noise” from “this is an underrepresented genre”; (3) multiple imputation, generating several plausible versions of the undated-work timeline, training and evaluating across all of them, and combining results with standard rules for averaging estimates and uncertainty across imputations, most statistically rigorous but the heaviest to implement, likely out of scope given the project timeline; (4) conformal prediction or similar calibrated uncertainty output, not touching training weights at all, instead reporting calibrated confidence or interval widths per prediction, so artists with less complete history naturally get wider uncertainty bands in the output rather than a pre-decided training discount. Recommendation if this needs to be decided under time pressure: try option 1 first (free, no new bias), and only escalate to option 2 if genre-stratified evaluation at the modeling stage actually shows the incompleteness is hurting performance enough to justify it. Options 3 and 4 are noted for completeness but not recommended given project scope. CLOSED 15-10-2026: not pursued within the scope of the project and recorded as a limitation (section Limitations).

## EDA

- WHAT minimum-history-threshold sensitivity check (notebooks/02_eda_visualization.ipynb, 5,949,217-row checkpoint, 08-10-2026): tested the same base-rate computation at min_previous_works = 2, 3, and 5 against the current checkpoint. Results: cutoff 2 yields 3,784,407 transitions at 20.18% new-style intro rate; cutoff 3 yields 3,254,604 transitions at 18.06%; cutoff 5 yields 2,565,515 transitions at 15.45%. The rate is stable (declines ~2.5pp per level, a consistent linear trend, not a cliff or anomaly), confirming the original config.yaml choice of cutoff 3 is defensible. Keeping min_previous_works=3 in config.yaml; **resolves PENDING item "Final WHAT minimum history threshold"**.

## Hypothesis Testing

**Hypothesis 1**: Does an artist's new-style introduction rate change with how many prior works they have? H0: new_style and prior_work_bucket are independent. H1: they are dependent. Caveat: transitions from the same artist are not fully independent, so the p-value is overstated; the rate gap is the stronger evidence.

**Method**: Chi-square test of independence, computed on 3,254,604 eligible transitions (min_previous_works=3). Transitions bucketed by prior-work count into five groups: 3-5 prior works, 6-10, 11-20, 21-50, 51+.

**Results** (notebooks/02_eda_visualization.ipynb cell hypothesis testing, executed 08-10-2026):
- 3-5 prior works: 927,574 transitions, 26.88% introduce new style
- 6-10 prior works: 729,148 transitions, 20.64% introduce new style
- 11-20 prior works: 632,029 transitions, 15.72% introduce new style
- 21-50 prior works: 576,937 transitions, 11.18% introduce new style
- 51+ prior works: 388,916 transitions, 6.20% introduce new style

**Chi-square test**: chi-square = 109,737.05, p-value < 0.000001, degrees of freedom = 4. **Result: REJECT null hypothesis** (p < 0.05).

**Interpretation**: The new-style introduction rate varies significantly by prior-work bucket, with a strong monotonic decline as artists accumulate more prior works. This is not noise; artist history length is a real signal that predicts stylistic consistency. The effect is large: a 4x difference between the lowest (6.20%) and highest (26.88%) buckets.

**Hypothesis 6 (20-10-2026)**: Does the chance that an artist releases something next year depend on how many years have passed since their last release? H0: next_1y (the artist has activity in year Y+1) is independent of the bucket of years since the last Work. H1: they are dependent. Hypotheses 1 to 5 study the WHAT target, so they use the transitions table. This hypothesis covers WHEN (does the artist release next year?), so it uses the snapshots table. It tests the main timing signal of the WHEN model, the years since the last release. The test describes a relationship in the data; all models are already locked and nothing is fitted, scored or changed. Caveat: rows of the same artist are not independent, so the p-value is overstated; the rate gap and Cramer's V are the stronger evidence, and with this many rows significance is guaranteed.

**Method**: Chi-square test of independence (5 x 2: gap bucket x next_1y) on 2,085,428 artist-years of 578,620 artists. Data: snapshots table from config.yaml (not rebuilt), the first snapshot of each artist-year (works_so_far_this_year == 1), history_count >= 1, years_since_last >= 1, resolved next_1y, all years with a reliable label, from the earliest (1896) up to 2022 (Y+1 at most 2023; later years have incomplete loading). Buckets of years since the last Work: 1, 2, 3-4, 5-9, 10+. Control as in Hypothesis 2: the test repeated inside buckets of history_count (1-2, 3-5, 6-10, 11-20, 21+). Code in src/eda.py (load_when_gap_rows and following functions).

**Results** (notebooks/02_eda_visualization.ipynb, Hypothesis 6 cells, executed 20-10-2026):
- 1 year: 1,134,284 artist-years, 54.82% release next year
- 2 years: 393,882 artist-years, 33.66%
- 3-4 years: 297,765 artist-years, 23.36%
- 5-9 years: 173,051 artist-years, 17.61%
- 10+ years: 86,446 artist-years, 12.46%

**Chi-square test**: chi-square = 203,924.55, p-value < 1e-300 (underflows to 0), degrees of freedom = 4, Cramer's V = 0.313, smallest expected count 35,866. **Result: REJECT null hypothesis** (p < 0.05).

**Control by history_count** (chi-square, Cramer's V, rate at gap 1 and at gap 10+): 1-2 works (754,999 rows): 44,925, V 0.244, 44.1% and 12.8%; 3-5 (500,776): 43,601, V 0.295, 49.2% and 10.6%; 6-10 (331,195): 32,099, V 0.311, 57.2% and 12.9%; 11-20 (240,098): 25,731, V 0.327, 64.3% and 13.8%; 21+ (258,360): 32,703, V 0.356, 73.1% and 14.1%. All p < 1e-300.

**Interpretation**: The next-year release rate falls steadily as the years since the last release grow, a 4.4x difference between the 1-year (54.82%) and 10+ (12.46%) buckets. The effect is large and does not disappear inside any history bucket (V 0.244 to 0.356), so it is not only a catalogue-length effect; it is stronger for long catalogues (gap 1 rate 73.1% at 21+ works against 44.1% at 1-2), while at 10+ years all buckets are between 10.6% and 14.1%. Years since the last release is a strong timing signal for WHEN. It does not say how much a model gains from the feature.

## Future improvements

- Neural networks and clustering, untested hypotheses outside the current scope (15-10-2026). Neural networks: the data is tabular (about 25 inputs, about 143k holdout rows for WHAT) and gradient-boosted trees usually match or beat neural networks on tabular data, so a gain from the model class alone is not expected. Possible gains would come from sequence models over an artist's release history or from learned embeddings for style, label and artist. Not tested. Any trial would need the same walk-forward protocol, and because the WHAT holdout is spent it could only be compared on development folds or on a new untouched period. Clustering: unsupervised, so not a predictor by itself. It could be used as feature engineering (for example career-stage or style-cluster ids) or to segment results. It would have to be fitted inside each training fold to avoid leakage. Benefit not tested. Neither is part of the current scope; the current choice favours the simplest effective model.

## Limitations

- Data source: Discogs is a collector database, so the Works table is not all releases ever made, and its counts depend on what the community catalogued (entries "Master is the current Work proxy", "Standalone releases (master_id == 0) belonging to artists with 3+ standalone releases become synthetic Works" and "Style trend forecast, design fixed before any model result exists").
- Collaborations are credited to the first artist only (entry "works_df combines masters_df ...", sentence "Credited artist rule"). The share of Works with more than one credited artist is not measurable from the checkpoint, which keeps only the first artist (`artist_id` and `artist` are single-valued columns).
- Artists with undated Works lose history in the snapshots: 510,357 undated rows are dropped (entry "WHEN target is measured at year level, snapshots stay one per dated work"). Mitigation: the year_missing flag and the Hypothesis 5 analysis of notebooks/02_eda_visualization.ipynb (entry "Missing-year artist dropout"); year_missing is not a WHEN model input (entry "Feature allowlist for WHEN modeling").
- WHAT rows exist only if the next active year has style tags and history_count >= 3, so the label is known for a subset of artist-years, skewed to short gaps (entries "WHAT is modeled at artist-year level" and "WHAT holdout label rule").
- Load delay of 2024 to 2026 and the 2023 anomaly are handled differently by target. WHEN: the holdout labels reach the lag years and censoring drops 0 rows (entry "FINAL holdout evaluation"). WHAT: labels with a next active year of 2024 or later are excluded and 2023 has no labelled rows (entry "WHAT holdout label rule"). Style forecast: 2023 is excluded from the test and used only as the base year of the 2024 projection (entry "Style trend forecast, design fixed before any model result exists"). 2024 and 2025 are excluded from training, tuning and the evaluation metric (entry "2024 to 2025 are excluded from training, tuning and the evaluation metric").
- The WHAT signal is moderate: holdout PR-AUC 0.499 against a base rate of 0.313 (baseline (c): 0.346) and ROC-AUC 0.678 (baseline (c): 0.559), from results/holdout_what/headline.csv (entry "WHAT final holdout evaluation of stage 0").
- Calibration was not checked for WHAT (entries "WHAT threshold-metric report" and "WHAT final holdout evaluation of stage 0"). The ranges of the style forecast under-cover: 0.62 to 0.64 observed against 0.80 nominal on the development top 10 (entry "Style trend forecast, adoption and pass rule results, final test, 2024 projection and output procedure").
- The style forecast test has only 5 years (2018 to 2022). top_10_styles mean on the test: random forest 6.00 [5.20, 7.00] against repeat last year 5.40 [4.00, 6.80] (results/style_forecast/test_20261015_173215.out), so the intervals overlap. On the development years the pass rule failed two of its five conditions and the recommended ranking is repeat last year (same entry as above).
- The demos are illustration, not evidence: they change no model, metric or decision (entry "Time Machine uses historical out-of-time predictions" and the docstring of scripts/run_xgb_what_live.py).
- WHEN metrics are per snapshot (an artist at the time of one of their works), not per artist (entry "FINAL holdout evaluation").
- The tolerant WHAT target was not run as a sensitivity analysis: 76,458 more labelled development rows (+10.0%) would be recovered, with a different horizon and a label prevalence of 40.0% against 30.9% (CUT entry "Optional sensitivity analysis with the tolerant WHAT target").
- High-volume artists are not capped or down-weighted: the top 1% of artists hold 17.15% of all Works and 20.56% of the potential snapshots (CUT entry "Whether to cap works per artist or down-weight high-volume artists").
- Artists with incomplete history (undated Works) are not down-weighted in training; `artist_undated_work_count` and `year_missing` are not model inputs (CUT entry "Whether to down-weight snapshots belonging to artists with incomplete history" and the missing-year entry).
- The ordering "rule fixed before result" for the WHAT tuning, adoption and threshold-report protocols and for the style forecast protocols rests on the timestamps in the result file names and on the wording of the entries, because protocol and results were committed together.
- Any decision recorded before its result is verifiable in git only for the WHAT holdout label rule (commit 564b8dc); for the other protocols the commit contains both protocol and result.

### Known limitation: catalogue date (17-10-2026)
Discogs is a community-maintained database, so releases are catalogued after their release date, in some cases years later. Counts per year come from the 01-09-2026 dump and include works dated to a given year but catalogued afterwards. The series is therefore not point-in-time by catalogue date, and the state of the database at past dates cannot be reconstructed. This is a property of the source, not of the pipeline, and it applies to every model. It is consistent with the load delay and the 2023 anomaly already recorded.

- Re-recordings in the demo (19-10-2026): the training and evaluation labels of WHEN and WHAT count a re-recording as a new Work, because the target counts any next Work of the artist and the cleaning does not remove re-recordings (entry "Demo: re-recordings (17-10-2026)"). The demo hides re-recordings on screen only (game.hide_rerecordings in app/demo_config.yaml) and shows a short hidden-count notice, so a year can show reality "Yes" with no visible new Work. Counts read from the files: results/demo/works_demo.csv has 17 re-recording Works from 2018 on (12 in 2021, 2 in 2022, 3 in 2023), all of the demo artist 1124645, so 1 of the 40 demo artists has any. For 1124645, in results/demo/when_demo_scores.csv no year has a next_1y label Yes only because of re-recordings: the Works of the following year are never all flagged in results/demo/works_demo.csv (2021 has 12 flagged of 14 Works, 2022 has 2 of 5, 2023 has 3 of 8), so 0 years show reality "Yes" with no visible new Work in the current data. The root fix, excluding re-recordings from the target and retraining the final models and the holdout, was not done because of the code freeze on 27-10-2026 and because it would invalidate the reproduction controls of the live assets.

- WHAT reliability line in tab 6 (19-10-2026): the percentages of the reliability line are holdout figures of the top 10% group only (results/holdout_what/top10_lift.csv, entry "WHAT final holdout evaluation of stage 0"), about the artist-years of 2018 to 2022 of the holdout; they say nothing about an individual artist, and the same cutoff is applied unchanged to the scores of later years, where the share of artist-years at or above it is not 10% by construction. The No-verdict line is also a holdout figure: of the 143,228 holdout rows in results/holdout_what/row_scores.csv, 14,323 are at or above the cutoff (selected_rows of results/holdout_what/top10_lift.csv) and 128,905 are below it, with 36,240 positives (rate 0.2811). Calibration of the WHAT scores was not checked (see the bullet above on calibration).

- Demo pool B (19-10-2026): membership is decided by data coverage only (at least 2 distinct years with dated Works within 2018 to 2023), not by irregularity, so some very irregular artists are excluded and some failures of 17-10-2026 may owe to coverage alone; the pool is not a random sample; its genres come from a data rule while the pool A genres were assigned by the author; two ids were resolved by hand (entry "Demo pool B: candidates and coverage rule (19-10-2026)").

## Style forecast: duplicate audit design (17-10-2026)

Context: same-title works in different years survive cleaning and the style forecast counts works per style and year, so duplicates may inflate the series of their styles.
Step A counts only: per style and year, works counted on the original table and on the table with definition A clusters collapsed to the earliest work, with no model. For years up to 2017, also the rank correlation and top-10 overlap of the year-over-year growth between both versions. For 2018 and later, only counts of affected works: no growth, no ranking, no hits, no model output.
Step B only after explicit approval: refit on development years only, with the same seeds and parameters, comparing hits@10 per method on the development folds.
Rule fixed before any run: the effect is material if the collapsed pooled development hits@10 of any method falls outside the 95% interval of the original, or the pass or fail status of any condition that can be evaluated on development years changes, or the ranking of methods by mean development hits@10 changes. The reading where the paired difference excludes zero is reported as secondary and neither reading is chosen afterwards.
The spent style test script is not run and the test years are not evaluated.

## Demo: WHEN scores file (17-10-2026)

The file results/demo/when_demo_scores.csv has one row per work, keyed by artist_id, snapshot_year, work_id and target, because snapshots are one row per work and many artist-years repeat. Any aggregation to one row per artist-year is decided later and recorded before use.
Rows keep their label wherever it is resolved, in every year. is_projection is true only when the label is unresolved. The column label_after_holdout marks snapshots dated after the last holdout year, whose resolved labels are partial and affected by cataloguing delay, and which enter no metric.
The script refits the final configuration on the original training rows and compares train rows, holdout rows and the aggregate PR-AUC of each target with the stored values before writing, and stops if any differs. The control passed on 17-10-2026: 0.7851 for next_1y and 0.8352 for next_2y, with 3,786,030 train rows and 1,020,151 holdout rows.

## Style forecast: duplicate audit result (17-10-2026)

Scripts: scripts/run_style_duplicate_audit.py (counts) and scripts/run_style_duplicate_refit.py (development refit). Outputs: results/style_duplicate_audit/style_year_counts.csv, summary.csv, dev_refit.csv and dev_refit_verdict.csv.
Counts: collapsing same-title works (definition A) removes 1.31% of dated and styled works before 2016, 0.64% in 2016-2017 and 0.70% in 2018-2022. Growth rankings between the original and collapsed tables have a Spearman correlation between 0.9974 and 0.9993 and a top-10 overlap of 9 or 10 styles in all 25 development years.
Control: the original table filtered to year 2017 or earlier reproduces results/style_forecast/dev_hits.csv for all 6 methods in all 25 years at 4 decimals. The locked forest on the original table matches the stored trial m_trees600.
Refit with the rule of the design entry. Default settings: mean development hits@10 of random_forest 6.16 on the original and 5.80 collapsed (paired difference -0.36, interval -0.60 to -0.16), repeat_last 6.00 and 5.80, bayes_ridge 6.12 and 6.04, xgboost 6.00 and 5.72, mean_growth 4.52 and 4.40. No collapsed mean falls outside the original 95% interval. Condition 5 (mean Spearman) changes from met to not met (0.7117 against 0.7069 on the original, 0.6918 against 0.7053 collapsed), the candidate changes from random_forest to bayes_ridge and the ranking of methods changes.
Primary reading: material for the default settings, driven by those three setting-level changes. Not material for the locked forest (random_forest 6.28 and 6.20, paired difference -0.08 with interval -0.32 to 0.16): the five conditions and the ranking are unchanged.
Secondary reading: the paired interval excludes zero only for random and for random_forest under default settings.
Recommendation: repeat_last on both versions and both settings. The frozen test results (2018-2022) were not recomputed and stand.
Limitations: definition A is an upper bound of true duplicates, and the margins that flip the default settings are small.
- Demo: on-screen wording before the text rules (replaced 20-10-2026 by the entry "Text rules for the audience"): "We are in year {year}, will this artist release a new Work next year?", "My own prediction", "Reveal reality", "Next year" as the next-year button, "Within 2 years", "Not forecast", "No Work", "Result (WHEN, WHAT)", "New work next year?", "(all used before)", "Score: 0.549", the tab 7 headline "Validated on 2018 to 2022: on average 6.0 of the real top 10 styles appear in the forecast top 10." and its long validation text with the recommendation sentence, the legend_reference line and the independent_note line in the expanders. Reason: mixed words for one concept, numbers typed by hand and repeated ideas.
